# J&H — Implementation Requirements

A private web app for exactly two people. An iOS PWA with four tabs: Chat, Calendar, Library (`보관함`, routed under `/archive`), Settings.

- **App name**: J&H · **Folder**: `jandh` · **Domain**: `jandh.jeheecheon.com` · **Users**: 2 (fixed, never grows) · **Repo**: GitHub Private
- **Reference project**: `~/Projects/everytldr/everytldr.com/frontend`

> **Language**: this document is English for LLM comprehension. **All user-facing copy is Korean.** Korean literals here (`오늘`, `새 메시지 3`, …) are the exact UI text — never translate them in code.

| Document                                    | Role                                                                        |
| ------------------------------------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| `docs/REQUIREMENTS.md` (this file)          | **What we build** — features, architecture, schema, API shapes, build order |
| `docs/DESIGN.md`                            | **How it looks** — tokens (sole source of hex values), visual specs         |
| `AGENTS.md` (root; `CLAUDE.md` symlinks it) | **How we write it** — conventions, component contracts, prohibitions        |

Precedence on conflict: visuals → `DESIGN.md`; code-authoring rules → `AGENTS.md`; everything else → this file.

## Reading This Document

- **✅ on a heading = that section is finished.** Its bullets are **invariants new code must not break**, not work to do. They are compressed on purpose: the argument that produced each rule is gone, the rule is not. Code comments cite these section numbers instead of restating them (`AGENTS.md § 7.2.`), so a line here is often its **only** written copy — deleting one deletes the reason a comment exists.
- **`[ ] = remaining work`, and this file is the authoritative spec for it.** Open items keep their full detail deliberately; do not compress them, and do not infer the design from the surrounding invariants. Tick the box in the change that lands it (`AGENTS.md § 0.3.`), then fold it into that section's invariant list on the next pass.
- A section with open work leads with **Landed:** (finished, no boxes) and then lists its `[ ]` items. A section marked ✅ has no boxes at all.
- **Never renumber a section, and never delete one** — `src/` comments reference these numbers.

## 0. Settled Technical Decisions ✅

| Item          | Decision                                                         | Rationale                                                                                                                                   |
| ------------- | ---------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| Framework     | Next.js 16.2.12 (App Router) + React 19.2.4                      | All server functionality lives in the single Next app                                                                                       |
| Hosting       | Vercel (serverless)                                              | Free, `git push` deploys, automatic HTTPS                                                                                                   |
| Database      | Neon Postgres + Drizzle ORM                                      | Free tier, serverless-friendly                                                                                                              |
| Auth          | Google OAuth (allow-listed emails) → our own session             | No password stored, so no brute-force surface                                                                                               |
| Session       | `sessions` table + opaque token in a domain-wide httpOnly cookie | Per-device revocation, instant profile changes, survives iOS ITP, one login across both apps (§ 5.2.1.)                                     |
| Realtime      | SSE + Postgres `LISTEN`/`NOTIFY`                                 | WebSockets conflict with one-Next-app; SSE gets cookie auth + retry                                                                         |
| Notifications | Web Push (VAPID) + a push-only service worker                    | Covers the app being closed, which SSE cannot (§ 16.1.)                                                                                     |
| Images        | Cloudflare R2 (private bucket + presigned URLs)                  | Free egress; a UUID in a URL is obscurity, not access control                                                                               |
| Rendering     | Dynamic per request; no `cacheComponents`                        | Every screen is per-user, so the shell blocks once and the § 12.2. wallpaper is in the first paint. Tried the flag and reversed it (§ 1.1.) |
| Architecture  | Feature-Sliced Design, enforced by steiger                       | Inherited from `everytldr`                                                                                                                  |
| Styling       | Tailwind v4 + semantic tokens (`theme.css`)                      | Dark theme becomes a value swap, not a refactor                                                                                             |
| Indexing      | Fully blocked (robots + meta + header)                           | Private app                                                                                                                                 |

## 1. Project Setup ✅

Next 16.2.12 + `pnpm` + Turbopack; local Postgres via `docker-compose` (`pnpm db:up`), Neon in production. Config inherited verbatim from the reference project: `tsconfig` (`@/*` → `./src/*`, `verbatimModuleSyntax`, `strict`), `eslint.config.mjs`, `.prettierrc` (`printWidth: 100`), `steiger.config.ts`, `postcss.config.mjs`, `components.json` (shadcn `new-york`, `@/shared/ui`, `@/shared/lib`, lucide), the SVGR turbopack rule, Pretendard Variable as a local font. `pnpm lint` = eslint + steiger; `pnpm dev` runs the app and steiger `--watch`.

- **Deliberately excluded — do not reintroduce**: all i18n (`next-intl`, `[locale]` routing, `messages/`, `Translation`), `orval` (Next _is_ the backend), `msw`, every ads / analytics / RSS / structured-data / sitemap file, the `rewrites` backend proxy
- **Check `package.json` before adding a dependency.** R2, push, virtualization (`@tanstack/react-virtual`), text measurement (`@chenglou/pretext`), query, validation, image cropping (`react-advanced-cropper` — it replaced `react-easy-crop`, whose crop box cannot be resized), client storage (`synced-storage`), drag-and-drop (`@dnd-kit`), browser-side media conversion (`mediabunny`, § 12.1.) and Korean particle selection (`es-hangul`, `AGENTS.md § 0.4.`) are installed. **There is no remaining unmet dependency**; a new one needs the user's approval first
- **`es-hangul` is named in `experimental.optimizePackageImports`** and has to stay there. It ships no `sideEffects: false`, so a bare `import { josa }` drags its romanisation and standard-pronunciation tables into the bundle behind one function
- **`mediabunny` is the one MPL-2.0 dependency**, where everything else here is MIT or Apache. Weak copyleft at file scope, so it constrains modifying the library itself and nothing about using it — recorded so the next licence audit does not have to rediscover it

### 1.1. Rendering — every screen blocks, and Cache Components was tried and reversed ✅

**There is no `cacheComponents` in `next.config.ts`, and putting it back is a decision rather than a tidy-up.** It was enabled, the whole app was migrated to it, and it was turned off again. This section is the account, so nobody re-derives it.

- **Every `(main)` screen is `ƒ`, and the `(main)` layout **blocks**.** It reads the session and four queries before it returns, so the first HTML already carries the participant set, the unread count and § 12.2.'s wallpaper — which is what puts the chat chrome tint in the **first paint** rather than a frame later (`DESIGN.md § 3.3.` needs exactly that, since iOS 26 samples an element's first paint and re-samples for nothing after). `/` and `/offline` are the only static routes
- **What the flag actually is.** `cacheComponents` is a **project-wide boolean**: `experimental_ppr` was removed in Next 16 and there is no per-route opt-in, so a partial migration is a failed build. Uncached runtime data outside a `<Suspense>` boundary becomes a build error, route segment configs (`dynamic`, `revalidate`, `fetchCache`, **and `runtime`**) are rejected outright, `generateStaticParams` may not return `[]`, and `<Activity>` starts preserving hidden routes app-wide
- **The caching it was adopted for never needed it.** § 11.7.'s 특일 정보 requests are cached by `fetch(url, { next: { revalidate } })`, which is its own layer and works with the flag off — that is why the holiday feature required no change when the flag came out. `use cache` would have added nothing here anyway: a cached scope may not read `cookies()` or `searchParams` and every screen reads one, so the migration was `<Suspense>` boundaries throughout and not a single cached component
- **What it cost, and why it was reversed.** Two regressions, both from the same mechanism. The `(main)` layout's boundary necessarily covered `{children}` — `ChatStreamProvider` is seeded from the cookie and wraps every screen _and_ the tab bar's badge, so a boundary that let the pages through would have to sit above the one place the session can be read — which meant the wallpaper arrived by stream and the room painted flat first. And `<Activity>` kept a departed route mounted, so a sheet left open on 이모티콘 관리 was open again on the way back (`DESIGN.md § 4.7.1.` has the interaction with `RouteTransition`'s key). Against those, the gain was a static shell of the tab bar over an empty column on an app where every screen is per-user
- **Kept from the migration, because none of it depended on the flag**: each screen's `loading.tsx` and its sized fallback (`DESIGN.md § 7.8.`) — `<Suspense>` streams without `cacheComponents`, and these are what a **tab switch** shows; 채팅's is the one that has since been deleted, on that section's own argument; `PushSync` inside the session gate (below); `/`'s redirect moved to `proxy.ts` (below); and `WeekdayHeader`, `SettingsRowSkeleton` and `LoginError`'s `Object.hasOwn` guard
- **`PushSync` belongs inside the session gate, and that is independent of any flag.** § 5.2.'s proxy proves only that a cookie exists, so a revoked session reaches the shell and is bounced by the layout's own check a moment later. Mounted above that check, `PushSync` acts in the gap: `useNotificationSweep` clears every § 16.1. banner this origin has delivered and `syncPushSubscription` POSTs into a 401 that `safelyGetAsync` swallows — the user is bounced **and** their pending banners are gone, for a session that never happened. The same test applies to anything else whose effects reach the server or the OS
- **A route whose whole body is a redirect must not become a document.** `/` used to be `app/page.tsx` calling `requireUserOrRedirect()` and then `redirect(HOME_ROUTE)`; under the flag that became a 200 with a painted spinner and a client navigation. The hop now lives in `proxy.ts`, which has already proven the cookie for `/`, and the database check stays on `HOME_ROUTE`'s own layout — so an invalid session still lands on `SESSION_EXPIRE_ROUTE` exactly as before. The page survives as an unreachable belt and reads no session, which is what keeps it `○`
- **If it is ever revisited**, the two things to solve first are the wallpaper's place in the first paint and `<Activity>`'s open sheets — not the boundaries, which were straightforward

---

## 2. FSD Structure ✅

```
app/                     Next routes — thin, delegate to src/pages
  (auth)/login/  (main)/{chat,calendar,archive,settings}/  api/{auth,chat,media,emoticons}/
  robots.ts  manifest.ts  icon.svg  favicon.ico
pages/                   EMPTY SENTINEL — README only, no route files
proxy.ts                 Next 16's renamed Middleware (§ 5.2.)
src/
  app/       providers / styles(globals.css, theme.css) / fonts
  pages/     login, chat, calendar, archive, settings, emoticon-settings, emoticon-pack
  widgets/   tab-bar, install-guide, chat-room, archive-shelves, calendar-month, settings-form
  features/  session, send-message, chat-stream, search-messages, push-notifications,
             upload-media, author-emoticon, emoticon-prefs, update-profile, manage-event,
             apply-photo, view-profile
  entities/  user, message, push-subscription, media, event, emoticon, chat-background
  shared/    db, auth, push, sound, storage, share, api, config, lib, theme, ui
```

**Placement rules that are not obvious from the tree** — each exists because moving the code breaks a steiger rule or drags `server-only` into the browser bundle:

- The session slice is `session`, not `auth` — `features/auth` collides with the `shared/auth` segment name (`fsd/ambiguous-slice-names`)
- The composer and emoticon picker are **`features/send-message/ui/…`, not widgets**: `widgets/chat-room` renders them, and a widget cannot import a sibling widget (`fsd/forbidden-imports`)
- Same-layer cross-imports go through the **`@x` gate**, never a barrel: `features/author-emoticon` → `@x/author-emoticon`, `features/upload-media` → `@x/send-message`, `@x/update-profile` and `@x/apply-photo`, `features/update-profile` → `@x/typing-indicator`, `@x/apply-photo` and `@x/view-profile`, `features/chat-stream` → `@x/view-profile`, `entities/emoticon/@x/message`
- **`features/view-profile` owns the § 12.3. overlay and the provider that opens it**, and it is a feature rather than a widget precisely so `widgets/chat-room` can reach the hook — a widget may not import a sibling widget
- **`uploadDraft` lives in `features/upload-media`, not `send-message`** — chat and the library both put an attachment in R2, and the slice named for uploading owns the one implementation
- **`shared/share`** owns handing stored objects to the OS: the library's 저장 (§ 10.) and a message's 공유 (§ 8.11.) are one route with two labels, and the callers are a widget and a widget's sibling. The segment is `share`, not `media` (`entities/media` claims that name). Its dialog lives there too — the hook toasts, so `shared/ui` importing it would close a cycle
- **`entities/chat-background` is singular where its table is plural**, and that is `fsd/inconsistent-naming` rather than an oversight: every other entity slice is singular, so `chat-settings` would be the only plural one. The table keeps the plural name because a settings row can hold a second setting, while the slice is named for the one thing it exposes today (§ 12.2.)
- **`useLongPress` and `GESTURE_SLOP` live in `shared/lib`** — chat bubbles and library tiles both arm the gesture
- **`MediaViewer`, `MediaCell` and `Avatar` live in `shared/ui`** — `widgets/chat-room` and `widgets/archive-shelves` both render them and neither may import the other. `Avatar` could not move to `entities/user` regardless: that barrel exports a `server-only` api segment
- Client modules import `@/entities/message` with **`import type` only** — a value import drags `server-only` into the browser bundle. Hence chat row components and the grouping model live in `widgets/chat-room`
- Root `pages/` MUST exist and stay empty, or Next treats `src/pages/` as the Pages Router and the build breaks
- Server-only code lives in `shared/` segments and imports `server-only`; entity fetching lives in each entity's `api` segment; every slice is imported through its `index.ts` (eslint-enforced)

## 3. Shared Utilities (`src/shared/lib`) ✅

Read `src/shared/lib/index.ts` for the inventory (nullish types, `safely*`, `mapPooled`, `assert`, `cn`, duration constants + Korean date formatters, text layout, DOM helpers, `useHydrated` / `useIsCoarsePointer` / `useIsVirtualKeyboardOpen`). Reach for it before writing a utility.

- **`safely*` and `mapPooled` sit under `lib/run/`.** `fsd/shared-lib-grouping` errors past 15 modules at the top level, so a new utility joins a group or makes one. `sound.ts` moved to `audio/` (beside `session.ts` and `voice-playback.ts`) when § 9.3. pushed the top level past the limit; the barrel's export names did not change, so no caller did
- **`cn` uses `extendTailwindMerge`; every custom token scale must be declared there.** An undeclared scale is not merged at all — `cn("px-md", "px-0")` emits both and stylesheet order wins, silently ignoring the call-site override. A new `--text-*` / `--spacing-*` scale in `theme.css` means a matching entry here

## 4. Design System and Theming ✅

`globals.css` imports tailwind + `tw-animate-css` + `theme.css` and defines `@custom-variant dark`. `theme.css` declares `--color-*: initial` first, which **removes Tailwind's default palette from the build**, structurally preventing raw color utilities; semantic + chat tokens, `--text-*`, `--radius-*`, `--spacing-*`, `--shadow-*` follow. **`DESIGN.md § 4.` is the sole source of hex values — never copy one here.** `.dark { }` is an empty shell; `shared/theme/provider.tsx` is pinned with `forcedTheme="light"` (dark ships by deleting that line). Icons: `lucide-react` first; custom marks are `.svg` via SVGR, re-exported from `shared/ui/index.ts`.

### 4.1. Responsive Policy — Two Layouts ✅

**Mobile below `md` (768px), desktop from it.** A left rail (`DESIGN.md § 7.3.1.`) replaces the tab bar at `md`; a collapsible side panel (`DESIGN.md § 7.20.`) joins it at `lg` (1024px), widening at `xl` (1280px). Geometry is Tailwind's own `md:`/`lg:`/`xl:` classes, never script — both trees mount and CSS keeps one. `useIsDesktop` (`AGENTS.md § 4.1.`) exists only for the handful of branches geometry cannot express: `BottomSheet` → `DialogShell`, `ActionSheet` → `Popover`. Pointer support is still mandatory everywhere: real `click` handlers plus `hover:` / `active:` / `focus-visible:`. `useIsCoarsePointer` is for interaction details only, never layout.

- [x] Rail, side panel, and the `md`/`lg`/`xl` breakpoints
- [x] `useIsDesktop` limited to `BottomSheet`/`ActionSheet` component choice
- [x] `AGENTS.md § 4.1.` rewritten from "one mobile layout" to the two-layout rule

### 4.2. Page Width and Centering ✅

Below `md` the shell fills the window — no max width, no gutters. From `md`, main content centres inside `--content-max-width` (**800px**) in the pane beside the rail/panel; the archive grid is exempt and fills the pane, and `ChatScreen`/`ShellOverlay`/the shell itself stay `max-w-none`. `Container`: `md` (default) = that cap, `sm` = `448px` (`--container-app-narrow`, login/offline only), padding `px-md`. `--tab-bar-height`, `--app-header-height`, `--rail-width`, `--pane-width`/`--pane-open-width` and `--content-left` (`--rail-width` + `--pane-width`) live in `theme.css` outside `@theme`. Screens never restate a viewport height — the `(main)` layout owns the column height, screens are `flex-1` inside it, and full-height screens honour `env(safe-area-inset-*)`.

- [x] `--content-max-width` (800px) replaces `--container-app` (576px) as the content cap; `--container-app-narrow` unchanged
- [x] `--rail-width` (72px at `md`), `--pane-open-width` (320px at `lg`, 360px at `xl`), `--content-left`

### 4.3. Visual Viewport and the Keyboard ✅

Landed; full rules in `AGENTS.md § 4.4.` and `DESIGN.md § 3.4.`–`§ 3.5.` What a new screen must respect:

- **Four elements are `fixed`, and a fifth — `NavRail` — earns the argument `AGENTS.md § 4.4.` asks for**: `AppHeader`, `BottomOverlay`, `ChatScreen`, `ShellOverlay`'s layer, and `NavRail` from `md` (`DESIGN.md § 7.3.1.`) — the document scrolls under it, and `sticky` would need an `h-dvh` wrapper the padded column does not have. Only `ChatScreen` is sized to the **visual viewport** (`VisualViewportSync` → `--viewport-height` / `--viewport-top`); `AppHeader` (through `--header-lift`) and `ShellOverlay`'s layer carry the pan as a term so they ride it, and `BottomOverlay` does not need to — it is collapsed to zero height for the whole time a keyboard is up (`DESIGN.md § 7.3.`). That term is **`--keyboard-pan`, never `--viewport-top`**: `offsetTop` is also non-zero while Safari collapses its toolbar, where a `fixed` box needs no correction and is pushed _down_ by one (`DESIGN.md § 3.4.`)
- **The document is the app's scroller on every route but chat** (`DESIGN.md § 3.3.`) — Safari collapses its bottom toolbar for the root scroller only, and a nested one costs ~100px of phone screen for the life of the session. `html` / `body` carry no `overflow` or height of their own, the layout's `<main>` slot is a plain flow container carrying no id at all — so nothing can look it up and scroll it — and `ScrollMemory` / `ScrollReset` address `window`. Chat is held out of it because a document with nothing to pan is what stops the composer wobbling under the keyboard
- Header and bars **float over** content: `fixed` header at `--header-lift`, `fixed` `BottomOverlay` at `--bar-lift` whose measured height becomes the `--bottom-inset` spacer `RouteTransition` trails below the screen (never the scroller's end padding — `DESIGN.md § 3.5.`). Both re-apply the shell width through `Container`, since the document moves under them. Both use the single `glass` utility; every floating strip is `pointer-events-none` at its root, re-enabled only on the visible surface
- **A `fixed` box flush against a viewport edge makes iOS 26 Safari paint its chrome opaque.** The lift is the box's own `top` / `bottom`, never padding inside it, and the two clearances are tokens (`--chrome-edge-gap` 6px at the top, `--bar-float-gap` 4px at the bottom) so they cannot drift. `ChatScreen` is deliberately flush and pays for it by wearing the wallpaper's own tint (§ 12.2.)
- **Nothing may depend on `interactiveWidget: "resizes-content"`** (Chromium/Firefox only, ignored by iOS), and `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` is **not** a keyboard inset — it reports the home indicator and never moves for the keyboard. **`--bar-lift` therefore goes to `0px` while the keys are up**, under a `data-keyboard-open` flag `BottomOverlay` publishes on the root: the keyboard is drawn over the home indicator, and the 4px float gap is both pointless there and exactly how much of the collapsed bar stays on screen (`DESIGN.md § 3.4.`, `§ 7.3.`)

### 4.4. Base Components (`src/shared/ui`) ✅

Read `src/shared/ui/index.ts` for what exists (buttons, inputs, the overlays below, `Container`, `AppHeader`, `EmptyState`, `Avatar`, `RelativeTime`, scroll helpers, `toast`). Check it before building any primitive.

- New primitives come from shadcn first, then have **every** visual decision rewritten against `DESIGN.md` and are refactored to the `AGENTS.md § 1.` props contract
- `Drawer` and `Dialog` are composition primitives, **never used directly in screen code**. Overlays in `shared/ui` are props-driven (`isOpen`, `header`, `onClose`, `children`; `ActionSheet` takes `items: { label, icon, variant, onSelect }[]`) and compose the primitives internally

## 5. Authentication ✅

### 5.1. Google OAuth ✅

`GET /api/auth/login/google` (arctic `state` + PKCE in cookies), `GET /api/auth/callback/google` (code exchange, `id_token` verified with `jose`, `state` and verifier checked, `email_verified === true` required, email lowercased and matched **exactly** against `ALLOWED_EMAILS`), `POST /api/auth/logout`. The Google consent screen stays in "Testing"; redirect URIs are `http://localhost:3000/…` and `https://jandh.jeheecheon.com/…`.

- The `users` lookup key is **`google_sub`, not the email** — Google lets an account change address, and matching on email collides with the existing row's unique `google_sub`
- **Google OAuth only.** No provider abstraction, no `[provider]` segment, no password login
- **The authorization URL carries `prompt=select_account`**, which arctic does not add. Google otherwise skips the chooser whenever exactly one account is signed in to the browser, and a rejected email lands back on `/login?error=not_allowed` with **that same account** on the next attempt — the app can revoke its own session but never Google's, so the chooser is the only way back to a different address

### 5.2. Session ✅

A 32-byte token is stored **hashed** in `sessions.token_hash`; only the raw token goes in the cookie (`session`, `httpOnly; Secure; SameSite=Lax; Path=/; Domain=.jeheecheon.com; Max-Age=180 days`). Sliding renewal updates `last_seen_at` and extends expiry at most once a day per device. The lookup is memoized per request with React `cache()`.

- The proxy re-issues the cookie with a fresh `Max-Age` on every page request — otherwise the row's new expiry is invisible to the browser, which drops the cookie 180 days after login however active the user was
- **Never use localStorage for auth state** — iOS ITP can evict script-written storage after 7 days of non-use; a server-set cookie is exempt
- `proxy.ts` (Next 16 renamed Middleware to Proxy: repo root, exports `proxy`) checks **only whether the cookie exists**; real DB validation happens in Server Components / Route Handlers. Unauthenticated → `/login`; authenticated on `/login` → `/chat`
- A cookie that exists but no longer validates is unwound through `GET /api/auth/session/expire`, which clears it and lands on `/login` — a Server Component cannot write cookies, so redirecting it straight to `/login` bounces off the proxy forever. That route re-checks the session and refuses to clear a valid one, since a cross-site `<img src>` reaches it with the cookie attached

#### 5.2.1. One session across both apps

jandh-emoticons writes into this database (its `REQUIREMENTS.md § 3.`) and always resolved its logins against **this** `sessions` table. What used to differ was only the cookie: a separate name on a separate origin, so the same person signed in twice and carried two rows per device. The cookie is now issued over the shared parent domain under one name, so **one row serves both apps** — there is nothing to distinguish in the schema and there never was.

- **`SESSION_COOKIE_DOMAIN` carries the domain**, unset outside production. `localhost` accepts no `Domain` at all, and `pnpm dev:tunnel`'s Tailscale funnel answers on `.ts.net`, which shares no registrable domain with production — a `Domain` naming either one from the other is rejected outright, so the two sessions cannot collide. This replaced a Cloudflare tunnel under `jandh-dev.jeheecheon.com`, where a cookie set on the parent **did** overwrite the production session with a token only the local database could resolve
- **Both deployments must set the same value.** A cookie issued to a different parent domain is simply a second cookie, and the apps fall back to two logins with no error anywhere
- **The name changed to `session`**, retiring `jandh_session` and `jandh_emoticons_session`. Those were issued host-only, and a host-only cookie survives beside a domain-scoped one of the same name: the browser sends both, a read picks between two values, and `clearSessionCookie` can only expire the scoped one — which is the `/login` bounce above, permanently. Both apps therefore ignore the old names and the pair signs in once more
- **`session` is a generic name over a whole domain, and that is a deliberate trade.** It is the default cookie name in several frameworks, so anything else ever hosted under `jeheecheon.com` both receives this token and can shadow it with a host-only cookie of its own — the failure directly above, arriving from a neighbour instead of from history. The domain holds these two apps and is not shared, which is what makes the short name safe; putting a third party on a subdomain is what would make it unsafe
- **Clearing carries `Domain` and `Path`.** A delete by name alone expires a host-only cookie that no longer exists and leaves the real one in place
- **Logging out anywhere logs out everywhere**, because there is one row. That is the intent: it is one person on one device with one login
- **§ 12.'s device list is now the list for both apps**, and one row per device rather than two. A 로그아웃 there revokes the session jandh-emoticons is also holding, which is what per-device revocation should have meant all along. It is also why an emoticons login carries no app prefix in its `device_label`: the row it names is a jandh login too, and a prefix would mislabel a live one
- **The OAuth `state` and PKCE verifier stay host-only.** Both apps write those two names too, and shared across the domain a login begun in one app would overwrite the values the other is waiting to check
- **The cookie reaches every subdomain of `jeheecheon.com`**, present and future — the cost of sharing at the parent domain. Anything else hosted there receives it, so a subdomain that is not ours is a subdomain that must not exist

### 5.3. iOS PWA Verification (highest priority) ✅

- **Verified on a real device:** the Google login redirect from a home-screen standalone PWA returns to the app correctly
- **Verified on a real device:** iOS transcodes HEIC to JPEG for a video file input, so § 9.'s "store whatever arrives" costs nothing and the viewer's download fallback never fires
- **`voice-playback.ts`'s shared element is discarded when a recording ends.** § 13.6.'s resting category is page-**wide** and capture moves it to `play-and-record` and back, while WebKit fixes an `<audio>` element's own category at its first playback — so a player minted before the switch would go on minting a Now Playing entry and stopping the user's music instead of ducking it. Restoring the resting category is **not** enough on an element already fixed; dropping it is what makes the next playback mint a fresh one under `transient`. `leaveCapture` in `use-voice-recorder.ts` is the **one** exit from `play-and-record` and does both, so a fourth exit path cannot restore half of it. The discard **stops whatever is playing and takes no `isVoiceActive` guard**, unlike every other caller of `stopVoice`: being alive across the switch is exactly what poisons the element, so a note still playing when the microphone was asked for is cut off — including where permission was refused and no recording happened. § 13.6.'s emoticon context is deliberately **not** discarded with it: `unlockSound` resumes it on every gesture and a replacement would sit suspended until the next one, leaving a live arrival unable to sound at all — where voice playback starts from a tap on the play control every time

### 5.4. Development Login ✅

`POST /api/auth/login/dev` takes an `email` form field, matches `ALLOWED_EMAILS` as § 5.1. does, and issues the same § 5.2. session. `/login` renders the form under the Google button when enabled.

- Gated on `IS_DEV_LOGIN_ENABLED`, and the route answers **404** wherever it is off. `ENABLE_DEV_LOGIN` decides it and outranks the `NODE_ENV === "development"` default in both directions — only `true`, `1` or `on` enable it, absent or blank falls back. **It exists for § 16.:** offline behaviour has to be tested on `next start`, where the dev default is off and a Google consent screen is the only way in. **A production deployment must leave it unset** — set, it is an email-only path to any `ALLOWED_EMAILS` address, with no Google verification behind it
- **Read twice, at two different times.** `/login` prerenders, so a production build takes the value at **build** time for the form while the route takes it per request — set it before `next build`, or the form is shown against a route that 404s (or hidden over one that answers)
- The row is upserted with a synthetic `dev:{email}` subject id; a later Google sign-in relinks that row to the real `google_sub`, so the paths never fork into two users
- The redirect is **303**, not `redirectTo`'s default 307 — a 307 replays the POST against the target page
- Auth Route Handlers redirect through `redirectTo` from `@/shared/api`, which emits a **relative** `Location`. `new URL(path, request.url)` cannot: `next dev` builds `request.url` from the bound `localhost:3000` and takes only the scheme from `X-Forwarded-Proto`, so behind an HTTPS tunnel it emits `https://localhost:3000`, which speaks no TLS. `proxy.ts` is exempt — Next already relativises middleware redirects

---

## 6. Database Schema (Drizzle) ✅

Schema, migrations, and triggers have landed. **Columns are not listed here — read `src/shared/db/schema/*.ts`** (`users`, `sessions`, `messages`, `message_media`, `media`, `events`, `emoticons`, `push-subscriptions`), one file per table. Never copy a column list into this document. The invariants below are what the schema cannot state on its own.

**Identifiers**

- **Every id is a 64-bit snowflake**, minted by the application and laid out `43 bits ms | 10 bits machine | 10 bits sequence`, epoch **1990-01-01 UTC**. The columns are plain `bigint NOT NULL` with **no `DEFAULT`** — one generator, in TypeScript, mirrored in jandh-emoticons (`src/shared/db/snowflake.ts`), where only `MACHINE_BASE` differs. A second copy in SQL would be the same layout written twice, and the drift would be invisible
- **The machine field is split by deployment**: jandh draws `0`–`511` and jandh-emoticons `512`–`1023`, per process, because a serverless instance has no stable identity to derive a number from. A collision needs the same millisecond, the same drawn number **and** the same sequence value, and surfaces as a primary key violation rather than as a shared id
- **In TypeScript an id is a branded string, never a `number`** — it exceeds `Number.MAX_SAFE_INTEGER`, and `JSON.parse`, `Number()` and `z.coerce.number()` all round it silently into a row that does not exist. `compareId` / `maxId` / `idBefore` in `@/shared/lib` are the only way to order or step one; `snowflakeSchema` in `@/shared/config` is the only way one enters from a request
- **The epoch is far enough back that every id is 19 digits**, and stays so for the whole life of the layout — `2^63 - 1` is itself 19 digits, so the width cannot change once the first id has crossed `1e18` (which the 1990 epoch puts in 2020, before any row here). That is what keeps a stray `>` from being merely wrong-in-waiting. The field runs out in **2268**
- **The layout is frozen, and an id is read as a timestamp.** This reverses an earlier decision that kept it free to be re-cut on the grounds that ordering was all the app asked of an id. `idToDate` in `@/shared/lib` is what every `created_at` column was replaced by (see _Timestamps_ below), so the epoch and the field widths are now load-bearing for the recorded age of every row already written — re-cutting the format means versioning it and keeping the old branch forever, not merely redistributing bits. `SNOWFLAKE_EPOCH` and `SNOWFLAKE_TIME_SHIFT` are exported from `identity/id.ts` so that even SQL which reconstructs the timestamp (`effectivePackPosition`) builds it from those values rather than writing the numbers out a third time
- **`messages.client_msg_id` is the one id that is not a snowflake, and it cannot become one** (§ 8.5.). A browser has no machine number to mint with and the value has to exist before the request is sent; a collision there is read as "that retry already landed", so it would drop a real message rather than fail an INSERT. A random uuid needs no coordination at all, which is the whole requirement
- **R2 keys are `{scope}/{owner snowflake}/{object snowflake}`** (§ 9.). The object segment is minted by the same generator and is not a row id

**Timestamps**

- **No table whose primary key is a snowflake carries a `created_at`.** The id already holds the instant, and storing it twice meant the ordering key and the displayed time could disagree. Ordering and every keyset cursor run on `id` alone — which also **removes a tie rather than breaking one**: `created_at` defaulted to the _transaction_ timestamp, so a multi-image send wrote rows that compared equal and needed the id as a tiebreaker anyway. `ArchiveCursor` is one id for the same reason, and `media.created_at`'s millisecond precision — which existed only because that cursor crossed the wire as an ISO string — went with it
- **`updated_at` is untouched by that, and the distinction matters.** An id records when a row was _created_, never when it changed. `emoticon_items.updated_at` in particular is `Emoticon.version` and is what busts the multi-day asset cache (§ 13.4.)
- **The clock is the minting instance's, not the database's.** `now()` was one authority; an id's timestamp comes from whichever serverless instance minted it, across two platforms, and `nextSnowflake` deliberately mints _ahead_ of real time when a millisecond's 1024 sequence values are exhausted. The error is sub-second and was accepted knowingly. Verified before the columns were dropped: the id-derived instant matches the old `created_at` to within one second on every row of `messages`, `media`, `emoticon_packs`, `emoticon_items` and `events`, so `0030`'s renumbering preserved the true times rather than stamping its own
- **`ChatMessage` keeps a `createdAt` on the wire, and it is the one exception.** A pending message is drawn before it is written, so it has a `client_msg_id` and **no snowflake at all** — and `buildChatRows` places pending and settled rows on the same day dividers and in the same minute groups. Only the source changed: `toChatMessage` derives it with `idToDate`

**Shape**

- There is **no `accounts` table** (one provider, § 5.1.), **no `conversation_members` table**, and **no `conversations` table**
- **Two participants, permanently.** `ALLOWED_EMAILS` gates the only row-creating path, so membership and identity are the same set. A third participant needs `conversation_members` back **and** a rewrite of § 8.8. (unread becomes a count of readers, not a boolean, reaching into the `1` marker and unread divider in `DESIGN.md § 7.`). A non-participant `users` row — a test or retired account — breaks `GET /api/users` the same way; keep such rows out of this database
- **The one conversation is implicit — no row, no id.** Every `messages` query is already conversation-wide. A second conversation needs a `conversations` table, a CHECK, an `ensureConversation` on the login path, an FK on every row, **and** the members rewrite above — not one of them alone. **§ 12.2.'s `chat_settings` does not reopen this**: it holds a setting, not an identity. Nothing references its key, no query joins it, and a second conversation would still need all five of the things above built from nothing
- **There is nothing to seed.** A `users` row exists only after that person's first login. The extension, trigram index, and triggers live in hand-written (`--custom`) migrations, since drizzle-kit cannot infer them
- Two connection strings: `DATABASE_URL` (pooled, normal queries) and `DATABASE_URL_UNPOOLED` (**required** for `LISTEN` and migrations)

**Messages**

- `messages` is **append-only** — marking messages read must never UPDATE it. The rule is about the read cursor, and the two columns that do write here say so by name: `deleted_at` (§ 8.10.) and `edited_at` (§ 8.13.). No third one joins them without an argument of the same kind
- `messages.edited_at` is nullable and `NULL` means "never edited", which is the entire `수정됨` test (§ 8.13.). `messages_edited_is_text_check` keeps it on `type = 'text'`, and `messages_notify_changed` fires on it and `deleted_at` together — narrowed by `AFTER UPDATE OF` and guarded by `IS DISTINCT FROM`, because `<>` answers `NULL` on a nullable column and a `WHEN` clause reads that as false
- A CHECK pins which columns each `messages.type` may fill (a `text` row carries no `emoticon_item_id` or event; a `system` row carries no text). The CHECK cannot reach `message_media`, so "a non-`media` message must not acquire media children" is a `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` **trigger** there. The reverse (a `media` message with zero media rows) is deliberately **not** enforced — it would need a `DEFERRABLE` constraint trigger, and § 8.5. writes both in one transaction
- **A `text` row carries its own emoticons in a second column.** `messages.inline_emoticon_item_ids` holds one id per `OBJECT_PLACEHOLDER` (U+FFFC) in `text`, in that order, repeats included, and the two are only ever read as a pair (`isMessageContentPaired`). **U+FFFC because no keyboard and no IME can produce it** — a marker a draft could contain by accident makes every count over `text` read a message's own words as an emoticon. `messages_inline_emoticons_are_text_check` keeps the array empty on every other type, and it is deliberately **not** a foreign key and cannot be one, Postgres constraining no array element — which is why the id is checked in the route instead (§ 13.6.) and why a deleted item keeps its row (§ 13.4.)
- **There was never a migration to write, and that is a property of the format.** Every message written before this reads as zero placeholders against an empty array, which is a valid instance of it — so nothing backfills, nothing that draws a message branches on an old shape, and `text` stays the plain sentence § 8.6.'s search, § 16.1.'s push body and § 8.10.'s quote have always read
- **These ids never enter `message_media`.** Membership in 보관함 is that table and nothing else (§ 10.), so an emoticon written into it is an emoticon in the gallery
- **One bubble is one `messages` row regardless of attachment count**: 3 photos = 1 message row + 3 `message_media` rows, which is why there is no `messages.media_id`. `sort_order` preserves the sender's order. `media_id` **must not** cascade, and carries its own index
- The `media` enum value was `image` until `0008_media_message_type`. It is `media` because one bubble may carry photos **and** videos, discriminated by `media.mime` — renamed with `ALTER TYPE … RENAME VALUE`, never a drop-and-recreate, which fails on existing rows and breaks the trigger function whose `DECLARE` names the type
- `messages.event_id` is `ON DELETE SET NULL`; § 11.5. composes its sentence from the `event_title` / `event_starts_at` snapshot, because a delete notice outlives its `events` row. Only the **user name** is resolved at render time (§ 8.7.)
- `messages.reply_to_id` is a **self-FK**, `ON DELETE SET NULL`, and carries **no snapshot of what it points at** (§ 8.10. gives the reason it is the opposite call from the line above). It is orthogonal to `type`, so it stays out of the payload CASE and takes a CHECK of its own for the one rule it needs (no system parent). It has **no index** — the only lookup is parent-by-id, which is the primary key
- **`users.last_read_message_id` is nullable, and the null is "everything is unread".** The cursor was always naming a message rather than an instant, and saying so is what lets § 8.8. compare `messages.id > last_read_message_id` — id to id, with no clock and no conversion inside SQL. It carries **no foreign key**: `messages` already imports the users module, so a reference would be the cycle `emoticon_packs.thumbnail_item_id` has to be patched in by a separate ALTER, and it would buy nothing since `messages` is append-only and a cursor can only name a row that still exists. The `last_read_at` it replaced had no default for the same reason the null works here — a `defaultNow()` would silently mark everything sent before that person's first login as read
- **`last_read_at` is gone**, dropped by `0037`. It took three changes rather than one, and rule 3 below is why: `markUserRead` stopped writing it once the build whose badge the double write kept alive was gone; `0036` relaxed the `NOT NULL` **in a migration of its own**, because the column had no default and `upsert-google-user` still supplied it on INSERT; and only then could the declaration, that last write and the column go together. The split was not ceremony — § 5.4.'s dev login inserts a user, so the `23502` window rule 3 warns about was reachable rather than theoretical
- `users` carries **three** nullable `media` references — `avatar_media_id`, `profile_background_media_id` (§ 12.1.) and `chat_background_media_id` — all `ON DELETE SET NULL`, all declared through `AnyPgColumn` to break the `users` ⇄ `media` type cycle. **The third is dead**: § 12.2. moved the wallpaper to `chat_settings` and nothing reads or writes that column any more. It stays declared until its own release drops it, for the reason the migration-ordering rules below give in full. `0019`'s warning governs both ends of its life and comes from one fact — `getSessionContext` selects `users` whole, so a column this table gains must exist **before** the code naming it deploys, and one it loses must stop being named **before** the migration removes it. Getting either backwards fails **every request that resolves a session** on `42703`
- **`chat_settings` is the one table with exactly one row** (§ 12.2.), pinned by `id boolean PRIMARY KEY DEFAULT true` and `CHECK (id)`. It exists because a conversation-wide setting has no user row to live on — not because the conversation acquired an identity, which is the bullet immediately below and is unchanged

**Media**

- **Never store an R2 URL in any table** — presigned URLs expire in minutes (§ 9.); store ids and mint per request
- **`media.kind` is what a row is, and it is a column rather than a deduction.** It used to be probed out of `filename`, then `waveform_peaks`, then the mime — **in that order**, because `isFileMime` is true for `audio/mp4` and an unguarded order files every recording as an attachment. Those two columns are payload now and nothing branches on them. `kind` is `image | video | audio | voice | file`; `voice` and `audio` are **not** the same, a voice message being a recording that carries peaks and is drawn as a player
- **`media.scope` records what an object was uploaded for** — `chat | avatar | background | emoticon`. It was derivable from the key prefix and nowhere else, and every rule `validateMediaUpload` applies is a scope question rather than a kind one: whether a `_thumb` sibling is required, whether the row is a library candidate, which cache policy the route signs
- **`media.width` / `height` are nullable, and `0` is no longer a value they may hold.** That sentinel is why every reader had to test `filename` and `voice` before trusting them as a ratio; a NULL cannot be mistaken for one, and `Nullable<number>` makes the compiler ask for the branch the prose used to. **Never reintroduce a `?? 0`** — it puts the trap back one layer down
- **The shape of each kind is held by CHECK constraints, not by `validateMediaUpload` alone.** That function still validates, because it has to tell the user _why_; but two deployments write this table and neither can be the guarantee. The set ties `filename` to `file`, `waveform_peaks` and a duration to `voice`, a duration to `video`, and a box **and only a box** to `image`/`video`
- **Removal is one act on one column** (§ 10., § 18. #1.), and it is **not scoped to the uploader**. `deleted_at` destroys the object and the bubble draws a tombstone; it is a **soft** delete because `message_media`'s FK does not cascade and § 8.13.'s resume reconciliation needs the row to survive. The `archive_hidden_at` / `archive_added_at` pair beside it is **gone** (`0043`): membership in 보관함 is now "a live message carries it" and nothing else, so there is no second way in and no reversible half to confuse the delete with
- **A deleted row keeps its geometry on purpose.** It is what lets the tombstone occupy the box the picture did, so § 8.3.'s virtualized list re-measures nothing when one is deleted out from under a reader
- **The `gallery_*` columns were renamed `archive_*`** once 갤러리 became the name of **one shelf** (§ 10.) rather than the tab. They mean "in 보관함 at all", and leaving them would have been a new ambiguity rather than the old harmless one

**Storage keys (`0044`)**

- **`storage_reservations` is a claim on an R2 key, written before the object exists** (§ 9.): `r2_key`, `owner_id`, `expires_at`, `r2_purged_at`, plus a btree on `expires_at` for the reclaim's candidate query. With `media` beside it, every key in the bucket is named by a row in one of the two tables
- **`r2_key` is the primary key and there is deliberately no snowflake id.** A third id-minting table would put a **third** copy of the id format in the tree, which `AGENTS.md § 4.2.1.` names as the drift that fails the most quietly — and jandh-ops reads and writes both tables in plain SQL, where a branded string buys nothing. The key is already unique by construction and is the only thing either side joins on
- **`media.r2_purged_at` is the other half, and it is what keeps the row outliving the bytes.** Stamped only after R2 has confirmed the delete, so a failed one leaves the row exactly as the next pass expects to find it — no attempt counter, no dead-letter state
- **There is no deletion-queue table, and adding one would be a third name for rows that already exist.** The queue is a _query_: `deleted_at IS NOT NULL AND r2_purged_at IS NULL` on `media`, `expires_at <= now() AND r2_purged_at IS NULL` on the reservations. `media_pending_purge_idx` is the partial index that makes the first one cheap, and it stays nearly empty because a stamped row leaves it

**Events**

- `events.ends_at` is NOT NULL — the create form defaults it rather than admitting an open-ended event, which would need its own branch in every month-grid calculation. `color` is nullable
- **Recurrence is yearly-only** (anniversaries): never introduce RRULE or a general recurrence engine — project `yearly` rows onto the requested year on read. The relationship start date, 100-day marks, and yearly anniversaries are **not rows** (§ 11.2.)

**Emoticons**

- **`emoticon_packs.type` is where the two kinds are told apart, and the only place.** An item's kind follows from its pack, so `emoticon_items` gains no column and cannot disagree with one. An enum rather than the text-and-CHECK `media.kind` uses, because that pattern builds its CHECK from a value array in `shared/config/media.ts` — the emoticon copy of that file is mirrored (`AGENTS.md § 4.2.1.`), so reproducing the pattern would create a second surface to drift. **The kind is fixed at creation**: `PATCH` refuses a body carrying it rather than stripping it, which is what lets `0045`'s keyword condition stay an item-side trigger with nothing able to strand a pack's index rows under it
- **A mini never occupies `messages.emoticon_item_id`.** Sending one alone is the format above with a single fragment in it and `messages.type` stays `text`; drawing it large is a rendering rule read off the content (§ 13.6.), not a second kind of row
- **`emoticon_items.deleted_at` is the one delete column — `retired_at` is gone (`0049`).** Deleting any item, mini or not, immediately soft-deletes its row and its media and purges the objects; every bubble that already carries it draws a tombstone in its place. Soft for `media.deleted_at`'s reason — the box a bubble reserves is read off the row, so whatever stands in the item's place needs the row to outlive the asset
- **`emoticon_packs.deleted_at` (`0049`) is what lets a pack be deleted even after its items have been sent in chat.** A hard `DELETE` cascades into `emoticon_items`, which `messages.emoticon_item_id`'s missing cascade would then block — soft-deleting the pack sidesteps that instead of refusing the delete
- **`emoticon_items` carries no geometry of its own**; the box the virtualizer reserves (§ 8.3.) is `media.width` / `height` on the row the item's animated slot names, falling back to its still. Both are NOT NULL for an image by `media_box_is_visual_check`, and `emoticon_items_has_image_check` leaves every item one of the two — which together are why the `coalesce` behind `Emoticon.width` cannot answer null
- **The three slots each carry a UNIQUE index**, and they are what makes a retried registration idempotent (§ 13.3.): `insertMedia` is idempotent on `media.r2_key`, so a retry re-registers the same objects to the same ids and the second insert collides on whichever slot it names. They replaced `emoticon_items.r2_key`'s uniqueness, which was doing that job while one column held one image's worth of a row that may hold two
- `emoticon_packs.thumbnail_item_id` points **into `emoticon_items`**, which points back — a deliberate cycle, added by a separate `ALTER TABLE` with `ON DELETE SET NULL` so removing the item a pack uses as its icon cannot remove the pack
- **`user_emoticon_prefs.position` is `numeric` and nullable, and the null is the design** (§ 13.5.). It is a sparse key rather than an index: a pack the user has never moved keeps no row and sorts on the **milliseconds reconstructed from its own id** in that same numeric space. A `NOT NULL` default — or the `smallint sort_order` `0027` replaced — puts every untouched pack on one value, which is the reshuffle the sparse key exists to remove
- **The fallback is milliseconds and not the raw id, and that is measured rather than aesthetic.** `0027` backfilled `position` from `sort_order`, so the stored values are small ordinals (`-1`, `0`, `0.5`, … `15`) while the fallback sits at ~1.7e12 — which means the fallback's _magnitude_ is what decides where every never-moved pack sorts among the moved ones. Running both expressions against production, the millisecond form left all 58 (user, pack) pairs in exactly the order they were already in and `id::numeric` reordered four. No rebalance was needed as a result
- **Deleting an emoticon item is the same soft-delete whether or not it has been sent** (§ 13.4.). `messages.emoticon_item_id` keeps its lack of a cascade and `messages_type_payload_check` keeps forbidding a `set null`, so a sent item's row survives its own deletion — that is what leaves it there for the tombstone to draw from
- **`emoticon_keywords` is a projection of `emoticon_items.keywords`, never a source** (§ 13.9.1.). The array on the item stays the authored, case-preserving list every screen renders; this table holds one row per `lower(keyword)` and can be dropped and rebuilt from the items at any time. It is maintained by a **trigger** and deliberately not by `write-emoticon-item.ts`, which exists in both repositories — in app code one write would have two copies, and the index would drift the first time only one of them was fixed

**LLM**

- **`llm_agents` is the AI-answer fallback chain, not a config file** (§ 8.15.): `provider`/`model`/`api_key`/`priority`/`config` (jsonb) live in rows so a new provider is a row plus an implementation file, never a migration. `provider` is `text`, deliberately not an enum, for the same reason. No id — the primary key is `(provider, model, api_key)`
- `messages.llm_provider` and `messages.llm_model` are filled only on the `system`/`assistant_reply` branch of `messages_type_payload_check` — every other branch, including the rest of `system`, requires both `NULL`. Both are a snapshot of the `llm_agents` row that answered rather than a reference to it, so a bubble still names its provider and model after that row is gone

**Indexes and triggers**

- `message_media(media_id)`, `events(starts_at)`, `sessions(token_hash)`, plus `pg_trgm` + a GIN trigram index on `messages.text` (§ 8.6.). `messages` needs no paging index of its own — its primary key **is** the § 8.2. cursor. The `media` index needs the `id` tiebreaker because `created_at` is the **transaction** timestamp, so a multi-image send's rows compare equal — **paginate on the pair, never on `created_at` alone**
- `AFTER INSERT` on `messages` fires `pg_notify('new_message', { id })`; `AFTER INSERT` **and** `AFTER UPDATE` on `users` fire `pg_notify('user_changed', '')`, and so does `AFTER UPDATE` on `chat_settings` (§ 12.2. — UPDATE only, since the CHECK leaves it one row that the migration already inserted). Payloads are minimal because `NOTIFY` caps at 8000 bytes and § 8.4. refetches rather than trusting a payload. Two user triggers because a `WHEN` clause cannot reference `OLD` on an INSERT, so `WHEN (OLD.* IS DISTINCT FROM NEW.*)` rides the UPDATE trigger alone. That guard plus § 8.8.'s `WHERE last_read_at < $new` keeps the channel quiet under the app's highest-frequency write — **both halves are load-bearing**; dropping either puts a `GET /api/users` on every throttle tick

- **`typing` (§ 8.12.) has no trigger and no table.** It is the one channel published by application code, because there is no row whose write could fire it — which is the point: the app's highest-frequency signal costs no WAL at all. Flipping § 12.'s switch does write `users` and so does raise one `user_changed`, but that is a rare deliberate action and the refetch it causes is the same one a rename causes
- **`emoticon_keywords` carries two indexes on the one column, and each answers one direction of § 13.9.1.'s match**: a btree for the reverse probe (`keyword = ANY(…)`) and a `pg_trgm` GIN for the forward one (`keyword ILIKE '%term%'`). The GIN is hand-written for the reason `messages_text_trgm_idx` is — drizzle-kit models no operator class. `AFTER INSERT` and `AFTER UPDATE OF keywords` on `emoticon_items` rewrite that item's rows, the update narrowed by `IS DISTINCT FROM` so § 13.4.'s asset edit does not churn the index on every save. **There is no delete trigger**, because the FK's `ON DELETE cascade` already is one, and one written anyway would fire after the constraint had removed the rows. `sync_emoticon_keywords()` also skips an item whose pack is a mini, which is what keeps a name out of a search index (§ 13.9.1.)
- **`user_changed` is named for its first publisher, not for its subject.** `chat_settings` fires it too (§ 12.2.), because the client answers that event by refetching `GET /api/users` **whole** — and that payload carries the shared wallpaper beside the participant set. A channel of its own would have bought a second `LISTEN`, a second SSE event, a second client listener and a second request per resume, to deliver one id. What the channel actually means is "the conversation-wide payload is stale"

**Migration ordering — four rules, and they disagree with each other on purpose**

1. **Default: ship code first, migrate second.** Reversed, a first login against the previous build inserts without the new column, hits `23502`, and the OAuth catch flattens it into the generic `?error=failed` copy — nothing in the UI names the column
2. **A new column the new code reads runs the other way: migrate first, ship second.** Drizzle's `select()` names every column, so a build that knows `reply_to_id` cannot read `messages` at all until it exists (`42703`). A nullable column the previous build neither reads nor writes is invisible to it
3. **Dropping a `NOT NULL` column has no safe order** — code-first leaves the new build inserting without it (`23502`), migrate-first leaves the previous build inserting a column that is gone (`42703`). The safe shape is a split: relax the column, migrate, ship code that stops writing it, migrate again to drop it. `0007_drop_conversations` deliberately skips the split — with two users and a hand-run migrate the window costs a retried send, never data
4. **A widening of what jandh-ops treats as live deploys _last_, after both apps.** The first three rules are about a column and one deployment; this one is about a **third** process that reads this database and deletes bucket objects from what it finds. Until both apps are enqueueing their soft-deleted rows, § 12.4.'s sweep is the only thing reclaiming a withdrawn message's bytes — so widening its keep set ahead of them stops the reclaim without starting the replacement, and the bucket grows silently with nothing failing

**§ 12.2.'s move needs both rules at once, which is why it ships as two changes rather than one.** `0025` creates `chat_settings` and is rule 2 — the new code reads that table, so migrating first is mandatory, and the running build is untouched by a table it never names. Dropping `users.chat_background_media_id` is rule 1 and points the other way: `getSessionContext` selects `users` whole, so the _previous_ build still names the column and dropping it before the deploy fails every request that resolves a session.

**Those two cannot be sequenced inside one release, because `pnpm db:migrate` has no "apply up to N" — it runs every pending file in one go.** A documented "migrate, deploy, migrate again" is therefore not a procedure anybody can follow with the repo's own script. So the wallpaper change ships `0025` alone and **leaves the dead column declared in `schema/users.ts`**, which keeps schema and database in agreement and leaves `db:generate` with nothing pending. The drop is its own later change: delete the column from the schema, `pnpm db:generate`, migrate. **A migration whose safe order contradicts a sibling's does not belong in the same release as it.**

**`0027` and `0028` are rule 3 with one more term in it, because two deployments read this database.** `0027` adds `user_emoticon_prefs.position`, backfills it from `sort_order`, and **makes `sort_order` nullable**; `0028` drops that column and is a separate file for exactly this reason. jandh is on Vercel and jandh-emoticons on Netlify with deploy cycles of their own (§ 13.7.), and the dropped column is one the other app's running build still selects — so the order is add-and-backfill, deploy **both**, then drop. Putting the drop in the same file would have made `pnpm db:migrate` erase the middle step, since it runs every pending file in one go.

**The nullable step is not cosmetic, and it is what makes a one-sided deploy survivable at all.** jandh's new code writes `position` and never `sort_order`, so a `NOT NULL` column with no default refuses its first insert; jandh-emoticons' deployed build still writes `sort_order` and reads it through `coalesce(sort_order, 32767)`, which already tolerates the NULL the other app leaves. Both directions work only while the column exists and accepts nothing.

**`0041` and `0042` are rule 3 run in full, and they are the shape `0027`/`0028` is a special case of.** `0041` relaxes `NOT NULL` on `emoticon_items.r2_key`, `mime`, `width` and `height` and adds the three slot UNIQUE indexes; both deployments then ship code that reads and writes none of the six; `0042` drops them. The nullable step is what makes the middle state writable at all — the new code inserts an item naming only its slots, and four `NOT NULL` columns with no default refuse that insert. The indexes have to land in the **first** file for the same reason: they are what the new code's `ON CONFLICT DO NOTHING` relies on, and `r2_key`'s uniqueness is going.

**Two files in one commit still need the middle step run by hand, and that is the trap `0027`/`0028` only half closes.** Separate files buy nothing against `pnpm db:migrate`, which applies every pending one — so `0041` was applied with `0042` held out of `meta/_journal.json`, both deployments were shipped and confirmed, and only then was `0042` put back and applied. A checkout taken between those two moments has both files and no way to know it may only run the first. Land the drop in a **later commit** than the code it waits on, or the next person reads a green `db:migrate` as permission.

**`0030` was the one migration with no safe order at all, and downtime is what paid for it.** It renumbered every uuid key and `messages.id` together (§ 6. _Identifiers_), so the previous build and the new one each broke against the other's column types — rules 1 and 2 both point the wrong way and no split helps. It ran with both deployments down and both repositories shipped immediately after.

**`0031` is a separate file for the same reason `0026` is, and this is the part worth keeping.** `0030` left a `snowflake_user_id_map` table behind, because `emoticon_items` carries no owner column and the uploader inside an emoticon's R2 key could only be recovered through it while the objects were being moved. A one-off script read it, the stored objects were copied onto snowflake keys — copied, never moved, so restoring the dump stayed a rollback until the old objects were swept — and only then was the drop written. **A migration whose reader has not run yet does not belong in the tree at all**: `pnpm db:migrate` applies every pending file in one go, so a drop sitting beside `0030` would have erased the map before anything read it. Both scripts were deleted once they had run; they were one-shot work, and a script that cannot be run twice is worse than no script.

**`0044` is a clean rule 2 and needs none of the above.** It adds `storage_reservations` and a nullable `media.r2_purged_at`: the new code reads both, so migrating first is mandatory, and a table plus a nullable column is invisible to the running build. What it does need is rule 4 — jandh-ops' keep set may not learn about the reservations until both apps are writing them.

**`0054` and `0055` are a clean rule 2 pair** — `0054` widens `system_action` with `ALTER TYPE … ADD VALUE` and `0055` creates `llm_agents` and adds the two nullable `messages` columns the new code alone reads, so migrating first is mandatory for both and neither is visible to the running build until it ships.

**`0045` is a clean rule 2 with one extra term.** It adds `emoticon_packs.type`, `emoticon_items.deleted_at` and `messages.inline_emoticon_item_ids`, all of which the new code on **both** deployments reads, so migrating first is mandatory — and all three are invisible to a running build that names none of them. The extra term is that it also replaces `sync_emoticon_keywords()` and rebuilds `emoticon_keywords` in full (§ 13.9.1.), so the index is level with the function from the moment it applies rather than from the next write to each item.

`pnpm db:migrate` is **manual and decoupled from the deploy**.

---

## 7. Layout, Tab Bar, PWA ✅

The `(main)` layout is the app shell — max `576px` and centered below `md`, filling the window from it (`DESIGN.md § 3.1.`) — holding a per-screen header, the content, and the tab bar. Four tabs — `채팅` / `캘린더` / `보관함` / `설정` — icon + label, one active fill that travels between tabs (`DESIGN.md § 7.3.`), hover/active styling, `env(safe-area-inset-bottom)` honoured. From `md` the tab bar is replaced by a left `NavRail` (`DESIGN.md § 7.3.1.`), sharing `TABS`, `isUnderRoute` and the pending-tab-on-tap smoothing (`usePendingTab`) with it rather than duplicating either.

- [x] `NavRail` at `md`, `TwoPane`/`SidePanel` at `lg` (`DESIGN.md § 7.20.`), `--pane-open-width` widening at `xl`
- [x] `AppHeader hasSidePanel` + `SidePanelToggle`, `⌘\`
- [x] `BottomSheet`/`ActionSheet` desktop forms (`DialogShell`, `Popover`) at `md`
- [x] 보관함 desktop columns (fixed 5) and the shared 1–7 pinch/slider range, `jandh:archive-columns` cookie, View Transition on change (`DESIGN.md § 7.10.3.`)

- **`보관함`'s route is `/archive`, and it was `/gallery` until the tab had three shelves.** This paragraph used to say the two were deliberately allowed to differ, on the argument that a route is not a string the user ever sees and that `/settings/emoticons` = `이모티콘 관리` is the same arrangement. **That argument held for exactly as long as the route was a leaf.** It stopped holding when 파일 and 음성 became segments _nested under it_: `/gallery/files` does not merely fail to match the label, it asserts something false — that files are a kind of gallery — and unlike a leaf route a nested one is read as a sentence about what contains what. The rename was mechanical and total (route, `app/` folders, `GET /api/archive`, `widgets/archive-shelves`, every symbol), and `/settings/emoticons` is untouched because it is still a leaf and 이모티콘 관리 is genuinely inside 설정
- **All three shelves take a segment, and `/archive` itself renders nothing.** The 사진 shelf was briefly left on the bare `/archive`, to save the tab's own URL a redirect. **With three segments that is the wrong trade**: one shelf without a path of its own reads as the parent of the other two, which is the very thing that made `/gallery/files` wrong a paragraph above. So `/archive/gallery` is 사진, and `app/(main)/archive/page.tsx` is a `redirect()` to it
- **`ARCHIVE_ROUTE` survives as a prefix, not a destination.** It is what `isUnderRoute` fills the tab from and what `RouteTransition` orders the slide by (`DESIGN.md § 4.7.1.`), so it stays the `TAB_ROUTES` member — but `widgets/tab-bar` splits `href` off the route and points the tab at `ARCHIVE_GALLERY_ROUTE`, because a redirect on every tab tap is a thing the user can feel. **`TAB_ROUTES`' order and membership are untouched by that split**, which is what keeps its existing `WARN:` true
- **The 사진 shelf's path says `gallery` while its chip says `사진`.** `/archive/photos` was proposed and the user chose `gallery`. Recorded here so nobody "tidies" it later
- **`0043` is a drop-last migration and was run first, which took production down.** It carries every `archive_hidden_at` over to `deleted_at` and marks the library-only rows deleted, then drops both columns — and Drizzle's `select()` names every column it knows, so the **previous** build's `isInLibrary()` asked for `archive_hidden_at` and got `42703` on every 보관함 read. Recovery was to re-add both columns as nullable (additive, and the carried values stay in `deleted_at`, so old and new builds both work), deploy, then re-run the two DROPs by hand — which is the state the journal already records for `0043`, so nothing was renumbered. **The rule this proves: a DROP deploys first and migrates second, the exact inverse of § 6. rule 2, and the two orderings cannot be run by the same habit.** Splitting the carrying UPDATEs into a migrate-first file would have been better still — left in one file with the DROPs, every hidden row is back on the shelf for both participants for the length of the deploy gap
- **`0043` does not delete the R2 objects behind the rows it marks** — SQL cannot reach the bucket. What it leaves is not orphaned, though: every row it stamped is `deleted_at IS NOT NULL, r2_purged_at IS NULL`, which is exactly § 9.'s reclaim queue, so the bytes go on the next pass rather than waiting for a bucket listing to find them

- The layout is the **only** place a screen's session is resolved (`requireUserOrRedirect`, `AGENTS.md § 6.4.`); a page re-reads it only when it needs the row, and `cache()` makes that free
- The header is per-screen, not layout-owned, because it carries screen-specific actions — screens render `AppHeader` themselves
- `ScrollMemory` preserves each route's position across tab switches in **module scope**, not `sessionStorage`: a tab switch is a client navigation, a reload is meant to start at the top. Its timing quirks are load-bearing and documented at the source. **Chat is exempt** — its scroll belongs to the virtualizer (§ 8.3.)
- The Chat tab's unread badge is live (§ 8.8.): seeded by the layout's server render, incremented by the shell's SSE stream, replaced by `GET /api/chat/unread` on resume

**PWA**

- `app/manifest.ts`: name `J&H`, `display: "standalone"`, `theme_color`, `background_color`, icons 180/192/512 + maskable, generated by `pnpm icons` from a **path-only** wordmark (an SVG `<text>` icon renders in whatever font the machine has) and committed under `public/icons`. iOS reads `apple-touch-icon` from the markup, so the root layout's `metadata.icons.apple` is what "Add to Home Screen" uses
- **`share_target` puts the app in Android's and Windows' share sheets**, and it is the same feature the iOS 단축어 is where no share target exists. `GET` on `CHAT_ROUTE` itself rather than a route of its own: the share arrives as query parameters, `useShareTarget` strips them and sends through the room's own `submit`, so it takes the live-edge return, the 전송음 and § 8.5.'s retry that every other send does. **Stripping precedes the send** and is the whole of the duplicate defence — a reload or a bfcache restore re-runs it against a URL that no longer carries a share. Each sharing app fills a different subset and `text` routinely already holds the title and the link, so `SHARE_TARGET_PARAMS` is ordered richest-first and a part contained by an earlier one is dropped rather than repeated. **A share arriving with no session cookie is parked, not lost**: the proxy writes the query to `PENDING_SHARE_COOKIE_NAME` and `takePostLoginRoute` spends it — the search string alone, since the destination is `HOME_ROUTE` either way and a path from a cookie is an open redirect, and only under `MAX_PENDING_SHARE_BYTES`, since a browser drops an oversized `Set-Cookie` whole. **A cookie whose session row is gone is the one uncovered case**: `requireUserOrRedirect` bounces it to `SESSION_EXPIRE_ROUTE`, which the proxy never sees, and the share is lost — parking on that branch instead would resend the share of anyone who logs out and back in inside the cookie's window
- **Files are deliberately out of scope, on both platforms.** A file target means `POST` + `multipart/form-data`, which no page can receive — `sw.js` would have to intercept it and hand the bytes on — and § 9.'s re-encode is minutes of ffmpeg for a video, which is not something to run unattended behind an auto-send. The 단축어 cannot reach the policy either: it encodes no AVIF, cannot set a bitrate, and has no action that extracts a video frame, so § 9.'s required `_thumb` object simply cannot be produced there
- **The launch screen is markup-driven on iOS.** WebKit honours only `apple-touch-startup-image`, and only when the image matches the launch surface exactly — so `shared/config/pwa.ts` holds the device table (`device-width`/`device-height` do **not** swap with orientation on iOS, so one row yields the portrait and landscape pair) and both `pnpm icons` and `metadata.appleWebApp.startupImage` derive from it. A device with no matching row falls back to the manifest `background_color`, which is the same `canvas`
- **The launch screen only paints in standalone**, so the root layout MUST declare `metadata.appleWebApp.capable` — and, because Next emits only the unprefixed `mobile-web-app-capable`, `metadata.other` carries the legacy `apple-mobile-web-app-capable` for iOS below 15.4. iOS caches this markup when the icon is added, so changing it means deleting and re-adding the home-screen icon
- `widgets/install-guide` is a dismissible "Add to Home Screen" banner, shown only in an iOS **browser tab** (iPadOS 13+ reports a Macintosh UA, so the check needs `maxTouchPoints`). Its dismissal flag is in `localStorage` — § 5.2. bans that for auth state only — and every access goes through `safelyRun` / `safelyGet`, since blocked storage throws and this renders in the `(main)` layout, failing hydration on all four tabs
- **This section's original "offline support is out of scope, `sw.js` is push-only" has been reversed and is kept only so the reversal is legible.** § 16. is the live rule: the worker registers a `fetch` handler and caches exactly two things — `/_next/static/` and `/offline`. Nothing else may enter the cache, which is the narrow form of the ban this bullet used to state outright
- `/icons/*`, `robots.txt`, the manifest, `/sw.js`, and `/offline` MUST stay out of the proxy matcher: a redirect to `/login` breaks the install prompt, the browser fetches a worker without following redirects so a gated `/sw.js` fails registration outright, and a gated `/offline` would answer a failed navigation with a redirect to a page equally unreachable (§ 16.)
- Anything needing a **secure context** is unavailable at `http://<lan-ip>:3000` — service workers, push, and `crypto.randomUUID` (which is why browser code mints ids through `randomId()` in `shared/lib/identity/random.ts`). Test those over `pnpm dev:tunnel`

**Haptics** (`DESIGN.md § 7.15.`) — iOS has no Vibration API and iOS 26.5 removed the scripted-trigger workaround, so `HapticTap` is an invisible native `input[switch]` laid under the finger: **an element, never a function call.**

- It rides a `haptic` prop on `Button` / `IconButton` / `Chip` / `Switch` / `SettingsRow` / `Link`, and is composed by hand for raw buttons (composer send, day cell, emoticon grid, list rows, staged-attachment removal, `ActionSheet`)
- It fires on a **committed change of state or a selection among peers** — never on dismissing a surface or going back, and it **cannot** fire on a drag threshold such as swipe-to-reply (§ 8.10.), because no scripted trigger exists on 26.5+
- A release that travelled `GESTURE_SLOP` is a drag, not a tap: the overlay refuses it, so neither the control nor the Taptic engine hears it (`DESIGN.md § 7.15.2.`)
- On a target that **tiles a scroller** (the month grid, the emoticon grid, a `SettingsRow`, the full-width name of an 이모티콘 관리 row) it needs `keepsScroll`, which moves the tap to a `<label>` — the switch keeps a drag of its own and would otherwise leave the surface unscrollable. That mode is **opt-in**: inside an `<a>` the anchor eats the label's activation and the tick vanishes (`DESIGN.md § 7.15.1.`)

---

## 8. Chat Tab (KakaoTalk-style) ✅

> **Only layout and interaction rules come from KakaoTalk**, never its colors or shapes (Kakao yellow, sky-blue background, drawn tails). `DESIGN.md § 2.2.` and `§ 6.` are the sole source of every visual spec below.

### 8.1. UI ✅

**Landed:** notch-corner bubbles (mine right, theirs left), avatar + nickname on the first message of a group, one timestamp per same-minute group on its last message, date divider pills (`오늘`, `어제`, `2026년 8월 3일 월요일`), the auto-growing composer with `+` and emoticon toggle (the send button appears beside the toggle once there is something to send, rather than replacing it), long-press / right-click → `ActionSheet`, link previews and tappable links (§ 8.9.), the scroll-to-bottom button appearing ~200px from the newest message with an incoming count (`새 메시지 3`), and media messages in full.

- A hardware keyboard sends on Enter and breaks the line on Shift+Enter; on a coarse pointer Enter stays a newline, since the iOS keyboard has no send key (`useIsCoarsePointer`)
- Tapping send keeps the keyboard open: the button cancels `pointerdown` so the tap never blurs the field, and `submit` refocuses inside the click gesture
- `DELETE /api/messages/{id}` soft-deletes and `PATCH /api/messages/{id}` rewrites the text (§ 8.13.), both scoped to the sender and both answering **404** for someone else's message and for one that never existed alike, so neither can probe ids (§ 14.)
- The tab bar and install banner leave while the keyboard is up. `useIsVirtualKeyboardOpen` requires both a drop below the tallest height seen at the current width **and** an editable `activeElement` — height alone misreads a collapsing address bar, focus alone survives Android's back button. They leave by **collapsing `BottomOverlay`, not unmounting**, and the shell eases its height over the same 200ms, so `--bottom-inset` carries the composer up on one timeline
- **The two 200ms eases do not end together, and the follow must not assume they do.** The bar collapse runs once, from the frame `useIsVirtualKeyboardOpen` flips; the shell restarts its ease on every step WebKit reports the height in (`DESIGN.md § 3.4.`), so it finishes last. Through that tail the history keeps shortening while the clearance is already at rest — so `useComposerClearance` re-pins on a **scroller that changed height** as well as on a clearance that did — and the two branches answer differently, because a height change is not always a keyboard. The clearance branch keeps the `AT_BOTTOM_THRESHOLD`-wide flag; the height branch subtracts the frame's own shrink and demands the reader was at the true bottom before it, so a rotation or a desktop window resize cannot throw someone re-reading 150px up to the live edge.
