- **The `AudioContext` is minted before the `await`, inside that same gesture.** WebKit starts one constructed off a gesture stack `suspended`, and a suspended graph renders nothing through the analyser — every sample reads as silence, the live meter never leaves its floor, and `toWaveformPeaks` stores 56 zeroes under audible speech
- **A refusal that never reaches `requesting` is reported by return value, not by state.** An engine with no `MediaRecorder` refuses before the state ever changes, and the bar closes itself off a state change — so without it the strip stands in the composer over a clock that will never move
- **The sample interval is cleared by whichever control ends the recording**, not left to `onstop`. That event is a queued task, and the 50ms tick can fire inside the gap: it re-raises the cap toast and calls `stop()` on a recorder already `inactive`, which throws `InvalidStateError` out of the interval
- **A pick abandoned while the permission prompt is still up is answered after the prompt, not before it.** 취소, 완료 and the bar's unmount all land in a window where there is no recorder to stop and no stream to release, so neither can undo anything — the request is marked abandoned instead and the stream is stopped the moment it arrives. Without it a permission granted after the bar closed opened the microphone with no UI left to close it, left the session in `play-and-record`, and left `start`'s own in-flight guard refusing every later attempt until a reload. 완료 is the same hazard read the other way: it means "finish", and without the flag a permission granted afterwards **starts** the microphone
- **`start` guards on the request in flight, not on the open session, and it clears the abandon flag before that guard rather than after it.** The session ref is assigned only after the `await`, so the whole permission window reads as "nothing running" — and the bar starts the microphone from a layout effect, which React runs setup → cleanup → setup under StrictMode. That opened **two** streams: the ref kept the second, and the first's sample loop went on writing the elapsed seconds off a `startedAt` of its own, so the clock flickered between two times twenty times a second over a microphone nothing was left to close, which after `MAX_VOICE_DURATION` re-raised the cap toast on every tick forever. The flag has to be cleared ahead of the guard for the same reason it exists at all: the teardown between those two setups abandons the request the second one rejoins, and left set it closes the microphone the bar had just opened
- **Capture moves the page's audio session to `play-and-record` and returns it through `declareRestingAudioSession`**, never by naming a category of its own (§ 13.6.). The resting category is written down in exactly one place, so revisiting it cannot leave capture behind. § 5.3. carries the real-device check this needs

**Sending**

- **The `+` sheet carries a `음성` row; the composer's control row is untouched.** `DESIGN.md § 6.6.` fixes that row at attach, field, emoticon toggle and send, and a fourth 44px target in it is what makes the round buttons oval on an engine without `field-sizing-content`. The row is last in the sheet, because the two above it open a picker and this one opens a microphone
- **A recording is sent outright rather than staged into the tray.** `useMediaSelection` holds one list that `toBubbles` splits by kind, so a staged recording would sit beside photos competing for a § 6. row it cannot share — and 완료 already reads as the commitment a tray exists to defer
- **It goes through `sendMedia`, not a path of its own** — that is where the § 9. upload, the per-bubble progress, the § 8.5. retry and the one delivery queue live, and a second copy of any of them would drift
- **`toBubbles` splits on three kinds, not two.** A recording carries no filename, so the old `filename !== null` split grouped it with the photos either side of it — one bubble whose first cell decides the layout for all of them. `isHomogeneousBatch` holds the same rule at the server, where a voice set is additionally capped at **one**: there is no layout for two
- **The optimistic bubble plays from a local blob URL**, handed to the cell's `originalUrl` — the one draft that supplies one, since a photo's preview is a thumbnail and has no full-size object until it registers. **A pending voice bubble is therefore dimmed but not inert**, which is the opposite of every other attachment and was wrong in the first cut: the player was gated on `isPending`, so the wiring that supplies the blob was dead code. Only a missing source disables the control, and the label says `음성 메시지, 전송 중이에요` because dimming reaches no screen reader. Retiring that bubble must `stopVoice()` **before** revoking, and only when `isVoiceActive` says the shared player is parked on that source: unconditional, it cuts off an older voice note the reader is listening to; reversed, `stopVoice`'s own `load()` runs against a freed blob

**Where a voice message is not**

- **It reaches the library under a shelf of its own** (§ 10.). `isOfKind`'s 사진 branch excludes it explicitly, because a recording shares `filename IS NULL` with a photo and would otherwise tile the grid with a broken image over a viewer that opens on nothing; 파일 excludes it for free. **The 음성 segment cost one clause (`isNotNull(media.waveformPeaks)`) and one literal in `LIBRARY_KINDS`** — no column and no migration, which is precisely the cheapness the derived discriminator was chosen for. An attached `.m4a` stays on 파일 and does **not** join it. That used to be justified by the two answering a tap differently — a recording played, a file downloaded — and § 9.1. has since given the file a play control, so **that justification is gone and the split is not.** The durable reason is the discriminator itself: peaks are sampled off an `AnalyserNode` while recording, which only works because `MAX_VOICE_DURATION` bounds a recording at two minutes, and an attachment is unbounded. A recording is audio the app has a waveform for; an attached audio file is audio it does not. Telegram's Music-versus-Voice split lands in the same place for a related reason, but the cost of the waveform is what makes it forced here rather than chosen
- **`toMediaKind` answers `voice` before anything else**, and off the first item alone — a voice bubble is always one clip. Its § 8.10. quote and § 16.1. push body read `음성`
- **It carries no § 8.10. quote thumbnail and no § 8.11. 공유**, and both tests are `filename` **and** `voice` rather than `filename` alone. A recording has no `_thumb` object, so the quote's `<img>` would be pointed at the audio itself — which the id route now serves as the original — over a `QUOTE_THUMBNAIL` the § 8.3. estimate had already reserved. 공유 is withheld for the reason § 9.1. gives for a file: `extensionForMime` answers `bin` for `audio/mp4`, so the sheet would be handed `{uuid}.bin` under a dialog worded 사진. The client's optimistic quote and `listReplyPreviews` answer this **the same way**, or the tile appears and disappears under the reader when the echo lands
- **It never survives a search.** The composer's whole stack is hidden and `inert` while one is open (§ 8.6.), and the recorder is **closed** rather than hidden with it — hidden, the microphone runs on with 취소 and 완료 both unreachable, and `MAX_VOICE_DURATION` then sends a recording the user walked away from two minutes earlier
- **It never survives leaving the room.** The shared element outlives every bubble addressing it, so `ChatRoom` stops it on unmount — unlike § 13.6.'s two-second ping a recording runs for minutes, and no screen outside the room draws a transport that could pause it

**Migration**

- **`0021` is a migrate-first migration** (§ 6. rule 2), and for the § 9.1. reason exactly: Drizzle's `select()` names every column, so a build that knows `waveform_peaks` cannot read `media` at all until it exists (`42703`) — and every chat page with an attachment, the whole 보관함 tab, and every `canReadMedia` goes through that read. Run `pnpm db:migrate` **before** deploying. A nullable column the previous build neither reads nor writes is invisible to it, so the reverse order is the only unsafe one

---

## 10. Library Tab (`보관함`) ✅

**Landed** in full — both jumps included, the link out to the message and the arrival on a given tile's position, and the grid windowed under them.

The tab is `보관함` with three segments, `갤러리`, `파일` and `음성`; the prefix is `/archive`, renamed from `/gallery` when the third segment made the old path assert something false (§ 7.). **The first was called `사진` until it stopped being true** — the shelf has always held videos too, and the label said otherwise on every screen. The rename is what forced the two axes apart (below). Each shelf is a route of its own (`/archive/gallery`, `/archive/files`, `/archive/voice`) and the bare prefix redirects to the first, so the back button walks between them and the chips are links rather than a control that changes nothing in the URL.

**Why 보관함 is a top-level tab, where KakaoTalk puts the same thing in a chat room's drawer.** Kakao has hundreds of rooms, so "everything we exchanged" is only answerable _per room_ — the drawer is subordinate to a room because it has to be. **This app has exactly one conversation, permanently (§ 6.), so "this room's drawer" and "the app's library" are the same set of rows.** Given that, a tab is strictly better than a drawer: it is one tap from anywhere instead of two from inside the room, and it does not compete with the chat header's own controls. The constraint that forced Kakao's placement does not exist here, and a second conversation is ruled out at the schema level — if that ever changes, this tab is one of the things that has to move, alongside the § 6. rewrite.

**Segments, not a second tab.** 파일 shares the query, the cursor, the month sections, the selection and the delete with 갤러리; only the row and the save route differ. A fifth tab would also break the four-column bar `DESIGN.md § 7.3.` is dimensioned for. **From `lg` the three segments also drive a side panel** — a pill switcher over a month-jump list, persisted across all three shelf routes by the route layout rather than remounted per page (`DESIGN.md § 7.10.3.`).

- **A shelf and a kind are two different axes, and conflating them is what made 사진 a lie.** `LibraryShelf` is `gallery | file | voice` — where a tile stands; `media.kind` (§ 6.) is what the object is. `SHELF_KINDS` maps between them and 갤러리 holds two kinds, which is exactly why the two cannot be one list. A future shelf is a mapping change rather than a schema change
- **`isOfShelf` reads `media.kind IN (…)` and nothing else.** It used to be nullable probing whose 사진 branch was literally "neither of the others", so a kind added without a clause did not open an empty segment — it **spilled into 사진**. That hazard is gone with the branch: a kind absent from `SHELF_KINDS` appears on no shelf at all
- **Membership and shelf are two predicates.** `isInLibrary()` is "not deleted **and** carried by a visible message"; `isOfShelf` is the segment. Only the first is security-shaped, and one 삭제 serves every segment because it is narrowed by membership alone. **The message is the whole of membership** (§ 18. #1.), which is what makes 보관함 exactly "everything the conversation exchanged" — and what makes a withdrawn message take its media off the shelf with it
- **A shelf is cut by what a tap means, never by mime.** 갤러리 opens the viewer, 파일 downloads, 음성 plays. That is why an attached `.m4a` stays on 파일 while a recording goes to 음성 — the two are the same bytes and a different act
- **A shelf's label is not a noun for its contents, and the two are separate tables.** `LIBRARY_SHELF_LABELS` names the screen — the chips, the staging sheet's title, the "landed on another shelf" toast — while what is _counted_ and reported as missing stays 사진 (`LOAD_FAILURE_SUBJECTS`, `toMediaCountUnit`). `갤러리를 더 불러오지 못했어요` would say the screen failed rather than the photos
- **`GET /api/archive` takes `shelf`, and keeps accepting the old `kind=photo|file|voice` as a deprecated alias.** A tab left open across the deploy goes on sending the old parameter, and this route is the only thing between it and a 400 on every page it asks for. Removed in the following cycle
- Images **and videos** sent in Chat **appear under 갤러리 automatically** — `media` is the single source, nothing is copied. **A § 9.1. file attachment appears under 파일** by the same rule; it is excluded from the 사진 grid alone (`filename IS NULL`), because it has no `_thumb` object and a tile of one is a broken image the viewer would open on nothing
- Reverse-chronological grid with month section headers (`DESIGN.md § 7.10.`, § 7.10.3.), the month resolved through `toDayKey` so a photo sent at 00:30 KST falls under the month it was taken in. **Column count is 1–7, not a fixed 3**: mobile pinches it (`useIsCoarsePointer`-gated), desktop fixes it at 5, and both read/write one cookie (`jandh:archive-columns`) so a route change and a fresh SSR agree — the header's `열 개수` control offers the same range for a pointer that cannot pinch. **파일 is the same month sections as a one-column list of `FileCard` rows** — `toArchiveSections` takes `ArchiveMedia` and needed no change at all
- **The grid is windowed with `@tanstack/react-virtual`, and one item is a _line_ of three tiles or a month label** — never a tile (§ 8.3., `DESIGN.md § 7.10.`). It rendered every loaded tile while a page was all the shelf could hold; the position jump below parks the window mid-history, where paging on from there is unbounded in a way scrolling from the newest never was. The month label is an item of the same list because it has to scroll **with** the grid, which is the same reason it is not `sticky`
  - **Nothing is measured, and that is the point.** A line is a square whose side is arithmetic on the grid's own width and a label is a fixed box, so `estimateSize` is exact — which removes every § 8.3. measurement trap at once (no `measureElement` in the commit phase, no estimate→actual correction for WebKit to drop mid-gesture, no rounding creep). It costs one duplication, the gutters, which are said in the CSS and again in the arithmetic the offsets are summed from
  - **The grid scrolls with the document, so it takes `useWindowVirtualizer`** and needs the grid's own offset down the page as `scrollMargin` (`DESIGN.md § 3.3.`). Pointed at the layout's `<main>` slot instead it reads a `scrollTop` that is permanently `0` off an `offsetHeight` equal to the whole list, and the range comes back as every row — the windowing is off, and nothing on screen says so. That offset is not a constant: the § 7.10. chips unmount in selection mode and the safe area changes on rotation, so it is re-read off layout rather than configured — and `window.scrollY` is added back into the reading, or the whole window resolves a screenful too high as soon as anybody scrolls. **`scrollY` alone**, never on top of a delta against `documentElement`'s own rect: that rect's `top` already _is_ `-scrollY`, so spelling it the element-scroller way counts the scroll twice and the window drifts a further screenful off with every screen passed. It is read on **those two triggers only** — the selection-mode swap, and a `ResizeObserver` on the root element for everything that resizes it — and never once per render: each reading forces layout three times, and a render is the common case rather than the rare one, since every tile a sweep crosses rewrites the selection. That is the gesture whose frames are worth the most
  - **`"use no memo"` is required on the component**, and it replaces a bailout it used to get for free. The React Compiler skips any component calling `useVirtualizer`, because TanStack Virtual returns functions that go stale once memoized — but that list is keyed on the one name, and `useWindowVirtualizer` is not on it. Compiled, the grid memoizes `getVirtualItems` and stops re-windowing as the reader scrolls
  - **The sweep is the constraint the windowing had to be built around**, and it survives because the range is positions in the flat cell list while only the hit test is a node — see `DESIGN.md § 7.10.` for why the **overscan** is load-bearing for that gesture
  - **The shelf's own skeleton stands in until the scroller is in hand**, which is one layout effect. A virtualizer with no scroll element renders no rows, so the alternative is a blank frame between `loading.tsx`'s tiles and the first windowed render — `DESIGN.md § 7.8.`'s objection to a fallback of the wrong shape applies just as much to a fallback of no shape. It draws the **month label placeholder as well as the tiles**, and matching the cell count is only half of matching the shape: this frame sits between `loading.tsx` and the first real month row, so tiles alone step the grid 28px up and then 28px back down — the very shift the frame exists to prevent. The paging sentinel is inside the same branch: mounted beside a skeleton a ninth of the window's height it is in view on every open, and spends a page request before a real row is drawn
- **Each shelf streams behind its own `loading.tsx`, never a shared one at `/archive`.** They were one file while the difference looked cosmetic, and it was not: the fallback drew 파일's 56px rows on 사진 too, so the grid opened on a screenful of tall bars that were then swapped out for square tiles. The four route files are one line each — `LibraryFallback` holds the header, the chips and all three shapes, so the shelf a fallback is for is the one thing a route says (`DESIGN.md § 7.8.`)
- **`FileCard` is one component in `shared/ui`, rendered by the bubble and by 보관함 alike** (`DESIGN.md § 6.5.`). Copied, the row a file is drawn as would drift between the two places it appears
- **The 파일 list has no hold and no sweep.** The grid's sweep fills a _range in grid order_ across three columns; in one column that range is the two taps it saves, and owning `touchmove` to buy them is not a trade. A checkbox states the selection instead
- **Tapping a file downloads it.** § 9.1. serves one as `Content-Disposition: attachment` whatever the query asks for, so there is no inline view and `MediaViewer` is not mounted on this segment at all
- **파일's selection bar keeps all three rows, and 공유 is a real one.** 저장 is `downloadMedia` directly rather than `useMediaShare`'s save, and 공유 goes to the sheet with `names` so each `File` carries its stored filename (§ 9.1.). Every counted sentence on this segment is in `개`, which is why the counter is a parameter of `toShareCapMessage`, `useMediaShare` and `MediaShareDialog` rather than a literal — and why the particles are chosen by `josa` (`3장을` but `3개를`, `3장이` but `3개가`) rather than written into the sentence — `AGENTS.md § 0.4.`
- Cursor-based infinite scroll on **one `media` id** (§ 6.), **shared by every segment** — same page size, same sentinel rule. It was a `(created_at, id)` pair until the id became the ordering key on its own; the pair existed to break ties `created_at` produced within a single multi-image send, and an id has none to break. 사진 pages it in **both** directions, since § 10.'s position jump can park the window in the middle of the shelf; the list shelves only ever start at the newest row and page downward. Both halves are required together; a half cursor is a `400`. The timestamp is bound as an **ISO string with an explicit `::timestamptz`** — a bare template parameter carries no column to take its type from, so postgres.js refuses a `Date`, and without the cast Postgres resolves the row-constructor parameter to `text`, where `2026-08-06T…` sorts after `2026-08-1…`. It is exact **only because the column is `timestamptz(3)`**
- **A page arriving that deduplication empties still advances paging.** The sentinel effect keys off the load _finishing_, not the row count changing — keyed off the count, a fully duplicate page leaves the list identical and nothing ever asks again
- **The shelf opens on a given photo's _position_, not merely on a photo.** 채팅's viewer taps through to `ARCHIVE_TARGET_PARAM` (`?target={mediaId}`, the mirror of § 8.6.1.'s `?message=`), and the grid has to load **around** that id rather than from the newest: the shelf keyset-pages `ARCHIVE_PAGE_SIZE` at a time, so an older photo is not off screen — it was never fetched, and no amount of scrolling reaches what is not there. This is § 8.6.1.'s jump with the same vocabulary and one fewer moving part, since a shelf has no live edge to be dragged back to
  - **`listArchiveMedia` takes `around` and it wins over `before`**, exactly as `listMessages` resolves that pair (§ 8.2.) — the two name different windows. It resolves the target's `(created_at, id)` **through the shelf predicate**, so a 파일 id handed to 사진 is not a real position on this listing and is not treated as one
  - **Half the page newer than the tile, the rest at or older than it** — `<=`, or the very tile the window is centred on is dropped. Both halves reuse the keyset pair and its `::timestamptz` / `::uuid` casts (§ 6.); nothing new is compared on `created_at` alone
  - **The older half asks for the whole limit and is sliced down after.** Paging reads "is there more behind this" off the page's own length, and a target within half a page of the newest leaves the newer query able to answer with a handful of rows — a short page there would stop the shelf paging at its first screenful
  - **A target that is not on the shelf answers with the newest page, never an error.** `?target=` is a URL anybody can type, and it also names a row a 삭제 may have taken out from under the link since it was drawn. The screen then says so once, `사진을 찾지 못했어요`, which is § 8.6.1.'s `missing` — the shelf itself is fully usable, so the sentence is all that is owed
  - **The id is shape-checked in the route, as § 8.6.1.'s `?message=` and § 11.5.'s `?day=` are.** It is bound with an explicit `::uuid`, so `?target=abc` is a driver error rather than a miss — a 500 on a hand-typed URL. The check is **`z.uuid()` itself, never a regex written out beside it**: this id has two readers, the route and `GET /api/archive`, and zod's shape is stricter than the obvious hand-rolled one — it requires the RFC version nibble and variant, so the loose form passes a copied regex and then 400s at the endpoint, which is the exact input class the check is for
  - **The landing tile flashes** (`DESIGN.md § 6.8.`, § 7.10.), for the reason 대화에서 보기 flashes on arrival and a search jump does not: nothing else on the screen says which tile the tap was for. It is consumed **once at mount** — a URL is a destination, not an instruction that repeats
  - **The scroll is asserted two frames in, not one.** `ScrollMemory` restores this route's remembered offset inside a `requestAnimationFrame` of its own, so a jump made any earlier is overwritten by wherever the reader last left the shelf; the second frame is also the first one with the scroller captured and the tile size measured
- **A jumped window pages upward as well as down**, so scrolling up from a deep target reaches the newest photo rather than stopping where the around query left off. `listArchiveMedia` takes an `after` cursor beside `before`, and the shelf carries a second sentinel above the window
  - **`after` is ordered ascending and reversed, never descending.** Descending from the top of the shelf answers with the newest rows in the whole library, which is a page from somewhere else; ascending from the cursor is the only ordering that returns the rows **contiguous** with the window being extended
  - **Whether there is anything above the window is read off the window itself**, not reported by the server: the around query spends `floor(ARCHIVE_PAGE_SIZE / 2)` on the newer half, so the target's own distance from the front of the array _is_ how many newer rows came back, and a half that filled is the same "there may be more" test every other page here is judged by. No target — or one the server could not place, which answers with the newest page — means the window already starts at the newest row, and `indexOf` answering `-1` must not read as one
  - **The page is fetched during the scroll and inserted only once the scroller is still**, through § 8.3.'s `useSettledCommit`, which moved into `shared` when this became its second caller. Inserting above the reader moves every row below it, and WebKit hands the scroll offset to the compositor for the length of a gesture, so the correction that answers for the move is dropped if it lands mid-flick
  - **The correction is a total-size delta, where chat re-finds a keyed row.** No key survives this prepend: lines are cut from each month's own start, so a page merging into the newest month by anything other than a multiple of three regroups every line in it and no row is the same row afterwards — offsets are the only thing still comparable across the commit. It is safe as a **delta**, which § 8.3. warns against, only because the grid measures nothing and sets no `anchorTo`: it is the single writer of this scroll, and adding either mechanism would make the two corrections compound
  - **The virtualizer's own `anchorTo` was the alternative and is the one thing it cannot do**, for that same reason. It corrects in-render — which is strictly better, since the rows for the corrected offset are the ones that get rendered — but it finds its anchor **by key**, and this prepend is exactly the case with no surviving key. What that costs here is one frame in which the mounted range still belongs to the pre-correction offset: the programmatic scroll's own event and React's flush both land before paint in principle, so it should not be visible, but § 8.3.'s history with dropped corrections on WebKit is precisely this area and it wants checking on a device. If it does show, the fixes in order are a wider overscan or a per-insert `anchorTo` toggle
  - **The upward sentinel carries no indicator, and is zero height.** Anything drawn there is above the reader, so raising it while the page loads and dropping it when the page lands moves the grid twice — which is the shift the correction exists to undo. § 7.8.'s spinner-for-a-wait would be right anywhere else on this screen
  - **`scrollMargin` is measured from the sized container, not the grid's root**, because the upward sentinel now sits between the two. Measuring the ancestor puts every row offset that much too high
  - **An upload still prepends without a correction, and that is the difference between the two prepends.** A row the reader just uploaded belongs at the top of the screen, so the shelf keeps its scroll offset and lets the new tile arrive into view; a page fetched above them is content they did not ask to be moved by. One list, two prepends, and only the second one anchors
  - **The upward cursor is the newest **paged** row, tracked apart from `media[0]`.** An upload prepends ahead of it, and paging upward off the front of the list would then ask for rows newer than the newest row in the library — the empty answer latches "there is nothing above this" on for good and strands every photo between the jumped window and the live edge behind a scroll that can never reach it. It is a **position** rather than a row, so a 삭제 removing the tile it names costs nothing: the cursor is compared, never dereferenced
  - **The two prepends are made to commute, by placing the page rather than sequencing the upload.** A held page is newer than the window and **older** than an upload, so inserted blindly at the front it sits ahead of one — out of order, which puts a second section carrying an already-used `monthKey` in the list and duplicates a React key. `insertNewer` therefore inserts at the page's **sorted place** in the shelf's own `(created_at, id)` order, which lands it behind the upload. The scan is bounded in practice: only an upload can be newer than a page fetched above the window, and an upload is always at the very front
  - **That is what lets the upload leave a held page alone, and it had to.** The upload used to flush the page first, so it could not land in front of it — but that flush ran from the hook, and the scroll correction is armed in the **grid**, which is the only thing that can see the scroller. So up to `ARCHIVE_PAGE_SIZE` rows were inserted above the reader with nothing holding their offset, and an upload finishing while the reader scrolled a jumped window threw the shelf thousands of pixels. Sorted insertion lets the page keep waiting for the settle it was always meant to wait for, and the upload stays the one prepend that deliberately does not hold the scroll
  - **The upward page carries no generation token**, where § 8.6.1.'s windows do. It is measured from `windowTopRef`, which an upload deliberately does not move, so nothing an upload does while the page is in flight can make its rows the wrong ones — and where they belong is now answered by the ordering rather than by when they arrive. The token this replaced discarded a perfectly good page on every upload
- Tapping opens the fullscreen viewer, which swipes across the **whole loaded library** rather than the month it was opened in. It is the § 8.1. viewer, moved to `shared/ui` (§ 2.)
- **The viewer mounts only the current slide and its two neighbours, and only the current one reaches for its original.** Every slide requesting its original on mount is bounded inside a chat bubble by `MAX_MEDIA_PER_MESSAGE` but by nothing here — opening one photo after three pages of scrolling started 180 requests. Slides outside the window render an empty box at the stored ratio, so the snap track never resizes
  - **The neighbours stay on the thumbnail they are already drawn from**, and the size of an original is why. § 9. stores `thumb` and `original` and nothing between, so a slide paints the untouched upload — a 12MP phone photo is ~48MB of decoded surface, and three at once is ~150MB. Crossing back and forth remounts the neighbours and re-decodes them every time, which is a visible stall on desktop and, on iOS, the point where WebKit stops painting new images at all: the element still reports `load` and the right `naturalWidth`, so `PreloadImage` goes to `loaded`, drops the preview and fades the blur out over nothing. The reader is left on a bare `scrim` under live chrome, and it is the slide **beyond** the ones they were flipping between that goes first — the one whose decode was started and abandoned on every pass. Warming a neighbour costs a full-resolution decode and the swipe it saves is one the thumbnail already covers
- **Multi-select is entered from the header control _or_ by holding a tile**, which picks that tile. A tap still opens the viewer, since the hold swallows the `click` it ends in (§ 8.11.). The hold is **touch-only**: `contextmenu` is left alone, because the header control is already this gesture's pointer equivalent (`DESIGN.md § 3.2.`). It is withheld while an upload is in flight. `MAX_ARCHIVE_SELECTION` caps a selection, said in the UI rather than reported as a rejected request
- **The hold arms a sweep: keep dragging and the whole range from the held tile to the one under the finger takes the hold's action**, in grid order, row by row. It is a **range, not a trail** — a trail leaves a diagonal drag full of holes, and the range is what lets pulling the finger back release what it left behind. Every range is applied to the selection **as it stood when the hold fired**, never to the last range. Holding an _already_ selected tile sweeps deselection. The drag scrolls the grid while the finger rests within `EDGE_ZONE` of the scroller's edge, and re-tests what is under the finger on every frame the grid moved — a parked finger sends no move event, and there the tiles are what travels. It is **touch events, not pointer events**: only a non-passive `touchmove` can `preventDefault` the scroll, and `touch-action` cannot stand in, since the finger is already down when the sweep arms. Four consequences of owning the gesture that far down:
  - **The listeners are attached inside the hold, never from an effect.** An effect runs a frame later, and a finger lifting inside that window is a `touchend` nothing is listening for — leaving a document-wide `preventDefault` swallowing every later scroll, with a live anchor selecting behind it
  - **The sweeping finger is tracked by `Touch.identifier`**, adopted on the first move as the touch nearest where the hold fired — not `touches[0]`, which is a thumb that has been resting on the grid since before it. Only that finger's `touchend` ends the sweep, or, before the first move, a release within `GESTURE_SLOP` of the anchor
  - **The tile under the finger is found with `elementsFromPoint`.** The selection bar floats over most of the bottom edge zone and takes pointer events, so the single topmost element there is the bar and the range would stop growing exactly where the auto-scroll is feeding it
  - **The cap truncates from the anchor outwards.** A sweep running _up_ the grid is ordered away from the finger, so keeping the first `MAX_ARCHIVE_SELECTION` would strip the held tile and light up a distant block instead. The cap toast carries a fixed `id`, because a sweep held past the cap reaches it again on every tile
- **Saving the selection takes one of two routes, chosen by platform — and the platform that branches is iOS, not touch.** (**갤러리 only.** A 파일 selection always downloads, `savesToPhotoLibrary` is false for it, and none of the rest of this bullet applies there — § 9.1.) On iOS it is `navigator.share({ files })`, because the OS share sheet is the **only** way a web page reaches the iOS photo library: a `Content-Disposition: attachment` download lands in Files, where nobody who tapped 저장 over a photo will look. Everywhere else it is one top-level navigation per file, staggered by `ARCHIVE_DOWNLOAD_STAGGER`, because navigations started in one tick collapse into one. **Android is on the download side deliberately**: its share sheet has no OS-provided "save to library" action at all, only whichever apps are installed, while a download lands in `Download/` and MediaStore indexes it straight into the library — so there the sheet is the unreliable route and the download is the good one. The branch is `isIos()` **and** `navigator.canShare`, not either alone. It cannot be a pointer check: an iPad with a trackpad reports a fine pointer and still cannot get a download into Photos, and an Android phone reports a coarse one and does not need the sheet. It cannot be feature detection either — nothing exposes "will this download be findable", so this is the one sanctioned user-agent branch (`AGENTS.md § 4.2.`). (§ 8.11.'s 공유 shares the machinery but not this branch: it names the share sheet outright, so it uses one wherever there is one)
- **The share route buffers the originals first, so it is bounded twice**: `MAX_ARCHIVE_SHARE_FILES` on the count and `MAX_ARCHIVE_SHARE_BYTES` on the total, read from each response's `Content-Length` **before** the body is buffered — otherwise one 500MB video is in memory before the ceiling is noticed. That header is an early exit and never the ceiling itself: a chunked response, a proxy that drops it, or an engine that does not expose it cross-origin all report `0`, and a total summed from those alone stops guarding exactly when it matters, so the running total is held to `blob.size` and re-checked after each body lands. Either bound, or any failed fetch, falls back to the download route rather than opening a sheet holding part of the selection, which would read as a successful save of all of it. This needs R2's CORS to allow GET as well as PUT (§ 15.)
- **The bar carries 공유 beside 저장 everywhere but iOS, where the two collapse into one row.** They are separate controls because they reach separate places: the download to disk or to the library, the sheet to whatever the OS offers. On iOS both would take the identical route, so only one row is rendered — and it is **저장's**, relabelled `저장/공유` and keeping the download glyph, because the sheet is what the tap arrives through and not what it was for. It calls the `save` route, so the buffering wait and the re-tap dialog are worded for 저장 (`DESIGN.md § 7.10.`)
- **On iOS the selection itself is capped at `MAX_ARCHIVE_SHARE_FILES`, not `MAX_ARCHIVE_SELECTION`.** The sheet is the only route out there and cannot be handed more than twenty files, so the cap moves onto the gesture that fills the selection rather than the tap that spends it: the sweep stops at twenty and says why through the cap toast it already carries. **The cost is that 삭제 is capped with it** on that platform, since one selection feeds both. Everywhere else the selection keeps `MAX_ARCHIVE_SELECTION` and 삭제 keeps its reach — which is why **공유 is guarded at the tap**: it renders where a selection can be ten times what a sheet accepts, and letting it through would fall back to two hundred downloads, which is not what 공유 was asked for. It refuses **before** clearing the selection, or the refusal would also cost the user the sweep they just made
- **A share refused for a spent user activation asks for one more tap.** iOS spends the tap's transient activation on the first `await`, and buffering the selection is many. The files are already in hand, so the dialog offers `사진에 저장` and calls `share` straight out of that click with nothing awaited before it
- **All three shelves have an add control, and all three take a drop.** The flow behind them is one hook, `useShelfStaging`: a drop target, the tray it fills, the two editors, and the 대화에 보내기 beneath. It is shared rather than copied three times because § 9.2.'s refusal rules are an **invariant** — a drop is refused for the length of a selection and under an editor or the viewer — and the shelf whose copy drifted would be the one uploading into a grid its selection bar is about to delete from

  | Shelf  | Add control   | Opens                                    | `acceptsFiles` |
  | ------ | ------------- | ---------------------------------------- | -------------- |
  | 갤러리 | `갤러리 추가` | the album picker                         | `false`        |
  | 파일   | `파일 추가`   | `MediaPickerSheet` with its 파일 row     | `true`         |
  | 음성   | `녹음`        | `VoiceRecorderBar`, mounted from the tap | `true`         |
  - **음성's control records; it does not pick.** An audio file carries no waveform, so picking one there would file it on 파일 and leave the screen the user was looking at unchanged. Recording is the only way to put a row on that shelf, which is § 9.3.'s discriminator restated as an affordance. The bar is portalled through `ShellOverlay` for `AGENTS.md § 4.4.`'s reason, and the header tap is what **mounts** it — mounting is what opens the microphone, and § 9.3. requires that to happen on the gesture's own call stack
  - **A recording made here is staged, not sent.** That is the opposite of the composer, where 완료 _is_ the send (§ 9.3.), and it has to be: the two 갈래 have still to be chosen between. `MediaTray` grew a voice tile — a mic glyph over the clock, with no edit control, branched ahead of `filename` in `toDraftKind`'s order — and `useMediaSelection.addDraft` is what takes an already-decoded draft
  - **A drop is taken whatever it holds, and lands by kind.** 갤러리 is the exception and refuses a file (`사진과 동영상만 올릴 수 있어요`), because the grid draws thumbnails and a `.zip` has none. The other two take everything: a photo dropped on 파일 goes to 사진, and **anything dropped on 음성 goes to 파일**, since only a recording carries peaks. Refusing instead was considered and rejected — the user's question would be "why not", and a save that succeeded elsewhere is better news than a refusal
  - **What lands on another shelf says so, once per shelf**: `갤러리에 추가했어요` / `파일에 추가했어요`, from `LIBRARY_SHELF_LABELS`, which is the same table the chips are drawn from so a renamed chip cannot leave the toast naming a shelf that is not there. Without it the row is genuinely in 보관함 and genuinely absent from the screen the user dropped it on, which reads as a failed upload. `onAdded` is likewise called only for the rows this shelf lists — prepending a file into the 사진 grid would draw a card as a broken tile
  - **`useArchiveUpload` therefore takes drafts, not files.** Validation and decoding have already happened by the time it runs; reading the files again there would decode every one a second time and throw the edit away
  - **The post splits with `toBubbles`, the send path's own splitter.** A mixed drop is exactly how a batch of photos and files reaches this path, and the server refuses a mixed `media` set (§ 9.1.) — chunking by count alone took a 400 for it. `toBubbles` is generic over its item so the rule lives in one place rather than being restated here
  - **The staging sheet closes while either editor is up** (§ 13.4.'s rule, and the same trap): `MediaEditor` and `VideoTrimmer` portal into the app shell while the sheet portals into `body`, so no z-index inside the shell can lift them over it and the sheet has to get out of the way instead
  - **The tray is emptied with `takeAll`, never read from state.** It hands the previews over without revoking them, and the upload revokes each as it settles — left to the hook's unmount cleanup, the blob would be killed mid-upload

- **Direct upload from the library always posts to the conversation** (§ 18. #1.) — one control, 대화에 보내기, on every shelf. The option not to post went with `archive_added_at`: a row reaches a shelf by hanging off a live message and by nothing else, so an upload that is not posted lands nowhere at all. **A failed POST therefore loses the upload**, which is what the failure copy says (`대화에 보내지 못해 보관함에도 담기지 않았어요`) rather than promising a shelf it never reached. Files upload through § 13.4.'s bounded pool, which answers in **pick order**, not completion order; a post longer than `MAX_MEDIA_PER_MESSAGE` splits into consecutive bubbles in order (§ 18. #10.). **A tile therefore appears per bubble rather than per upload**: no `media.id` exists until the post that registers and attaches it (§ 9.), so the grid prepends what each post hands back and a failed post leaves nothing registered to roll back
- **Selection is unavailable while an upload is in flight**, and "in flight" runs until the last bubble's `postMessage` settles, not until the last byte lands — until then the grid is still gaining rows under the reader, and a selection made over a moving list is one the user did not make
- **A second pick while the first batch is running is ordinary**, so the progress counter is written **relatively, never absolutely**. It is claimed before decoding rather than after — reading forty photos' real dimensions takes seconds, and an absolute write would let the first batch finishing zero the counter under the second and flip the screen to its empty state mid-upload
- **Read authorization needs no library clause of its own.** The message join `canReadMedia` already runs is what admits every row on a shelf, since a live message is now the whole of membership (§ 18. #1.). The library-only branch beside it went with `archive_added_at`
- **The viewer carries 사진 사용하기** (§ 12.1.), here and in a chat bubble's viewer alike — three targets, 프로필 이미지로 / 프로필 배경으로 / 채팅방 배경으로. It is withheld on a **draft**, which has no stored object to read the pixels back from, and on a video **outside § 12.1.'s caps**; a clip inside them keeps the control and reaches the profile cover alone. **This line used to say the control was hidden on a video, and that was already false against § 12.1.** The sheet is mounted **outside** the viewer's own conditional, so dismissing the viewer over it cannot unmount it mid-write
- **The viewer also carries `대화에서 보기`, which opens the conversation at the message the slide was sent in** — under the slide track, never in the header row, which is already at its width (`DESIGN.md § 7.10.`). It keeps its box on a slide that has no message, exactly as the header's controls do: unmounted, it would resize the track and move the photo under a swiping finger. The tap navigates to `CHAT_MESSAGE_PARAM`, which the chat route shape-checks the way § 11.5.'s `?day=` is checked, and § 8.6.1.'s jump does the rest. It **flashes** on arrival (`DESIGN.md § 6.8.`) where a search jump does not — there is no query lit inside the bubble to say where the tap landed — so the room takes it as a prop of its own, consumed once at mount, rather than as a `jumpTarget` that closing a search would fall back to and re-fire. The viewer is dismissed **before** the navigation: it is a `ShellOverlay` and the shell outlives the route change. 사진 alone offers it; 파일's tap downloads and mounts no viewer (§ 7.10.1.)
- **The viewer carries 삭제 too, and it is not the chat viewer's 삭제.** A chat bubble's trash withdraws the whole message (§ 8.13.); this one destroys the object the slide is drawn from, the same `DELETE /api/archive` the selection bar spends, so the message keeps its place and draws a tombstone inside it. The two are one control with two reaches, so **the label is what distinguishes them** — `메시지 삭제` against `삭제` — and `MediaViewer` takes the label **with** the handler in a single `deletion` prop rather than as a second optional one beside it, so a caller cannot hand it a delete and leave the trash unlabelled. Deletion in 보관함 lived only in selection mode until here, which meant removing one photo cost entering a mode, picking it, and confirming, from a screen that was already showing exactly that photo
  - It is **withheld while an upload is in flight**, exactly as the 선택 control is disabled and the sweep withheld, and for the same reason
  - The confirmation names **one slide** rather than a count (`이 사진을 삭제할까요?`, `이 동영상을` on a video — the shelf carries both and 장 counts either, but a single named slide does not), through the modal the selection's confirmation already uses. The noun is carried on the pending deletion rather than being decided in the heading alone, because **the description and the failure toast take it too** — left hardcoded they read 사진 under a heading saying 동영상. `josa` picks every particle that follows it, since two nouns and two subjects reach those sentences (`AGENTS.md § 0.4.`)
  - **What the modal is about outlives its dismissal, and openness is a separate flag.** `DialogContent` stays mounted through its 200ms exit (`DESIGN.md § 7.4.`), so clearing the subject on close empties the heading and the modal fades out untitled
  - Confirming **keeps the viewer open on the next photo**, and that is the point of the control — deleting several in a row is one tap each, where closing would cost a re-open and a scroll back to where the reader was. The row leaves the track rather than the viewer leaving the screen
    - `cells` is the **snapshot** taken when the tile was tapped, so the screen has to be told; left alone the track keeps a slide whose object no longer resolves. `index` is not touched with it, and not clamped to the shorter track either: it is `MediaViewer`'s `initialIndex`, read once at mount, and where the reader goes next is § 8.1.'s id reconciliation
    - **The offset is re-asserted rather than left to scroll snapping.** Removing the snapped element leaves re-snapping to choose the closest position, which _is_ the slide that followed — but this is a `snap-mandatory` track and the platform is WebKit, where § 8.3. already documents scroll corrections being dropped. It is written out, so the reader lands on the next photo on every engine. It hangs off the track's **length** rather than off the index, or the same effect force-snaps a swipe that is still in flight
    - Where the reader lands is **named, never counted**: the viewer re-points itself at the id of the slide that followed the one removed, walked out of the track as its own last render drew it (§ 8.1.). Deleting the last one falls back to the photo before it, since the walk turns around at the end of the track. Deleting the only row closes the viewer, there being nothing left to show
    - The zoom drops with the slide it belonged to (§ 18. #6.). No `scroll` fires for a delete, and the re-point above leaves nothing in the offset to recognise one by, so the reset hangs off the flag that render set — under the same track-length gate as the correction, never off the swipe
- **The viewer names the sender, from the query that already resolves the message.** `users` is joined onto that second query rather than onto the listing — the warning below is about a join that can multiply rows, and this one is a foreign key to a primary key, so it adds a lookup per row and no rows at all. It is **not** gated on 사진: the branch and the second row shape would cost more than the column, and a file row has a sender too. The name is **resolved on every read through `resolveDisplayName`, never projected onto the row** (§ 8.7.) — a copy goes stale the moment either person renames, and § 8.7. makes a rename retroactive across the whole history on purpose. `entities/media` reaches that resolver through the FSD cross-import gate `entities/user/@x/media`, which exports the rule and not a projection. It is `null` on exactly the rows `messageId` is: a library-only upload was never sent, so there is nobody to name
- **The message a tile was sent in rides on `ArchiveMedia.messageId`, resolved by a second query and never a join.** `message_media` has no unique index on `media_id`, so a joined row set can outrun the `LIMIT` it was paged with — which both repeats a tile and spends a slot of the page on the repeat, with the keyset cursor none the wiser. `null` is ordinary rather than an error: a row uploaded straight into the library was never sent, and neither, any longer, is one whose message has been deleted

**Routing and the API**

- **Three routes, one tab.** `/archive/gallery` is 사진, `/archive/files` is 파일 and `/archive/voice` is 음성, with the bare `/archive` redirecting to the first. Nested under `ARCHIVE_ROUTE`, so `isUnderRoute`'s prefix rule keeps the tab bar's fill on 보관함 and `RouteTransition` resolves all three to the same tab index — which makes `directionBetween` return `none`, so **a segment switch does not slide** (`DESIGN.md § 4.7.1.`). That is the correct outcome and it needed no code: a segment is not a sideways move between tabs
- **The segments are three `Chip`s wrapping `Link`s, not an underlined segmented control** (`DESIGN.md § 7.10.`). Links, so the back button walks between them and each is announced as navigation; chips, for the reason `DESIGN.md § 7.3.` gives about two travelling indicators one strip apart. They are withheld in selection mode, as the header's own controls are — a segment tap there is a navigation that would drop the selection with nothing having said so
- **One endpoint, not two.** `GET /api/archive` takes `kind`, defaulting to `photo`; an unknown value is a **400** rather than a silent fallback, so a client asking for a shelf this build has never heard of is not handed photos instead. It also takes `around`, the same `media` id the route above reads off `?target=` — the initial window comes from the server render, so nothing on the client asks for one yet, but the wire contract is the function's contract and a second reader would otherwise reinvent it. `DELETE /api/archive` takes no `kind` at all and reaches every segment — `destroyArchiveMedia` narrows on `isInLibrary()` alone, so one 삭제 serves all three. It takes no `mode` either now that hiding is gone, and **`mode: "hide"` is refused rather than ignored**: a tab left open across the deploy still offers 숨기기, whose whole promise was that the bytes survive, and answering it with the destroy would spend that tap on the act the dialog had just ruled out
- **삭제 no longer deletes the bytes in the request.** It stamps `deleted_at` and returns; the reclaim purges the objects once `MEDIA_DELETE_GRACE` has passed, which is one grace for every media-scope delete and covers the read already in flight rather than the whole window a peer may replay a cached 302 inside (§ 9.). `deleteObjects` used to fail silently here and the § 12.4. sweep was the net that caught it — **now the unstamped row is**, and it is a better one: the work is named by a row the next pass finds rather than by a key only a bucket listing would

---

## 11. Calendar Tab ✅

### 11.1. D-day Header ✅

- The relationship start date comes from the **`RELATIONSHIP_START_DATE` env var** (ISO `YYYY-MM-DD`); it is **not stored in the database**
- Following the Korean convention, **the start date itself is day 1** (`countDays(start, today) + 1`)
- Computed in a Server Component and passed down, so a skewed client clock or timezone cannot give each user a different number. `todayKey` ships with it for the same reason — the grid's "today" cell must not be the device's opinion either
- Recomputed when the app comes back, in case it was left open across midnight. It is a refetch of `GET /api/calendar/summary`, **never a client-side recount**, or the bullet above would be undone the first time it ran
- **`focus` and `visibilitychange` both, and coalesced across them.** They land for **one** return of a desktop window raised from behind another, so they share a `SSE_SYNC_COALESCE_WINDOW` guard rather than each spending a session lookup plus a query. There is no dormancy check beside them any more: § 8.4.1. sleeps only while 채팅 is on screen, so the gate is never shut under this one

### 11.2. Derived Anniversaries ✅

- **Derived from `RELATIONSHIP_START_DATE`** — no database rows, no user input: every 100 days (100 / 200 / 300 …) and yearly anniversaries
- Marked in the month grid distinctly from ordinary events — a `primary` diamond (DESIGN § 7.9.), which is why `primary` is kept out of the event colour set (DESIGN § 4.1.7.)
- **Days remaining until the next anniversary** shows beneath the D-day header, hidden past a one-year horizon
- The derivation lives in **`shared/lib/date/calendar.ts`, not `entities/event`** — the grid derives markers for every month it is swiped to, and a value import from that barrel would drag `server-only` into the browser bundle. Yearly anniversaries are built by **calendar-field arithmetic, never by adding 365 days**, so a leap year cannot walk them a day early

### 11.3. Month View ✅

Event markers in day cells (three dots then a `+N` count), swipe left/right to change month with the header's chevrons as the pointer equivalent, tapping a day shows that day's list in the agenda under the grid, timezone pinned to `Asia/Seoul` on both server and client.

- **The day's list is a section under the grid, not a `BottomSheet`.** A day is therefore _always_ selected — today on arrival, or the `?day=` a § 11.5. notice tapped through with — and the month and the day are legible at the same time. The sheet cost a dismiss and a tap to reach tomorrow, hid the grid behind a scrim to answer a question about the grid, and made selecting a day an interruption. The document already scrolls (`AGENTS.md § 4.4.`), so the column simply grows and nothing had to become `fixed` to pay for it
- The swipe requires horizontal **intent** (|Δx| > |Δy|), or a diagonal drag would change month while the user was scrolling the shell
- **A drag over the grid never selects a day.** A finger that scrolls the shell or swipes the month from inside a cell still releases into a `click` on it, so the grid's own `onClickCapture` swallows the tap once the pointer has moved past `GESTURE_SLOP`
- The grid is **always six rows** — a height that changed with the month would reflow the screen under the thumb on every swipe
- **And nothing above it may change height either**, which is the same rule read upwards (DESIGN § 7.9.). 다가오는 일정 sits between the D-day band and the grid, where it used to be the last section on the screen precisely because it varied between nothing and three rows and moved the month as events passed, on every add or delete, and once more on arrival. It went back above the grid only once it stopped varying: it always draws (§ 11.5.), its list is pinned at the height it had when 더 보기 was first pressed, and 캘린더 has no `loading.tsx` left to insert a card the fallback had not drawn (DESIGN § 7.8.). The agenda is the only variable thing under the grid, and it is the answer to the tap
- The month's fetch asks for the **grid's** range, not the month's, so the adjacent-month days in the first and last rows carry their markers too
- **Selecting a day moves the month with it.** The upcoming card reaches a year ahead and the grid's edge rows reach into neighbouring months, while the agenda can only see the range currently loaded — without it it reads `이 날은 일정이 없어요` for a day the card just said had an event
- Month fetches are **request-id guarded** — two quick swipes leave two in flight, and the slower one landing last would leave the grid showing the wrong month's dots with nothing left to re-trigger the fetch
- Opening the event form **closes the action sheet first.** Both are modal `Drawer`s portalled to `body` and neither is declared nested, so leaving both up means two focus traps — and dismissing the top one can leave the one underneath inert
- **"Jump to today" is a `오늘` chip beside the month label**, not a control above the grid — the D-day band and the upcoming card both navigate already. It **selects** today rather than only moving the month: selecting no longer opens anything, so the reason to withhold it is gone and a chip meaning "take me back" that left the agenda on another day would only half arrive. It is withheld only once the tap would move nothing — today's month with today already selected
- The month header is a **`1fr auto 1fr` grid, not `justify-between`** — the centre column holds `DESIGN.md § 7.9.`'s centered label against the middle of the row however wide the sides get. Under `justify-between` the chip appearing would push the label off centre and shift it back on every return to this month

### 11.4. Event CRUD ✅

- Create / edit / delete — title, description, start and end time, all-day flag, color, recurrence (`none` | `yearly`). Create and edit are **one form**; the fields are identical
- **The description shows on the list row, clamped to three lines** (`EventMemo` — the agenda's rows, § 11.5.'s 다가오는 일정 and § 11.5.1.'s chat panel alike) — it was readable only from inside the edit form before. `whitespace-pre-line`, because the breaks are the writer's own
- **A tap on an event row opens `EventDetailDialog`, not a menu about it.** It is the whole event — when, 구분, 추가한 사람, and the memo in full, which is what the three-line clamp leads to. 수정 and 삭제 are behind its own `Settings2` control, which opens the § 11.3. action sheet exactly as the row itself used to. **The dialog owns that entire flow**, so 캘린더 and § 11.5.1.'s chat panel share one component rather than a copy each
- **Its three surfaces are shown one at a time**, never stacked: all three are modal and portalled to `body` and none is declared nested, so two up at once is two focus traps (§ 11.3.). The occurrence on show is also held past its own closing — read from the prop it is already null through Radix's exit animation, and the dialog visibly collapses to its header before it fades
- 저장 closes the dialog rather than reopening on what was written: its occurrence is the screen's, and the refetch has not landed
- **The memo wraps anywhere it has to.** A pasted URL is one unbreakable word and the app wraps on spaces (`DESIGN.md § 4.2.3.`), so it overflowed a dialog that is a `grid` over `overflow-y-auto` — which gives the box a horizontal scroll, and scrolling it slides the title under the corner controls its padding was reserving
- **채팅's attach sheet offers 일정 too**, below 사진/영상 · 파일 · 음성: it attaches nothing and opens this form on today, and 저장 refreshes § 11.5.1.'s panel. The form belongs to the screen, as `EventDetailDialog` already does — `ChatRoom` only reports that the row was tapped
- Both users can **view and edit every event**; there are no permission tiers. No write path is scoped to `created_by`, so a missing row is the only 404. Authorship shows via `events.created_by` — the avatar, in the agenda's row
- The form works in **wall-clock fields** (`<input type="date">` / `type="time"`, which iOS renders as its own wheel picker) and converts once, at submit. `TIME_ZONE_OFFSET` is a literal `+09:00` and is sound **only** because Korea observes no daylight saving
- **An edit seeds from the occurrence's anchor, never its projection.** A `yearly` event edited while looking at a later year would otherwise be saved into that year and stop recurring from where it started
- `PATCH` sends only what changed. The ordering check runs against the stored row when a patch moves one end alone, since the body schema can only compare two ends it was given both of
- **The patch schema is built on an undefaulted shape.** `zod`'s `.partial()` makes a key optional but does **not** strip its `.default()`, so a schema shared with the create path would resolve `PATCH {"title":…}` into a body carrying every default and the UPDATE would wipe description, colour, recurrence and scope. Defaults belong to `eventBodySchema` alone
- **The form's date and time fields are clearable**, so `toInstant` is nullable rather than throwing. `isDraftSubmittable` runs during render, where a `RangeError` out of `toISOString` is a blank screen rather than a disabled button
- **A dead submit button says why.** `toDraftIssue` names the two states a filled-in draft can be stuck in — an unparsable date or time, and an end before its start. A missing title is deliberately silent: the field is on screen, empty, under its own placeholder, and a form that scolds you for not having typed yet is worse than one that waits
- **A new event starts at the next half hour, not at a fixed 12:00**, and runs an hour. The clock is read in the `useState` initializer — the sheet already remounts per opening (`key`), so that is a mount-time constant, where the same read in the component body would move the default under whoever is typing

### 11.5. Sharing Features ✅

Include only what pays off for exactly two users. Invitations, RSVP, permissions, and external calendar sync are **not adopted** — with two mutually trusting users they are pure complexity.

- **Event `scope`** — `shared` (ours) or `mine` (my personal schedule), defaulting to `shared`. `mine` events are still **visible** to the other user, **title included** (§ 18. #9) — this is a distinction, not a privacy control; the point is to communicate "I'm busy that day". They are told apart in the month grid by marker **shape** (filled dot for `shared`, ring dot for `mine`), not colour, which is already spent on the event's own hue. In the agenda, where there is room for a word, a `mine` row says `개인 일정` outright — a 4px ring is a 2px hole and nobody reads it. The word is `개인` and never `내`: both users see the row, so `내` would be wrong for whichever of them did not write it
- **A `type = 'system'` message posts to Chat when an event changes** — e.g. `지희님이 8월 10일 '영화 보기' 일정을 추가했어요`
  - **The event title is a line of its own** — `지희님이 8월 25일 일정을 추가했어요` then `"영화 보기"` under it. The break is a `\n` inside the composed string, so `SystemNotice` renders the pill under `whitespace-pre-wrap` and § 8.3.'s `toNoticeHeight` measures it under the same mode; measured under `normal` the break collapses and every notice row is priced a line short. The pill's own gutters are `px-md` for the same sentence-shaped reason, and that number is subtracted in both places too
  - **Do not bake the name into the stored text.** Store `sender_id`, `system_action`, and the `event_title` / `event_starts_at` snapshot, then **compose the sentence at render time from `users.nickname`** (§ 8.7.). The event snapshot is deliberate, not a violation: a delete notice must still say which event it was, and its `events` row is gone. Only the _user_ name must never be copied
  - Post on create, time change, and delete. **Do not post** when only the title or description changed. § 16.3.'s reminder is the fourth producer of these rows, and the only one nobody performed
  - This rides the existing SSE pipeline — the `AFTER INSERT` trigger on `messages` (§ 6.) publishes it, and the event routes publish nothing themselves
  - It also raises the § 16.1. **push** banner through the same `notifyMessageRecipients` the chat send uses. `features/notify-chat` exists so there is literally one fan-out: § 16.1. permits exactly one alerting channel, and a second copy written beside the event routes is how that stops being true. The banner's body is the notice **without** its actor, since the banner's title is already the sender
  - Rendered as a centered pill with no bubble (same treatment as the date divider — DESIGN § 6.4., § 6.5.); tapping navigates to the event's **day** (`?day=`). The day, not the event id, is deliberately the whole destination: a delete notice outlives its `events` row, so half of these notices have no id to carry
  - The `day` parameter is **shape- and value-checked** before it reaches a date helper (`isDayKey`). `?day=` or `?day=2026-13-45` otherwise arrives as an Invalid Date, and `Intl.DateTimeFormat.format` throws on one — a 500 on a URL anybody can type
- **Every list of events is sorted by `compareOccurrences`, and its last arm is `compareId`** — day, then all-day ahead of timed, then the instant, then whichever event was added first. A snowflake orders by the moment it was minted (§ 6.), and without that arm the tie falls to whatever order the rows came back in, so two events at one instant can swap places between renders of the same list
- **다가오는 일정** directly under the D-day header and above the month grid, opening on `MAX_UPCOMING_EVENTS` events and paging by `UPCOMING_EVENTS_PAGE_SIZE` behind 더 보기 until a page comes back short — § 11.5.1.'s mechanics exactly, since the two are one list drawn on two screens. It is titled on screen, because the § 11.3. agenda is a second list of event rows on the same screen and two unlabelled stacks read as one
  - **It always draws**, an empty state standing where it used to render nothing. Above the grid a section that disappears is a section that moves the month (DESIGN § 7.9.), and that empty state is a row inside the same card rather than § 7.6.'s box, which at `2xl` padding would be taller than the rows it replaces
  - **A row's tap selects its day and scrolls the grid to the top of the screen.** The grid is the answer to the row, and it now sits below a section tall enough to push it off the fold. It does **not** open § 11.4.'s `EventDetailDialog` — that is § 11.5.1.'s behaviour, where the panel floats over a conversation with no calendar to hand the reader back to
  - **The page is a database `LIMIT`, not a slice.** `listUpcomingOccurrences` used to read a year of rows and every `yearly` row unfiltered, then sort and slice in memory — so every 더 보기 re-read the same year. It is one statement now: a `yearly` row's next single occurrence is a scalar expression, which is what makes a `LIMIT` possible where the § 11.3. month grid's range cannot have one (its range can span a year boundary, where one row appears twice)
  - **The projection rule is therefore written twice, in SQL and in `projectYearly`, and they are held together by agreeing on `setUTCFullYear`'s own arithmetic** — months from January of the target year, then days, so a 2월 29일 anchor overflows to 3월 1일 rather than being clamped to 2월 28일 the way `+ interval 'n years'` would. Three shifts are considered, not one: the year before today's is what keeps an event straddling New Year reading `진행 중` on 1월 1일
  - **There is no index behind the `ORDER BY`, and there cannot be one.** The sort key is the projected start, which is a function of today as well as of the row — not immutable, so no expression index may be built on it, and `events_starts_at_idx` cannot serve an ordering a `yearly` row does not sit in. The sequential scan is the decision: this is two people's calendar
- **"Upcoming" is measured against the clock, not the calendar day.** A timed event is retired at its end instant, an all-day one at the end of its day, and one already under way reads `진행 중` rather than announcing the date it started — a breakfast that ended at 09:00 used to hold a slot until midnight, and a trip that began last week used to head the card with a date in the past
- **A dot on the Calendar tab-bar icon when there is an event today** (a single dot, not a count). Resolved by the shell's server render, not over the stream — `user_changed` carries `users` rows, and this changes at most once a day
- **Empty state** for a day with no events (DESIGN § 7.6.)

### 11.5.1. 다가오는 일정 is reachable from 채팅 ✅

The § 11.5. card is also what 채팅's header opens, because the conversation is where the two of them actually are — asking "when is that again" cost a tab change and a scroll to the foot of the calendar.

- **A second `icon-button-floating` in the chat header, `CalendarClock`, with 검색 to its right.** It toggles a panel `absolute` under the header, over the conversation — never a row above it, since `ChatScreen` is sized to the visual viewport (`DESIGN.md § 3.4.`) and a laid-out panel would take its height out of the room and step the messages down on every open. **Both header icons, and the 뒤로 button, are `lg:hidden`**: from `lg` the same 다가오는 일정 (always expanded, no toggle needed) and 검색 live in chat's side panel instead (`DESIGN.md § 7.20.`), beside the partner block
- Rows are **soonest first**, the same order and the same `formatUpcomingWhen` line the calendar's card uses, each carrying its § 11.4. memo. A row opens § 11.4.'s `EventDetailDialog` in place — never a crossing to 캘린더, since the question the panel answers is "when is that again" and a tab change to read a memo loses the conversation. Unlike the card this one **does** draw an empty state: it answers a tap, and a control that opens nothing has not said whether it worked
- **A row starting inside the day counts down instead of naming a date.** `오늘 오후 7:00` gives way to `3시간 뒤`, to `곧 시작` in its last hour, and to `진행 중` once it has begun — in `primary`, the colour the header's bloom and dot already say the same fact in (`DESIGN.md § 7.12.`). Hours and never minutes: a label that moves every minute re-renders § 8.3.'s virtualizer for a number nobody watches tick. **§ 11.5.'s calendar card keeps the plain date**, the countdown being the panel's half of a header that blooms, and that header exists only in 채팅
- **The card pages by 더 보기, `MAX_UPCOMING_EVENTS` at a time, with no ceiling** — where § 11.5.'s calendar list pages by `UPCOMING_EVENTS_PAGE_SIZE`, its rows being on the document rather than in a floating card. The list ends when a page comes back short. Every request asks the summary for **one past** what is drawn and slices the extra off — a page returning exactly what it asked for cannot say whether anything is behind it. `GET /api/calendar/summary?upcoming=` takes that count and falls back to the card's own rather than 400ing, since the parameter only widens a response the caller was getting anyway
- **The desktop side panel is the same list on its own terms: it opens on `SIDE_PANEL_UPCOMING_PAGE_SIZE` rows and scrolls, asking for the next page as its end comes into view (`loadsOnScroll`) instead of showing 더 보기.** The server render carries the panel's page on every width, and the card slices its `MAX_UPCOMING_EVENTS` off the same rows (`cardOccurrences`) — one summary, two cuts, so the panel is full on the first paint without a render path keyed on the panel cookie. A 더 보기 on the card that outruns what the panel holds widens the shared fetch to meet it
- **Both screens page identically, and every mechanic in the four bullets below is shared.** § 11.5.'s calendar list carries them too; only the page size differs
- **The first 더 보기 pins the list at the height it already had and starts it scrolling, and it does not go back.** The desktop side panel opts out (`pinsHeight={false}`): its column already bounds and scrolls the list, and a pinned height would leave the rest of the column empty. The **list** is what is measured, not the card: pressing 더 보기 must not resize the list under the finger, and the button leaving on the last page takes its own row off the panel. **§ 11.5.'s calendar list is the one place this differs** — there the list absorbs that row instead, growing by its height, because the month grid is directly below and a card that shrank would pull the screen up under the press. 더 보기 sits outside the scroller so it stays at the foot of the panel — inside, the one control that grows the list would be the one the reader has to scroll to reach. A 닫기 sits in the panel's own top-right beside the heading, since the header button that opened it is behind the panel by then. **Esc closes it too**, through that same 닫기 path so the dismissal is recorded either way — in the capture phase and stopped there, since the room's own shortcuts see no overlay marker on this panel and would otherwise peel the composer's stack underneath it
- **The button is held on screen while its page is in flight.** `limit` steps on the press and the page lands a round trip later, so the summary in hand is briefly one page short — read plainly, the button blinks out under the finger and comes back
- **A landed page scrolls its own first row to the top of the list**, so what the reader asked for is what the list is showing. No inset is held back for the row before it: a page arrives at the **end** of the list, so this is already the maximum scroll and anything past it is clamped away to the same pixel. It is held until the page has **landed**: the stepped limit reveals the one row already in hand first, so a scroll computed a render early runs against a list a page short, clamps to its bottom, and leaves the reader where they started
- **Arriving in 채팅 with something imminent opens the panel already** — for a 우리 일정 or the reader's own 개인 일정 only. The other person's 개인 일정 is on the list to be read (§ 11.5.) but is not theirs to be interrupted by. The decision is made from the arrival's own snapshot and never re-made, so a reader already in the room never has it thrown over their conversation. Closing it records **which occurrences were imminent** in `localStorage`, not a flag: one of them passing leaves it dismissed, and a new one entering the window re-opens it. The record is the current set rather than a union — anything dropped from it could not have re-opened the panel anyway
- **The panel is capped against the screen** — `--chat-screen-height` less the header inset it starts under and the `--bottom-inset` the composer holds up — and its list scrolls unconditionally. The 더 보기 pinning cannot answer this: the list is a scroller only once it has been pinned, so an unpinned one grows a row per event and runs off the bottom of a box sized to the visual viewport
- The panel is **laid out at all times and hidden by opacity**, `inert` while closed — unmounted it has no closing frame to animate (§ 7.3.'s trade)
- **The summary comes from the server render**, in parallel with the message page, and refreshes on the `focus` / `visibilitychange` pair § 11.1. already coalesces — the button's own appearance depends on it, so a summary fetched after hydration lights it a beat late
- **The button blooms and carries a dot while something of the reader's own starts within 24 hours** — the same 우리 일정-or-my-own set the arrival prompt reads, the bloom being the header saying the panel has something in it for them (`DESIGN.md § 7.12.`), and the halo swells once as it arrives rather than on a loop. The crossing is scheduled as a single `setTimeout` — at the instant the nearest event enters that window, and after that at each hour due to come off a row's countdown — never a polling interval — a tick on this screen re-renders the § 8.3. virtualizer. Only a crossing inside the next day is armed, since `upcoming` reaches a year ahead and a timeout past 2³¹ ms wraps and fires immediately
- The panel is withheld while § 8.6.'s search is open, for the reason the composer's stack is: the control that opens it is not on screen

### 11.6. Notifications ✅

Landed as § 16.1. Calendar changes reach the user as § 11.5. chat system messages on the same pipeline; **there is no calendar-specific notification and no scheduler.**

### 11.7. Korean Public Holidays ✅

관공서의 공휴일 are drawn as 빨간 날 — the numeral in `semantic-error`, the same colour the weekday header has always given Sunday (DESIGN § 7.9.) — and named in the § 11.3. agenda above the day's milestones.

- **The source is 공공데이터포털 특일 정보 (한국천문연구원's `getRestDeInfo`), read on the server; `holiday-table.ts` is the fallback under it.** `loadHolidays` in `shared/holiday` merges the API's answer for the mutable years over the committed table and hands the whole thing to the screen. There is **no workflow and no pull request** — the monthly Actions job that used to propose a regenerated table is deleted, because a PR nobody intends to review is a refresh that never lands
- **The table still crosses to the client whole, and that is what keeps the swipe synchronous.** The grid resolves markers for every month it is swiped to, so a holiday source that is `async` or reaches the network at swipe time breaks it — `findHoliday` is an O(1) day-key lookup over an object already in the browser, and the whole table is under 1 KB gzipped. The request is on the server, once per render, behind the same `<Suspense>` as the rest of § 11.'s runtime data
- **It cannot be computed, which is why it is a table.** 설날 · 추석 · 부처님오신날 are lunisolar; 대체공휴일 follows from wherever those land; and an 임시공휴일 is a cabinet decree with no rule behind it at all (2024-10-01 국군의 날, 2025-01-27). A rule engine would render the _old_ statute after every amendment — and there were two in 2026: **노동절 (5월 1일) and 제헌절 (7월 17일) became 공휴일**, both subject to 대체공휴일. Third-party holiday packages had not ingested either at the time of writing
- **특일 정보 rather than any of the alternatives, and the reason is attribution.** Google Calendar, Calendarific, Abstract, HolidayAPI and Nager.Date were re-checked against it. It is the legal source of record, it carried 2026 노동절 three days after the cabinet decided it, and it is the only one that says **which** holiday a 대체공휴일 replaces (`"dateName": "대체공휴일(삼일절)"`) — the field the cells' `광복절 대체공휴일` is built from. Nager.Date still omits 2024-10-01, 2025-01-27, the 2025 대선 and the 2024 총선
- **Cached with `fetch(url, { next: { revalidate } })` for roughly a month, and never with `use cache`.** Reversing that breaks the feature rather than restyling it: the default `use cache` handler is in-memory and per-deployment-instance, so a month-long lifetime would almost never be hit on serverless, where the `fetch` Data Cache persists across instances and across deploys. A month is the cadence the data actually moves at — 월력요항 is published once a year, and an 임시공휴일 is decreed with weeks of notice
- **A failure is not cached, so a circuit breaker stands in for one.** Neither a rejected nor an aborted `fetch` is written to the Data Cache and React's `cache` is per-request, so nothing remembers a failure on its own — and the case this section was written for, a **lapsed 활용기간**, leaves the variable set and the key looking fine, so every single render would spend the whole request budget rediscovering the same rejection and blocking on the deadline to do it. A year that fails is therefore not asked about again for **an hour**: long enough that a dead gateway costs one blocked render an hour instead of one per reader, short enough that an operator who renews the key sees it work in the session they renewed it in.
