It used to wait for the element's `playing` event and mount a frame later, and the two landed a variable few tens of ms apart, differently per device and output. Until the reveal the box holds a `display: none` slot — never `opacity: 0`, which WebKit animates and Blink does not — and a skeleton where nothing was on screen yet. `ChatRoom` still hands the one arriving row `awaitsArrivalSound` (`useArrivalEmoticonSound`), skipped unless the reader is **at the live edge**, and the bubble that mounts takes the playback over and settles it at once; the room's timer is now only for a row that never mounts. **The hold is capped at `EMOTICON_PLAYBACK_HOLD`** and the cap is the point — past it the sound plays alone and the network `<img>` is drawn, so a message is never left invisible. A solo mini takes the same contract through `InlineEmoticon`; an inline run and the composer draft never pass it
- **The shared player declares `navigator.audioSession.type = "transient"`** before it is minted. Left at `auto` the page resolves to the `playback` category, and `playback` is what put the app on the iPhone lock screen and Control Center as Now Playing — with transport controls for a two-second emoticon sound — and took the session away from whatever the user was listening to. `transient` is the category for a notification ping: no Now Playing entry, and other audio ducks and recovers instead of stopping — and it follows the silent switch, as Web Audio does under every category short of `playback`, so moving off the element changed nothing there. Set before `new AudioContext()`, since WebKit fixes the category from the page's first audio. Safari 16.4+ and implemented nowhere else, so it is read off a widened `Navigator` and skipped where it is absent
- **`navigator.audioSession.type` is a property of the _page_, not of an element.** So "the emoticon ping is `transient` and voice is `playback`" is not an arrangement that can be expressed — there is one category and everything the app plays shares it. § 9.3.'s voice messages are minutes rather than the two seconds this rule was written for, and `playback` (a lock-screen Now Playing entry and background playback) was weighed for them and **rejected**: the session being page-wide would put every emoticon ping on the lock screen — the exact regression this bullet removed — and `playback` stops the user's music where `transient` only ducks it. The price paid is that voice playback has no lock-screen transport and no guaranteed background playback
- **The resting category is written down in exactly one place**, `RESTING_TYPE` in `shared/lib/audio/session.ts`, and it is not exported. Anything that moves the session for the length of an operation — § 9.3.'s capture is the only one, to `play-and-record` — comes back through `declareRestingAudioSession()` and never by naming a category of its own, or revisiting this decision silently leaves one caller behind
- **Voice uses an `Audio` element**, beside the context. A voice note is minutes long and is seeked and paused, which an `AudioBufferSourceNode` cannot be — and `playSound` stops whatever it was playing, so sharing would cut a voice message off mid-sentence when an emoticon arrived. The **session** is still shared, so the `transient` decision above governs both. That element needs no `useSoundUnlock` twin: playback only ever starts from a tap on the play control, which is already the gesture iOS wants to see
- A tap also **restarts the animation**, through the same synced play. A GIF or animated WebP has no seek API and re-assigning the same `src` is ignored by the cache, so a silent restart — a viewport re-entry — remounts a keyed `<img>` with the token folded into the URL (`toReplaySrc`) for iOS Safari's per-URL animation state, or replays the synced frame without the sound once one is up. **A solo mini's tap does the same and plays its optional sound** — `InlineEmoticon` takes an `isTappable` flag that `MessageRow` sets only on the solo, box-drawn case; an inline run and the composer draft pass no flag and stay untappable, since neither is the large bubble-less case this reaches for
- **The packs are warmed from the room, not from the panel** (`useEmoticonPreload`) — the panel does not exist until the tap this exists to make cheap. Three halves now rather than two: the pack list, the items of the **one tab that will actually open**, and that tab's images. Without the first the panel opened empty for a round trip; without the rest it then filled in cell by cell, each cell costing the § 13.3. route's 302 as well as the object. **The warm resolves the opening tab exactly as the panel does** — the same stored key through the same fallback to 최근 사용 when the remembered pack has since been deleted or hidden — or it heats a tab that never opens and leaves the one that does cold
- **A warmed cell's skeleton is held back `PLACEHOLDER_DELAY` (`hasDeferredSkeleton`), and only a warmed one's.** Everything above closes the ordinary case, where the asset is warm and the element reports `complete` on mount; what is left is the cold one — a tab reached before the warm caught up. A placeholder that is drawn and then faded out over an image that arrived 40ms later reads as the panel being slower than it is, so the skeleton waits, and an asset that lands inside the wait is never preceded by one. Off everywhere else on purpose, § 13.8.'s results row included — nothing warms a search, so those cells really are being fetched, and for those DESIGN.md § 7.8.'s plate is the reserved box that delaying leaves an empty hole in. The delay is declared on the placeholder wrapper and never on `Skeleton`, which already sets `animate-pulse`: two `animation` shorthands at equal specificity survive tailwind-merge together and the pulse wins, which quietly turned the whole thing into "the pulse starts late"
- **The eager window widens in three steps — twenty cells collapsed, forty open, and the whole tab once its warm has landed — and the widest step is exactly `MAX_WARMED_PER_TAB`.** `lazy` on a warm cell is the whole skeleton: a lazy image begins loading after layout and its intersection check, so `img.complete` is false when `PreloadImage` reads it back on mount however cached the bytes are, and the placeholder is committed and then faded out over an image that was ready. That read-back is the only thing `eager` is for here. **Below the head it used to fail twice over**, which is what the three steps close: `hasDeferredSkeleton`'s wait is a CSS `animation-delay` armed when the placeholder mounts, and nothing here is virtualised — every cell mounts with the panel, before the first open — so a cell scrolled to seconds later has spent its wait off screen and meets the reader with an opaque plate already standing. **The widest step costs nothing**: those URLs are the ones the warm has fetched and decoded, so § 13.3.'s cache answers the redirect and the decoded copy is the one the warm is holding, and tying it to the warm's cap leaves no band that is eager without being warm. **The narrower two are not that width, and must not be.** `eager` bypasses `WARM_CONCURRENCY` and `fetchPriority`, so a tab whose warm has not run would hand the § 13.3. route a hundred presigns at once — collapsed, against a room still drawing its first screenful, which is why the panel pays the narrowest of the three until it is actually opened. **Counted in cells and never in rows**: 미니 draws six columns to 이모티콘's four, so a row count silently gives half again as many to the menu that needs it least — a mini's replay already stands its previous frame in where a still would show a plate. A list nothing warms stays wholly `lazy` (§ 13.8.'s results)
- **The warm walks outward from the open tab, not from the room** (`useOutwardTabWarm`). The room's warm covers the tab that opens and stops there, so every swipe landed on a grid of skeletons — the neighbour's items had not been asked for and its images had never been seen. Warming them at room entry instead would put two more list requests and two packs of images on every visit, including the visits where nobody reaches for an emoticon; from the open, the cost falls only on the person who is already looking at the panel, and it has the length of their first read to finish in. 최근 사용 as a neighbour costs nothing extra — the panel already holds its items — and 검색 has nothing to warm until a word is typed. **Both 최근 사용 tabs resolve through that**, not `RECENTS_TAB` alone: the walk carries one menu's kind and the items it is handed are already that kind's, so 미니's tab read as a non-pack and warmed an empty list — the tab a 미니 open lands on by default
- **Each pair starts only once the pair before it has finished**, stepping out to `MAX_WARM_DISTANCE` (fifteen) and no further — wider than the strip is today, so for the library as it stands this is "every tab, nearest first", about 8.5MB of stills once per browser per § 13.3. cache window. `warmEmoticonImages` resolves when its pool drains, which is what the walk waits on. Started together the tabs would interleave at the pool's width and the one a single swipe away would finish behind a tab four away — the near ones are what a gesture reaches first, so they are warmed first. It is a bound all the same and must stay one: § 13.5. puts no ceiling on packs, so what it refuses is a library an order of magnitude larger being fetched entirely in the background of one panel open. Past the retention cap of three tabs a warmed tab is evicted from the map but its bytes stay in the browser's own cache, so the far end of the walk is a disk read rather than a round trip — which the deferred skeleton above covers. Landing somewhere re-centres the walk, and what was already warmed is skipped on the URL
- **A reveal never moves an axis the scroller has no room on.** `revealWithin` adds `REVEAL_MARGIN` to its clipping test, so an item merely sitting inside its scroller's padding still produced a non-zero term on that axis — and `scrollBy` moves an `overflow: hidden` box as readily as a scrolling one. On the tab strip that was a few pixels of _vertical_ travel every time a tab was pulled in from off the end, which is what read on iOS as the panel wobbling under a sweep. The comment claiming the vertical term "resolves to 0 here" was the assumption, never the arithmetic
- **The strip's own pack thumbnails are warmed and decoded before the walk**, and the head of the row is `eager`. They are `still-image` assets like any other and were the one row nothing warmed, so the strip drew its plates while the reader was already looking at it — where a cold _tab_ is at least somewhere they have not gone yet. Uncapped, since the row is one asset per pack and the list is its own bound; `EAGER_TAB_COUNT` covers what fits on screen and the rest stay `lazy`, which is what still holds a two-hundred pack library to what has been scrolled to
- **A 미니 tab's grid cells and its warm both take `animated-image`, never `still-image`.** A mini is only ever shown moving — a still cell in that grid would draw a frozen frame of a picture the reader has never seen paused, and only there. `warmEmoticonImages`'s `kind` parameter is what the slot follows: `useEmoticonPreload`'s opening tab and `useOutwardTabWarm`'s whole walk (every tab in one call is one menu's own tabs, so this is one value per call rather than a per-item lookup) resolve the open menu's kind and warm that slot alone — a mini tab's still is never fetched at all. An item the server falls back to its still for (no animated file on that item) simply draws and warms the still that request answers with, unremarked — the slot asked for is still always `animated-image`. The management grid at `/settings/minis` (§ 13.5.) draws the same slot for the same reason
- **A mini cell fakes a loop an `<img>` cannot be told to hold.** A GIF's Netscape extension or a WebP/APNG's own loop count is not always infinite — an asset encoded to stop after a few passes froze on its last frame in a grid meant to read as always-playing. There is no runtime API to override that count, so `useViewportReplay`'s `loopIntervalMs` fakes forever by remounting the `<img>` on a fixed timer, its `src` also cycled through `toReplaySrc` for iOS Safari's sake (`MINI_ANIMATION_LOOP_INTERVAL`, `shared/lib`) for as long as the cell is actually in view — the same remount a tap or a re-entry into view already does elsewhere, just on a clock instead of an event. Off entirely for an 이모티콘 cell's still, where there is nothing to loop
- **A warmed tab within two of the open one is decoded, not merely fetched** (`MAX_DECODED_DISTANCE`). This is the difference a reader could see: a tab already visited never shows a skeleton again, while a tab that was only warmed still did. A detached `Image` puts the bytes in the cache and nothing more — the picture has never been in a render tree, so nothing decoded it, and WebKit keeps no decoded copy for an element it never painted. `decode()` closes that, and is rationed because it is the expensive half: a decoded still is its pixels, roughly 700KB at `EMOTICON_MAX_EDGE` against 17KB of PNG, so five tabs is ~90MB and the whole fifteen-tab walk an order of magnitude more — inside an iOS tab already carrying the conversation's media. Past two, a tab is bytes in the cache, which is a disk read rather than a round trip
- **A warmed `Image` is held, not dropped once it loads.** Released, it takes the resource out of the memory cache with it, and the cell that eventually draws it pays a disk read and a decode — both asynchronous, so `PreloadImage` commits its placeholder first and the panel opens on a grid of skeletons over images the network never re-fetched. Held, the element is `complete` when the cell mounts and `syncCachedStatus` reveals it on the first commit. Capped at the decoded working set — five tabs, `MAX_DECODED_DISTANCE` either way, since eviction drops the element and the decode paid for with it. Eviction is least-recently-warmed: a URL already held is touched rather than re-fetched, and insertion order alone evicted a tab still in the set — landing on P3 from P2, the arriving P4 threw out exactly the P2 a swipe back was about to draw
- **The panel is in the document before the first open**, mounted on the same idle frame the warm runs on rather than on the tap (`hasMountedEmoticonPanel`, still one-way). The tap used to pay for mounting a grid of forty cells on the frame it also animated the open on. The two share `runWhenIdle` and the same ceiling deliberately: mounted first, the panel would build its cells against a cache that has not been filled yet, which is the whole of what the warm is for. What the early mount buys is the mount: past the twenty head cells above, a collapsed panel's cells are `lazy` and intersect nothing, and the warm is still what puts the bytes in front of them
- **The warm is deferred to `requestIdleCallback`, never started on mount.** § 8.3. is the reason: the list is one more round trip against a room already making several, and a pack of images is decode work landing while the first screenful of bubbles is still being measured. iOS Safari only shipped the API in 17, so a `setTimeout` of the same delay stands in — a second late is not the failure mode worth writing a polyfill for
- **The list has one descriptor, `toEmoticonPacksQuery`**, and the key is never restated at a call site: two spellings are two caches, and the preload would warm a list the panel never reads. The `staleTime` belongs to the **preload's call alone** — the descriptor's own `0` is what makes the panel re-ask on mount, which is when a § 13.5. edit has to land, while the preload would otherwise re-ask on every return to the room. That window is longer than it was, because the list is summaries: an item edit no longer changes it, only § 13.5.'s own screens do. **That `0` is declared on the descriptor and not left off it**, which it was at first: `getQueryClient` defaults every query in the app to a minute, so an omitted `staleTime` is a minute rather than none. **What the panel itself reads is no longer that `0`.** Its mount stopped being the tap — § 13.6. mounts it on an idle frame — so a `0` honoured there is a packs request on every visit to a room nobody opened the panel in, which is the request `PRELOAD_STALE_TIME` exists to withhold. The picker holds the same five-minute window and moves § 13.5.'s guarantee onto the **open's rising edge**, where the tap used to put it
- Images are warmed through § 13.4.'s pool, and **every task resolves**: an asset the route refuses cannot stop the queue on the rest of the pack. This withdrew "a user who never opens the panel never fetches the packs" — the cost is one list request, one tab's items and one tab's images per visit, against an affordance one tap from the composer
- **The per-tab cap is a guard now rather than a ceiling, and its old justification is gone.** It read "§ 13.5. puts no ceiling on packs or on items per pack, so an uncapped flat-map is a request per authored emoticon on every visit" — true of a warm that flat-mapped the library, and false of one that warms a single tab — a hand-authored pack is a few dozen items and never reaches the number. What it bounds today is one unusually large pack, which is a guard rather than the ceiling it used to be. Past it a cell is loaded by being scrolled to, which is what every cell did before the warm existed
- **The pool width is not what keeps the conversation ahead of the warm.** The app is one HTTP/2 connection, so a narrow pool bounds only how many requests are outstanding — `fetchPriority: "low"` on each warmed image is the mechanism, and an out-of-DOM `new Image()` is dispatched at default priority without it

---

### 13.10. Emoticon favorites ✅

- **A user may mark any choosable emoticon item as a favorite**, persisted to `user_emoticon_favorites`. The table carries a snowflake `id` (PK), `user_id` and `item_id`, with a `UNIQUE(user_id, item_id)` guard so a double-tap is idempotent, and `INDEX(user_id, id DESC)` so the most-recently-favorited item sorts first.
- **`item_id` carries `ON DELETE CASCADE`** so a hard-deleted item row cleans up its favorites automatically. In practice items are soft-deleted (`deleted_at`) and the cascade is a safety net for the edge case.
- **`isChoosable` filters soft-deleted items** out of every favorites query — a deleted item is no longer offered in the picker or the 즐겨찾기 section regardless of whether a favorite row still exists for it.
- **The picker's 최근 사용 tab on the 이모티콘 menu now holds two sections**: an upper `최근 사용` section showing `recentsVisibleRows × columns` items with a `더보기 ∨` button that adds three rows per tap (up to `MAX_RECENTS` = 48 items stored), and a lower `즐겨찾기` section showing all favorited items. The 미니 menu keeps its standalone 최근 사용 grid and plain `Clock` tab icon without a favorites section.
- **The tab icon on the 이모티콘 menu changed** from a plain clock to `RecentsAndFavoritesIcon` (split clock and star), while the 미니 menu keeps `Clock`.
- **The preview card carries a ★ button** (top-left) that toggles the current emoticon's favorite status with an optimistic update, alongside the existing ✕ dismiss button (top-right).
- **Favorites are fetched via `GET /api/emoticons/favorites`** and mutated with `POST` (add) / `DELETE /api/emoticons/favorites/{id}` (remove by `item_id`). The client hook `useEmoticonFavorites` manages this with React Query optimistic updates, a 5-minute `staleTime`, and rollback on error.
- **`EMOTICON_FAVORITES_URL` is declared in `shared/config/emoticon.ts`** alongside the other emoticon URL constants and follows § 13.7.1.'s `EMOTICON_API_BASE` switch. The route exists in both apps — `favorites` and `favorites/{id}` — and both copies MUST be changed together.

---

### 13.7. jandh-emoticons is a zone inside this origin

- **`next.config.ts` rewrites `/emoticons/:path*` to jandh-emoticons**, which sets `basePath: "/emoticons"` of its own. It stays a separate repository on a separate **Netlify** project with a separate deploy cycle — this app is the Vercel one, and the two are not the same platform. What changed is only that a browser reaches it at `jandh.jeheecheon.com/emoticons` rather than at a subdomain. Its `REQUIREMENTS.md § 2.1.` is the authority on the arrangement
- **iOS is the whole reason.** An installed Home Screen web app has its own cookie store, and a link outside the manifest's scope opens in Safari View Controller, which does not carry it. From this app's installed PWA a subdomain meant signing in again on every 임포트 — into a session this app could not then see. § 5.2.'s shared cookie is not enough on its own, because the store the cookie lives in is what iOS splits
- **The rewrite target is `NEXT_PUBLIC_EMOTICONS_ORIGIN`**, the bare origin with no `/emoticons`. A trailing slash is stripped by `next.config.ts` rather than trusted to the operator: one that survived built `//emoticons/:path*`, and because the zone answers that path like any other, the misconfiguration ran in production without a single failed request to report it. Unset it falls back to `http://localhost:3001`, which is what a local `pnpm dev` in both repos wants — and therefore what a production deployment must never inherit
- **`NEXT_PUBLIC_`, and read a second time in `shared/config/emoticon.ts`.** The calls that skip the rewrite and go to that origin directly — § 13.8.1.'s suggester always, and § 13.7.1.'s emoticon API when its switch is on — are made from the browser, which cannot see a server-only variable. It is deliberately **one** variable rather than a public twin: two names holding the same origin is a pair that drifts, and the drift would be invisible — the rewrite would still work while the direct call went somewhere else. § 13.7.1. is a switch rather than an address for exactly that reason. The strip is duplicated instead, because `next.config.ts` is loaded before the path aliases exist and cannot import the other copy
- **A rewrite is the default for everything a browser _navigates_ to, and only a `fetch` may leave the origin.** The zone is reached by document navigation and by its own relative fetches, both of which need the same-origin illusion; a `fetch` this app writes by hand can afford to go direct, and only because it can be given `credentials: "include"` and answered with CORS. § 13.7.1. is what that permission has since been spent on, and it is the whole of it — nothing that is not a `fetch` may be "optimised" the same way
- **One prefix covers pages, route handlers and `_next` assets alike**, since `basePath` puts all three under it. Narrowing the rewrite to page paths would leave that app's assets to 404 against this one's `_next`
- **`proxy.ts`'s matcher names `emoticons/_next` and `emoticons/api` as their own exclusions.** The existing ones are anchored at the first segment and do not reach a copy one level down, so without these every asset the zone serves would run through this app's session gate and redirect to `/login` while signed out. `/emoticons` **pages** are deliberately left in: answering the redirect here saves a round trip to the other origin only to be sent back
- **§ 13.5.'s URL로 추가 crosses over with `window.location`, never `<Link>` or `router.push`.** Same origin, but a route this app's tree does not contain — the client router would ask for an RSC payload that does not exist. That makes it a document navigation, which is also why the 뒤로 control on the far screen reads history instead of pushing a fixed route
- **The import screen takes the kind as its route rather than a query** — `/emoticons/import` and `/emoticons/import/mini`. What this app links to is then an address that survives a restore and that a bfcache return can tell apart, and there is no path on which the screen fails to state a kind and `emoticon_packs.type`'s default (§ 6.) files a mini among the ordinary packs
- **Coming back is a bfcache restore, and the pack list has to be told about it.** The browser serves the document back frozen: no render replays and nothing refetches, so 이모티콘 관리 draws the list it was rendered with, one import out of date. `useBfcacheRestore` recognises `pageshow` with `persisted` and the screen answers with `router.refresh()`. **That is not enough on its own**: the list is seeded into state once and every edit since has been its own, so it re-seeds when the `packs` prop identity changes, or the refresh lands on a screen that never re-reads it
  - **§ 8.4.1. needs nothing here any more.** Leaving blurs the window and sleeps on the way out, and the return used to find 절전 모드 over a screen the user never felt they had left — a `sessionStorage` note written before the navigation was what told that return apart from an app-switch. A departure raises no overlay now and every return dismisses the sleep itself, so the note, both its functions and the whole distinction went
- **A signed-out visitor to `/emoticons/*` lands on this app's `/login`**, not that app's, and that is correct rather than incidental: one `sessions` table stands behind both (§ 5.2.), so the session this login produces is the one the zone needs. The destination is not carried through, so the visitor lands on `HOME_ROUTE` afterwards rather than back at the import screen — an acceptable loss on a path reached only by bookmark or expiry
- **That app writes prefixed OAuth cookies** (`jandh_emoticons_oauth_state`, `…_verifier`) where this one's are bare. Both are host-only, which used to be the whole separation while the two sat on different hosts; under one origin the shared names were one cookie serving two flows
- **§ 16.'s worker controls the zone too, and wants no change for it.** A worker controls its whole origin, so `/emoticons/*` reaches it — but `IMMUTABLE_PREFIXES` is `/_next/static/`, which the zone's `/emoticons/_next/static/` does not start with, so its assets go to the network uncached rather than into a cache keyed against the wrong build. Navigations are network-first, so a zone document is fetched normally and only a real network failure reaches the `/offline` fallback. Do not "fix" either by adding the prefix: a second app's hashed assets in this app's versioned cache is a stale bundle that `activate` has no reason to evict

---

#### 13.7.1. The emoticon API may be pointed at either origin, and the default is this one ✅

**`NEXT_PUBLIC_EMOTICON_API_REMOTE` decides whether the browser's emoticon calls go to this app's routes or to jandh-emoticons' copies of them.** The motive is cost rather than architecture: the emoticon work is a self-contained slice of traffic, and moving it puts that slice on the other deployment's budget instead of this one's.

- **A switch, not a second address.** The origin stays `NEXT_PUBLIC_EMOTICONS_ORIGIN` (§ 13.7.) — a public twin holding the same value is the pair that drifts, and the drift would be invisible, since the rewrite would go on working while the direct call went somewhere else
- **Off is not a code path.** With the switch off the base is the empty string, so every constant is the relative path it was before the switch existed, character for character. There is no "local mode" to keep working; there is the string that always shipped
- **Default off, which is the opposite polarity to `IS_SSE_IDLE_SLEEP_ENABLED`.** That one defaults on so a cost control cannot be lost by forgetting a variable; this one defaults off so a _reachable_ API cannot be. A new environment that names nothing gets the routes this app deploys with itself
- **Fifteen calls move and one does not.** `packs`, `packs/{id}`, `packs/{id}/items`, `items`, `items/{id}`, `prefs` and `upload-url` are `fetch` calls and follow the switch. It was eleven across six paths before § 13.9.1. moved search to the server: `items` is new, and `packs/{id}/items` gained a `GET`. **`items/{id}/asset` never moves**, and it is written out separately rather than built from the items constant so that it cannot follow by accident: it is reached by an `<img>`, an `<audio>` and a bare `new Image()`, which are element loads where the cookie rides on same-site and SOP never enters — so jandh-emoticons answers it without CORS on purpose (that repo's § 8.8.). § 13.6.'s preload is the second reason: it warms a whole pack over this app's one HTTP/2 connection, and a second origin is a second connection. **It is exempt from the switch and not from the mirror**: that repository serves the same route to its own management screens, so the two copies still change together
- **`credentials: "include"` is keyed on the URL being absolute, never applied to every caller.** Credentials mode belongs to the whole fetch rather than to its first hop, so setting it blanket makes a same-origin request that redirects off-origin into a _credentialed_ CORS request at the destination — and § 9.'s media download 302s into R2, whose S3-compatible CORS cannot answer `Access-Control-Allow-Credentials`. `collectShareFiles` reads that redirect, so the blanket version takes § 10.'s 공유 down with an opaque network error for a change that was about emoticons. Every path this app writes is relative, which is what makes "absolute" a sound signal for "deliberately off-origin"
- **`credentials: "include"` is set once, in `request`, for every caller rather than for the cross-origin ones.** It is identical to the `same-origin` default while the request is same-origin, so it costs nothing off and cannot be forgotten on. The failure it removes is the quiet one — a fetcher reaching the far origin with no session is answered 401, not an error naming what was left out
- **The switch does not reach `EMOTICON_KEYWORDS_URL`.** That route has no copy on this side to fall back to (§ 13.8.1. keeps `GEMINI_API_KEY` off this deployment), so it is absolute unconditionally
- **This app's own routes stay, and they are a fallback rather than dead code.** Deleting them would orphan `entities/emoticon`'s whole write layer — `registerEmoticon`, `updateEmoticonItem`, `setEmoticonPackOrder` and the rest have no other caller — and leave the repository that owns the schema (jandh-emoticons' § 3.1.) with nothing in it that writes one
- **Changing an emoticon route means changing it in _both_ repositories, in the same piece of work.** All **eight** exist twice — here under `app/api/emoticons/`, and in jandh-emoticons under the same paths: `packs`, `packs/{id}`, `packs/{id}/items`, `items`, `items/{id}`, `prefs`, `upload-url` and `items/{id}/asset`. For the seven the switch governs, the browser reaches whichever copy it names, so a validation rule, a status code, a query parameter or a response shape edited on one side alone is not a change that half works: it works until a variable moves, and then silently stops. `items/{id}/asset` is mirrored too and fails differently — each app always serves its own, so a one-sided edit there leaves the other app's screens on the old behaviour indefinitely. Each of the eight carries a `WARN` saying so, and jandh-emoticons' § 8.8. states the same rule from its end. That repository's § 8.8. also records what a one-sided edit already cost once — `?enabled=1` against `?items=1`, and a missing empty-patch guard — found only when the copies became reachable
- **§ 9.'s reservation changed three of the eight, and one of them is the quietest kind of drift there is.** `upload-url`'s `POST` reserves before it presigns; `items/{id}` and `packs/{id}` purge through the reclaim's own helper. `upload-url`'s **`DELETE` keeps its URL, its body and its 204 while its meaning moves** — from "delete keys no item references" to "release this caller's live reservations" — so nothing about the client protocol says the two copies have diverged, and a one-sided edit here fails only once the switch moves and only as objects quietly surviving or quietly vanishing. **The old test was also wrong**, which is the second reason it may not be left in place on either side: it defined "registered" as slotted into an item, so it deleted objects that did have live `media` rows
- **`GET /api/emoticons/packs` now reads four query parameters, and both copies took them in the same change**: `enabled=1` (§ 13.5.'s 사용중 tab), and `q`, `cursor` and `limit`, whose **presence** — not their content — switches the answer from `{ packs }` to `{ packs, nextCursor }`. `EMOTICON_PACK_PAGE_SIZE` and `MAX_EMOTICON_PACK_PAGE_SIZE` are declared on both sides beside the constants below, because a page size that drifts hands back different pages depending on where the switch points, against a cursor the browser wrote from the other one
- **The routes are no longer the only thing mirrored, and the rest of it fails more quietly.** A route copy cannot answer without the constants it is written against, so `RELEVANCE`'s three numbers, `toKeywordRelevance`, `splitKeywordQuery`, `MAX_KEYWORD_QUERY_LENGTH`, `EMOTICON_SEARCH_CANDIDATE_LIMIT`, `EMOTICON_SEARCH_PAGE_SIZE`, `MAX_EMOTICON_KEYWORD_LIST` and `MAX_EMOTICON_ID_LOOKUP` are now declared in **both** `src/shared/config/emoticon.ts` files, beside `normalizeKeywords` and `MIN_KEYWORD_LENGTH`, which already were. **They MUST move together, and this is the same hazard as the routes with the alarm taken off**: a route edited on one side is at least a shape somebody can diff, where `6/4/3` against `6/4/2` is two deployments that both answer, both rank, and rank differently depending on which one the switch happens to be pointing at. jandh-emoticons' `CLAUDE.md § 0.2.` names that file a mirror from its end
- **The switch takes both variables, and an unset `NEXT_PUBLIC_EMOTICONS_ORIGIN` overrides a set switch.** That origin's own default is a dev server (§ 13.7.), so honouring the switch without one would point every emoticon URL at `localhost:3001` — mixed content a production browser blocks outright, with the feature down and nothing naming why. Ignoring the switch instead leaves this app's own routes answering, which is what the default-off polarity is for
- **Turning it on has two prerequisites in the other repository**, and skipping either takes the feature down rather than degrading it: that deployment must be **live with its CORS routes** — a response missing those headers is withheld from the browser entirely, so this reads as every emoticon call failing at once — and it must set `CORS_ALLOWED_ORIGIN` to this app's origin
- **Flipping it is a redeploy, not an environment edit.** `NEXT_PUBLIC_` is inlined at build time (§ 8.4.1. carries the same note for its own switch), so the variable is read when this app is built and never again
- **Every write costs a preflight once the switch is on.** `application/json` is not CORS-safelisted, so `POST`, `PATCH`, `PUT` and `DELETE` each pay an `OPTIONS` per browser cache window — `Access-Control-Max-Age` asks for a day and browsers cap it far lower. **The reads send no header that would trip it**, which matters more than it used to: § 13.6.'s panel is four `GET`s now — the pack list, the open tab's items, 최근 사용's ids and § 13.9.1.'s search — and every one of them is on a hot path where a doubled round trip would be visible. Keeping them free of a request body is what keeps them at one

- [x] 13.7.1. The emoticon API follows a switch; this app's routes remain the default and the fallback.

---

### 13.8. Search keywords, and finding an emoticon from the composer ✅

**Typing `고민` in the composer underlines it; tapping it opens the picker's search tab already holding that word.** KakaoTalk's own behaviour, and the reason `emoticon_items.keywords` exists.

**This reverses § 13.4.'s "an item carries no title."** That decision was right when nothing read the string back; this is the reader. What returned is not a title — it is a **list** (`text[]`, `MAX_EMOTICON_KEYWORDS` of `MAX_EMOTICON_KEYWORD_LENGTH`), because one picture answers to `우와`, `놀람` and `헐` alike and a single field would have made the user choose.

- **Keywords are authored, never inherited.** An item arrives with none whatever produced it, and gets them one of two ways: typed into § 13.4.'s sheet, or suggested by § 13.8.1. and edited there. Nothing derives them from a file name, a pack name or the art
- **The underline is client-side and costs no request per keystroke, but the list behind it is no longer the pack library.** § 13.6. used to ship every pack to the browser, so `keywords` rode along on `Emoticon` and the composer matched against the panel's own data; the panel holds summaries now, and the composer reads a distinct keyword list of its own — `GET /api/emoticons/items?keywords=1`, `SELECT DISTINCT keyword` off § 13.9.1.'s index. It is strings and nothing else, because the underline asks only whether a typed word has _something_ behind it; **which** emoticons is § 13.9.'s search, one tap and one request later. The list is cached for an hour, since a keyword is only written from § 13.4.'s sheet and § 13.8.1.'s suggester — authoring screens, which are routes of their own and not ones a composer is on
- **That request is made by § 13.6.'s preload, with one input-time escape hatch.** `MessageComposer` mounts with the room, so an enabled query would fire `?keywords=1` on every single room entry — precisely the moment the pack list was moved off, and for the same reason: a room already has several requests in flight and the underline is worth none of them. The composer normally reads the descriptor with `enabled: false`, which subscribes it to the cache without fetching, and `useEmoticonPreload` warms the same descriptor from the `requestIdleCallback` that warms the packs. If the user types before that idle warm lands, the composer explicitly refetches the descriptor once, so typo recovery is not silently unavailable on the first keystroke
- **At a much larger library even that list stops being shippable**, and this is the known ceiling rather than a defect. A few thousand distinct keywords is 30–80KB and rides fine; the ten-thousand-pack case § 13.9.1. is built for would have to debounce the underline into a server call as well. That lands at the same time as § 13.6.'s tab-strip redesign and is deliberately not done now
- **`MAX_EMOTICON_KEYWORD_LIST` is where that ceiling is made visible instead of silent.** The cost of this list is entirely payload — the index answers the `DISTINCT` in a few milliseconds at any size the app will reach — so outgrowing it does not fail, it just makes every room entry heavier: measured at 36,741 distinct words and 490KB of raw text over a synthetic five hundred packs, which is past a megabyte as JSON. Truncated at 10,000 instead, the query is bounded and the symptom is legible: the list is sorted, so what the cut drops is the tail of the collation and those words simply stop underlining. Hand-authored packs at the two to three hundred this ships with do not come near it
- **The per-item cap survives the move, on a different argument.** It used to be justified as the composer's per-keystroke working set; the composer no longer holds the column at all. What it bounds now is the row count of the search index and the width of § 13.9.'s ranking pass over a candidate page
- **Searching — and the underline that leads to it — looks across every pack, hidden ones included** (§ 13.9.). Only the tabs and 최근 사용 are this user's enabled list; a word is a property of the item, and a pack taken out of the tab strip is not an answer withdrawn
- **A keyword and a query match by containment, in both directions** (`matchesKeywordQuery`), and each direction pays for a different case. `keyword.includes(query)` is why an item tagged `고민되는군` is reachable by typing `고민` — nobody types a whole sentence to find a picture of it. `query.includes(keyword)` is Korean grammar: a particle attaches to the word, so `우와가` and `고민되는군요` are the keyword plus an affix that is not part of it. Dropping either half loses half the feature
- **These were anchored to the front, and an anchored match cannot answer a Korean interjection.** `와` is what somebody types; `우와` is what the item is tagged. Neither is a prefix of the other, so the single most obvious query in the language reached nothing at all — and `와아` against `와!` fails anchored matching in both directions at once. KakaoTalk answers `와` because its index expands a query semantically; this app has no expansion and never will, so the match itself has to be the thing that gives. **Unanchoring is safe here because Korean writes a syllable per code point**, which is what makes it a different trade than it would be in English: `와` matches the syllable 와 and nothing else, where a one-letter Latin substring would match half the library. Measured against the real column before the change — every query but two returned an identical item set, `와` went from **0 items to 2**, and the broadest query tested reached 14% of the tagged items. **`MIN_KEYWORD_LENGTH` is more load-bearing under containment, not less**: a stored `아` would answer every drafted word carrying that syllable anywhere rather than only the ones starting with it
- **A one-character query searches; a one-character keyword is refused. These are not the same rule, and one constant used to state both.** `MIN_KEYWORD_QUERY_LENGTH` gated three things — the composer's underline, the picker's search field, and `normalizeKeywords` — under a single justification that only ever fitted the first. Typing `왈` has to reach the item tagged `왈?`, which is KakaoTalk's own behaviour and the whole point of the feature; refusing it left a keyword the app had itself suggested unreachable by the one string anybody would type. **A short query is safe precisely because a short keyword is not allowed**: with every stored word at two characters or more, a one-character query can only ever reach the `keyword.includes(query)` direction, so it widens the result set without inventing matches. The floor that remains is `MIN_KEYWORD_LENGTH`, on the stored word, and it is the particle direction that earns it — a keyword of `아` answers to every drafted word carrying that syllable. The picker's search field now guards the **empty** string rather than a length, since every keyword contains `""`; its empty state reads `단어를 입력해 보세요`
- **The underline is a mirror layer over the `<textarea>`, not anything inside it.** A textarea renders no elements, so there is nowhere to hang a mark or a tap target. The layer shares the field's box through one `FIELD_BOX` constant and wraps by the same rules — any padding or font reaching one and not the other slides the mark off the word. Every glyph in it is transparent and only the decoration paints, because the real text belongs to the textarea underneath; `pointer-events` is `none` on the layer and `auto` on the one span, or it would swallow every tap meant to place a caret
- **The last matching word wins**, not the first or the longest — the offer follows the caret, and keyed on the first match a word typed later would underline something at the far left of the field
- **The toggle recovers one unambiguous typo locally, then holds the last answered keyword through further input that finds nothing.** The browser already has the distinct keyword list for the underline, so before a previous result exists `어딩`, `어딩?` and `이어딩?` may resolve to the sole keyword `어디` at one **NFD jamo** edit, ignoring trailing Unicode punctuation, and start that search; `이어딩?` also tries its one-character suffix `어딩`, without ever caching `이` as a query. Two possible corrections offer nothing. This is why `딩` to `디` is a removable final `ㅇ` rather than a whole-syllable replacement. Once it has answered, a Hangul IME can turn `어디` into `어딩`, and punctuation can follow it, while the preview and its tap keep searching `어디` and the composer keeps the literal draft. A deletion or a non-typo replacement releases that fallback and asks again from the current draft, so a new word never inherits an unrelated result
- **Opening with `⌃E` preserves that suggestion when the reader later selects 검색.** `⌃E` itself restores the remembered tab, but the room holds the composer's current query and gives it to the picker only as an empty search field's seed; a word already typed into the picker is never overwritten. This makes `⌃E` → 검색 and `⌃1` agree on `어디` without changing what either shortcut opens
- **검색 is the first of § 13.6.'s three menus**, holding a field in the second region and its results as a **4-column grid** in the third. It is the only menu reachable without the panel already being open, since a tap in the composer opens it. It is drawn at 이모티콘's four columns rather than 미니's six whatever kind the results hold, because a set of mixed kinds has no one width to be right at
- **검색 is the one exemption from § 13.6.'s "never on screen with the keyboard"**, because the menu has its own field, so the keyboard is there at the panel's own request. **The keyboard covers the sheet rather than shrinking it** (`DESIGN.md § 3.4.`): for as long as the exemption holds, the room publishes `data-keyboard-overlaid` and the chat screen keeps `--viewport-resting-height` instead of following the visual viewport down, so the keys slide over the sheet's lower rows and the field — at the top of a sheet the focus has just expanded to the header — stays clear of them. The composer stays in the stack and usable; a tap on its field closes the panel and drops the exemption at once, since that keyboard is the composer's now and will never go down on its own. It used to be a single sideways row of cells, on the argument that one row is short enough to share the screen; the grid replaced the row, so the constraint had to move into the height. **It is deliberately not a sum of its parts any more**: a 4-column cell is a share of the shell's width rather than a constant, so the hand-summed value was wrong by a row on every width but the one it was measured on, and read as correct only because the grid clipped. **The exemption is keyed on the menu, never on that field's focus** — a blur and the keyboard's retraction are separate frames, and between them an unexempted panel closes underneath the user
- **Arriving on the tab does not focus the field.** It used to — the panel opening onto 검색 and a switch to it from another menu were both read as a request to type — and the sheet expanding on that focus (§ 13.6.) is what overturned it: a menu tap that raised the keyboard and opened the sheet to the header was two things the reader had not asked for. The field is the reader's own tap, or § 8.14.'s ⌃1 through `menuRequest`; a query seeded with results still puts focus on its first cell, silently, for the arrow keys. Where the field is focused by script it is from a layout effect, which React flushes inside the commit the key renders — a passive effect lands outside the user activation WebKit requires and the field comes up with no keyboard (the same note `message-search-bar.tsx` carries)
- **The exemption outlives the tab by the length of the keyboard's retraction.** Leaving 검색 unmounts the field that was holding the keyboard, and the viewport only reports it down some 250ms later; released with the tab, those frames are an unexempted panel that collapses to nothing and reopens by itself once the keys finish sliding. A latch in the room holds the exemption until the keyboard is actually gone, so the panel stays up, already at its full height, and the keys simply slide off it.
- **§ 13.6.'s tab swipe no longer reaches 검색, and the results' axis carve-out went with it.** Both existed because the results were one sideways row sharing the swipe's own axis: the row kept first claim on any drag that started inside it while it still had somewhere to scroll, and gave the axis back once it did not. The results scroll vertically now and 검색 is a menu rather than a tab, so there is no neighbouring tab for a swipe to reach and nothing left for the two gestures to contend over
- **The tap fills the field and moves the tab during render, not in an effect.** An effect lands a frame later, so the panel opens on the remembered tab, paints a grid of the wrong pack, and only then swaps — which reads as the wrong panel flashing up. The forced tab is component state rather than the stored one, because writing `localStorage` during a render is a side effect and comparing tokens is not. **The search tab is never remembered**: it is a place the user passes through with a word in hand
- **A word that was the whole draft leaves with the emoticon it found.** `고민` alone was a search term, and sending it beside the picture would put the search in the conversation; `오늘 고민 많다` keeps its sentence and sends as § 13.6.'s second message. The composer owns the draft (§ 8.6.), so the room bumps a token and the composer decides — a token rather than a boolean, since two sends of the same query are two instructions
- **The send spends the word and leaves the search standing.** The draft is cleared and the panel stays on 검색, holding the same results — sending one of a row of related pictures is the reason to have searched at all, and dropping back to the remembered pack means finding the word again for every emoticon after the first. This is the same place 따라하기 (§ 13.9.) already left the panel, so the two routes onto the tab now behave alike. **What the send withdraws is the word, not the request**, which is why the two are held apart in the room: the request is what forces the tab, and the word is the one-shot the consume above matches the draft against. Read off the request, it stayed armed across the send, and a draft that merely read `고민` again — typed as an actual message this time — was swallowed by the next emoticon staged from the still-open results
- **Nothing is offered until an item has words.** Keywords are authored in § 13.4.'s sheet, as chips committed by the `+` beside the field, by Enter or by a comma. The commit is guarded on `isComposing`: a Korean field fires `keydown` for the Enter that _closes_ the IME's composition, so without the guard the first Enter after typing `우와` commits `우`
- **The `+` is what a thumb has, and it is the only one of the three a Hangul keyboard can reach without leaving itself.** The comma sits behind the symbol layer, so committing a chip means 특수문자 → `,` → 한글 for every word in a list; and the return key beside it commits only on its **second** press, because the first is the one the guard above deliberately swallows. Neither is a defect to fix — the comma is also what splits a pasted `우와, 놀람` into two chips, and the guard is what keeps `우와` from being stored as `우` — so the button is added rather than either being taken away. **The placeholder is an instruction, `대화에서 찾을 단어를 넣어 보세요`, where it used to be the example `우와, 놀람`.** The example existed to teach the separator, and the separator is the trip through the symbol layer the `+` was added to end — so it was teaching the gesture the field had just stopped asking for. Its second word was a caption besides, which is the register § 13.8.1. spends a prompt section refusing. **It is bounded at roughly 13 Korean characters**: the narrowest shell leaves this field some 200px of text once the sheet's padding and the 44px `+` are taken out, `body-md` is 15px, and a placeholder truncates rather than wrapping. The comma survives as something a paste arrives in, not as a gesture the field asks for. It carries `keepsFocus` and cancels its own `pointerdown`, exactly as the composer's send disc does (DESIGN.md § 6.6.): keywords are typed in a run, and a tap that blurred the field would drop the keyboard between every one of them. It is an `IconButton` rather than a labelled 추가 for a second reason as well as the sheet's own 추가 button — `Button`'s haptic overlay is `keepsScroll`, and a `keepsScroll` overlay whose `disabled` settles inside its own click is the one combination DESIGN.md § 7.15.3. measured going silent
- **A keyword is at least `MIN_KEYWORD_LENGTH` long and at most `MAX_EMOTICON_KEYWORD_LENGTH`, and `normalizeKeywords` is no longer the only place either holds.** § 13.9.1.'s index applies **both** bounds, in the trigger and in the backfill, and there they are load-bearing in a second way: the reverse probe enumerates a term's substrings between exactly those two lengths, so a stored keyword outside them would be unreachable by the direction that matches most widely. The ceiling is there for the floor's own reason — a row older than `normalizeKeywords` can be longer than twenty characters as readily as it can be one character short. Those bounds are also **retroactive** where `normalizeKeywords` was not — it guards only the two write paths in `write-emoticon-item.ts`, so rows older than the rule can carry a one-character keyword, and those are dropped from the index rather than indexed unreachably. Measured on the development database before the backfill: 37 such keywords across 34 items (`밥`, `잠`, `헉`), and **no item lost its last searchable word** — every one of the 34 carries a longer keyword as well. The reason for the floor itself is unchanged and is the `query.includes(keyword)` direction alone: `아` would underline every drafted word carrying that syllable. It is **not** that one character is unsearchable — the bullet above allows exactly that — so the authoring field says `두 글자부터 넣을 수 있어요` and its copy must not claim otherwise. **A word the floor refuses stays in the box**, where every other outcome of `commit` clears it: `normalizeKeywords` drops it, so clearing would delete the text the warning is asking the user to finish. **The comma path has to honour that refusal too** — it commits the text before the comma and then sets the box to the text after it, so a `commit` that returned in order to leave `아` standing was undone by the next statement, which cleared the box and took the warning with it. `commit` reports whether it consumed the value, and a refusal with nothing after the comma puts the word back. Only that case: a paste carrying a word after the comma has a caret to place, and one refused segment among several is the drop `normalizeKeywords` performs everywhere else. **The line is a permanent `aria-live="polite"` region, never a `role` toggled on with its text.** A region is registered when its node is inserted, so one appearing in the same commit as the change it describes is commonly announced by nothing — the refusal it exists for. `polite` and not `alert` because every Hangul word passes through a one-character draft as the IME settles its first syllable, and an assertive region cuts the reader off once per word in a run of keywords. **The `+` stays enabled under that line and refuses**, because disabling it is what causes the deletion rather than what prevents it — a disabled control receives no `pointerdown` and `IconButton` drops the haptic overlay with it, so both of the focus guards above go, the tap lands on the wrapper, and `onBlur` commits the half-typed word away. **The cap is applied and then the result is trimmed again**, since a multi-word keyword cut at exactly a space would otherwise be stored with a trailing one — and matching never trims, so that keyword could only ever be reached by typing the space too
- **A keywords-only write does not touch `updated_at`.** That column is `Emoticon.version` and rides on every asset URL (§ 13.4.), so bumping it to record some text invalidates that item's cached redirect and its presigned GET — and § 13.8.1. writes one per item, which would re-download a whole pack, chat history included
- **Closing the panel releases the search, and so does leaving the tab.** The picker never unmounts (`hasMountedEmoticonPanel` is one-way), so a search left standing keeps it forced onto 검색: the toggle would reopen onto a finished search, and the keyboard exemption above would stay latched on for the rest of the session. **Both routes onto the tab have to be released, not just the composer's.** Withdrawing the request covers the tap in the composer; a tab tap or a swipe from 최근 사용 forces the tab with no request behind it, so the panel closing is what releases that one — keyed on the request alone it was never released at all, and one swipe was enough to latch the exemption on. **Leaving the tab withdraws the request too**, because the word is only consumed by a send (above) when the emoticon beside it is one the search produced — a request surviving a walk to another pack swallows a typed word that merely equals it. **A send is deliberately not one of these routes**: it ends the word, not the search
- **An absent `keywords` on `PATCH` keeps what the item has; `[]` removes them all** — the same tri-state `audioKey` carries, and for the same reason: an image-only edit must not wipe them by omission
- **The pack grid shows each item's words under its cell**, one truncated `caption` line, `키워드 없음` in `meta-soft` where there are none — and nothing at all on a mini's grid, which has no words to carry (§ 13.). Without it the column is invisible on the one screen that exists to edit it: which items § 13.8.1.'s button is about to fill was legible only from a count in its own label, and whether a suggestion was any good meant opening § 13.4.'s sheet item by item

#### 13.8.1. Gemini suggests them, and it is called from exactly one place ✅

- **The suggester is jandh-emoticons' route, and this app calls it at that app's own origin.** `EMOTICON_KEYWORDS_URL` is absolute **unconditionally**, where § 13.7.1.'s four constants are absolute only when their switch is on — this route has no copy on this side to fall back to, since `GEMINI_API_KEY` is deliberately not an environment variable here. It skips § 13.7.'s rewrite for a reason of its own besides: the hop that rewrite adds is a Vercel proxy in front of a Netlify function, and the batches below are sequential, so it is paid once per batch for nothing this call needs. The `/emoticons` segment survives anyway, because it is that app's `basePath` rather than an artifact of the rewrite
  - **`credentials: "include"` is required and is the whole reason the session arrives**, and since § 13.7.1. it is `request`'s own rather than a line at this call site — every emoticon call is now a candidate for leaving the origin, so naming it per caller was one more thing to forget. `fetch` defaults to `same-origin` and would send no cookie at all. What makes the cookie acceptable once asked for is § 5.2.'s `.jeheecheon.com` domain plus **same-site**: the two hosts share a registrable domain, so `SameSite=Lax` does not refuse. Same-site is not same-origin — SOP compares scheme, host and port — so the request itself is settled by CORS and by nothing else
  - **jandh-emoticons answers with CORS on every response, errors included**, naming this origin through its own `JANDH_ORIGIN` (that repo's § 8.7.). Its 429 carries `rateLimit`, which the batch loop **acts** on, and a 429 the browser withholds for want of a header is not a 429 to any caller — it is an opaque network failure that keeps chunking into a quota that already said no
  - **`proxy.ts`'s `emoticons/api` exclusion stays, and is not this call's.** Nothing here reaches that path any more, but the zone's own import screen does: it is served through the rewrite and its fetches are relative, so they arrive at this origin and must not meet the session gate (§ 13.7.)
- **It lives there for two reasons, and both are load-bearing.** The prompt, the model name and the batch size get one definition rather than two that drift; and the model call is the expensive part of this feature, so it is hosted where the emoticon work already is — this app's function budget stays on the conversation. `GEMINI_API_KEY` is therefore **not** an environment variable here. **Do not move the route back**
- **The route takes item ids or posted bytes, and never a URL.** Items that exist are read from R2; images that are not items yet — a caller staging them, with nothing uploaded — arrive as `multipart/form-data`. That is deliberately the whole interface: taking no URLs, it can never be aimed at a host by whoever calls it (`AGENTS.md § 6.5.`). Fetching those bytes is the caller's business. § 14.'s allow-list applies to a posted image as to a stored one — nothing is written, but the bytes go to a third party
- **`gemini-3.5-flash-lite`, three images per request** — and the number is set by **answer quality, not by latency**. It reached three from sixteen by way of four, and the direction is what the rule is: this number moves **down freely and upward only against a fresh measurement**, which is also why moving the model off `gemini-3.1-flash-lite` did not raise it. Sixteen was measured and fitted comfortably: ~17k image tokens, about five seconds, per-image order intact. What the measurement did not cover is whether the words were any good, and they were not. The whole feature rests on the model keeping N inline pictures apart while reading the Korean line off each one, and that is the first thing a lite model loses as N grows — silently, because plausible keywords for the emoticon next door look exactly like correct ones. A smaller batch costs round trips and nothing else worth having, and since the batches are pooled the round trips are no longer paid end to end
- **The batches run `KEYWORD_SUGGESTION_CONCURRENCY` wide, and the free tier's per-minute quota is the only thing that sets that number.** Six, because a run is safe exactly while it fits inside one minute's allowance — Google answers 15 requests a minute and a 36-item pack is 12. Raising it cannot rescue a run already past the allowance, only reach the refusal sooner; a longer one needs the quota spread across minutes, which nothing here does. `NEXT_PUBLIC_KEYWORD_SUGGESTION_CONCURRENCY` overrides it, inlined at build time like every other public variable, and a blank falls back to **6 rather than to 1** — `.env.example` ships every name empty, and a copied blank that silently restored the sequential run is the one failure a switch like this must not have
- **Pooling is `mapPooled` rather than a `Promise.all`, and the 429 is the whole reason.** A quota refusal is the one failure that ends the run (below), and it can only end anything while something is still unlaunched — the pool halts its queue at the first rejection, settles what is in flight, and rethrows. So `fillBatch` rejects on the quota **alone** and swallows everything else, which puts both rules on one pool: an ordinary failure costs its own chunk, a quota costs the run. `onBatch` then lands out of order, so `remaining` counts what has **settled** rather than what lies behind a cursor — derived from a chunk's position it ran backwards the moment a later chunk answered first
- **Each image is labelled in the turn** — `## Emoticon A` immediately before the bytes — and the prompt tells the model to copy back the letter it was given, which `responseSchema`'s `enum` then constrains it to. A letter rather than a number because a digit competes with the prose around it and a zero-based ordinal fights the counting a model learned from natural language; positions stay the app's business. Position alone used to tie `index` to a picture, and position is exactly what degrades as the batch grows, so this and the smaller batch are one fix: the batch size alone would only have made the drift rarer
- **The model is `GEMINI_MODEL`, and it is optional because its default is a real one** (`AGENTS.md § 6.2.`) — a deployment that never sets it behaves exactly as this one does, which is the test separating a plain `process.env` read from `ensureEnv`. It is read `?.trim() ||`, never `??`: `.env.example` lists every name with an empty value, and `??` would pass a copied blank through and ask Google for a model named `""`. **Whatever it names must be multimodal and must honour `responseSchema`** — a text-only model does not fail loudly, it answers, and the answers describe nothing. The batch size above was measured against the default and is not re-measured by changing this
- **The prompt's instructions are English, its examples are Korean, and it leads with the text drawn in the picture.** The transcription rule is load-bearing: a Korean emoticon usually has its line written on it, and that line is the exact string the user will type looking for it. A wordless cut still answers `화남` from its expression, which is what OCR alone cannot do. **The examples are the specimen of the output language rather than illustrations of the rules** — translating one to `wow` teaches the opposite of the language block above it, so they stay Korean even though everything around them is English (jandh-emoticons' § 8.7.1. owns the wording)
- **The line is transcribed with its punctuation, and stripping it was a bug against `MIN_KEYWORD_LENGTH`.** The prompt used to ask for the drawn text `문장부호 없이`, which turned `왈?` into `왈` — one character, so `normalizeKeywords` dropped the only string the user would have typed, and the model named the glyph instead (`물음표`). Punctuation is what makes the short interjections reachable at all: `왈?`, `헐!`, `앗!` clear the floor **because** of it. `고민되는군...` now comes back as both itself and `고민되는군`, and the floor is stated in the prompt rather than enforced only after the answer — jandh-emoticons' § 8.7. owns the wording
- **A keyword is an utterance, not a caption of the picture.** The suggester used to answer `놀람`, `응시`, `관찰`, `기록` — nominalized forms that describe an emoticon and that nobody types into this composer looking for one — and it padded every item to five with synonyms of one another (`우울, 좌절, 슬픔, 힘들어, 지침`). Kakao's own ranked keywords are the opposite (`킹받죠?`, `지루해`, `먹고싶어`, `할거야`), and the prompt now states the rewrite (`놀람` → `헐`) and refuses the padding. **This matters here rather than only there**, because `matchesKeywordQuery` matches text and nothing else — there is no synonym expansion behind it, so a caption the user would never type is a row this composer can never reach. The same absence is why the count went **up** to 5–8 rather than down: a stored keyword is one more reachable query, and `헐`, `대박`, `미쳤다` are three ways in where `놀람`, `충격`, `경악` are none. jandh-emoticons' § 8.7. owns the wording
- **Suggestions are returned, never written.** They land in a field the user is already editing, so a bad answer costs a glance rather than a column of nonsense. Nothing throws: a batch that fails costs those items their suggestions and nothing else
- **The fraction starts at the first batch, never at zero.** Until one answers, the button reads `검색 키워드를 만들고 있어요` and its `Sparkles` is a spinning `LoaderCircle`; from then on it is `3/24개 채웠어요`. `0/24개 채웠어요` is a completive sentence about nothing having happened — it reads as a press that failed rather than one still running, and at three images per request it is what the user sees for the first couple of seconds of every run. The spinner is what says "wait", so no line of copy has to
- **The progress is a fraction, not § 13.4.'s countdown.** That convention counts what is _left_ because its grid gains rows as files land, so a fixed total would contradict the rows beside it — here every item is already on screen and only its keywords are filling in, so the total is settled before the first batch and stating it is the more useful half. `2/24개 채웠어요`
- **The pack screen fills only the items with no keywords**, so the model never revises work somebody typed. The import preview writes over everything instead — nothing is saved yet and the grid is right there, so "suggest again" is a reasonable thing to press twice
- **The button carries a reason under it, not a restatement of itself.** `키워드를 넣어두면 대화 중에 단어로 찾을 수 있어요`, in `caption` / `meta`, centered below the button and hidden while a batch runs. It is an optional action, and the label already says what it does — what it does not say is why anyone would want it, which is that an item with no keywords is one § 13.8.'s composer search can never reach. Copy naming only the mechanism (`검색 키워드를 등록하세요`) adds a line and no information
- **A 429 stops the run, and it names which quota.** Google returns `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` for three limits — requests per minute, tokens per minute and requests per day — and they are not the same advice, so the route answers `rateLimit` and the toast is either `요청이 몰렸어요. 1분 후에 다시 눌러 주세요` or `오늘 쓸 수 있는 양을 다 썼어요. 내일 다시 시도해 주세요`. It is the one failure that ends the loop rather than costing a batch: every chunk behind it would be another request against the limit that just refused. What was already filled stays filled
- **`GEMINI_API_KEY` is read with `ensureEnv` and sent as a header**, never a query string, which is what proxies and error logs keep verbatim

---

### 13.9. 따라하기 — an emoticon in the conversation opens the picker on its relatives ✅

**Tapping an emoticon in the history opens § 13.8.'s search on the emoticons related to it**: that emoticon at the head of the row, ready to send straight back, and the ones like it behind it. KakaoTalk's own behaviour, and the shortest route there is between seeing an emoticon and using it.

**This is not "open the panel where that emoticon lives", which is what it was first built as** and what the first screenshot of it disproved. A pack tab scrolled to the cell answers "where is it", and nobody tapping an emoticon is asking that — they are asking for that one _or something like it_, which is a question a single pack cannot answer however well it is scrolled. So the tab an item's pack happens to draw is not consulted at all.

- **The tap keeps everything it already did.** Restarting the animation and replaying the sound are § 13.6.'s, and one of the four moments an emoticon may sound at is a tap on a bubble — the panel opening is added to them rather than put in their place. A tap that stopped sounding in order to open a panel would be answering a question nobody asked
- **A mouse reaches the same action by right-click**, through an 이모티콘 따라하기 row in the § 8.11. message sheet. `useLongPress` already maps `contextmenu` onto the sheet a hold opens (`DESIGN.md § 3.2.`), so the row costs one entry and gives touch a second route that raises no sound
- **Where the panel opens is the picker's decision and not the room's.** The room holds no pack list, and the pack list is what fills the row
- **The row is the tapped item, then every keyword match in the library, ranked by relevance.** `toKeywordRelevance` sums a per-term score over the query's terms (§ 13.9.1. holds the ladder), so an emoticon sharing several of the tapped item's words outranks one sharing a single word loosely. The ranking runs on the **server** now, over a bounded candidate set, and the picker may filter the revealed item out of what comes back but **must never re-sort it** — a second ordering in the browser is a second definition of relevance. The sort is stable, leaving pack order to decide inside a step. **The item itself is shown whether or not the search finds it**: it is already in hand, and an item nobody has described matches nothing
- **Its own pack is the fallback and only the fallback.** Appended to every answer it buried the cross-pack matches under one pack's shelf, and the whole feature then read as "this set only" — the exact thing the ranking exists to undo. It is still needed for the item nobody has described, which answers to nothing and would otherwise stand in the row alone. **It is a request of its own since § 13.6. stopped shipping the library** (`packs/{id}/items`), so it arrives a round trip behind the search rather than out of a list already in hand — and it is asked for only once the search has answered with nothing, or a pending search would fetch a pack it is about to have no use for
- **A failed search is not "nothing found", and it used to reach the fallback anyway.** The error unlatches the pending flag, which is the only thing the fallback waits on, so a search that never answered drew one pack's shelf behind the ringed item and asserted the relatedness nothing had computed — silently, because `찾는 이모티콘이 없어요` and `검색하지 못했어요` are only ever reached by an **empty** row and this row holds the tapped item. So the fallback is withheld on the error path too, and the sentence is drawn under the row instead of in place of it: the tapped emoticon stays, alone, with `검색하지 못했어요` beneath it. The plain search path was already right, since a failure leaves it with no row at all
- **The previous query's answer is kept across a keystroke and dropped across a 따라하기**, and those are two different things wearing one flag. `keepPreviousData` is what stops the row flickering once per character on a Hangul field (§ 13.9.1.); a reveal is not a keystroke but a jump to an unrelated query — the last search's results, held over behind the tapped item and inside its ring, read as "these are related to this one". Reproducible as: search `고민`, send one of the results (§ 13.8. leaves the panel open on them), then tap a **different** emoticon in the conversation. The honest intermediate state is the tapped item alone until its own answer lands, which the panel already described and only achieved on a cold cache. Typing releases the reveal, so nothing keyed on it can flicker per character
- **Which is also why the reveal carries the whole `Emoticon` rather than its id.** The tap holds an item the loaded list may not — the other participant can have authored it minutes ago, and the panel never unmounts to re-ask — so the row can show one the search itself cannot reach yet
- **The ring on that first cell is `ring-inset`.** § 13.8.'s results row is `overflow-y-hidden` and its cells fill its height exactly, so an outset ring loses its top and bottom edges and reads as a broken box — which is exactly how it shipped first. A ring rather than the tabs' `bg-primary-tint`, which in this panel means selected; this cell is not selected, it is the one the tap was about
- **A cell spells `touch-pan-y` on its overlay as well as on its control.** `touch-action` is intersected down the whole ancestor chain, so a pair that disagrees resolves to `none` and the scroller cannot be dragged at all
- **The horizontal swipe starts a fresh gesture on any primary `pointerdown`**, never "only when none is standing". A gesture ends on a release this element sees, and there are ways for that release never to arrive — the panel going `inert` under a finger, an ancestor taking capture mid-press. Refusing later presses on the strength of that leftover stops the swipe for the rest of the session, since the picker never unmounts to clear it. `isPrimary` is what keeps a second finger from taking the gesture over, which is what the old guard was actually for
- **The row is scrolled back to its head on the request's token, not on the item.** It keeps whatever offset an earlier search left it at, and keyed on the item a second 따라하기 for the same emoticon would put it at the head of a row still scrolled somewhere else
- **Search looks across every pack, hidden ones included, and that survived the move to the server.** `searchEmoticons` carries no `enabled` filter and must not grow one; the pack list is likewise unfiltered and the picker narrows to `isEnabled` only for the tab strip and 최근 사용. Filtered in SQL, a hidden pack's emoticon would be unreachable by any means at all: no tab, no search result, and nothing for this feature to land on. Hiding is a statement about the tab strip, not a withdrawal of an answer
- **The search field takes several words at once, separated by commas** (`splitKeywordQuery`), matching an item that answers **any** of them. 따라하기 fills it with the item's whole keyword list, because one picture answers to `우와`, `놀람` and `헐` alike (§ 13.8.) and searching only the first of them is not "related emoticons". A comma because § 13.4.'s keyword field already commits a chip on one; a field with no comma in it is the single term it always was. `MAX_KEYWORD_QUERY_LENGTH` is what the field is capped at, not `MAX_EMOTICON_KEYWORD_LENGTH` — that is the cap on one stored word, and a field filled with twelve of them could not then be edited
- **Typing releases the pinned item**, since a keystroke is the user taking the search over
- **Landing on 검색 this way does not raise the keyboard**, which is the one exception to § 13.8.'s "the field takes focus whenever the tab comes on screen". Every other route onto that tab is a request to type; this one is a request to look, and the keyboard would put the panel behind it — leaving the emoticon further from the thumb than it was before the tap
- **The reveal is released exactly where the search is** (§ 13.8.): the panel closing, the tab being left, a keystroke in the field, and **a word tapped in the composer**, which is a search of its own and can land on a panel already showing a revealed row — left standing there it pins and rings an emoticon the typed word never matched, and withholds the keyboard from the one tap that is unambiguously a request to type. And **the request has to be standing for the release to hold off**, exactly as `searchRequest` does — a tap made while the keyboard is up opens nothing until it retracts (§ 13.6.), so the reveal is applied across several renders in which the panel is still closed, and a release that did not check would wipe it on every one of them
- **The bubble is excluded from § 13.6.'s tap-to-dismiss**, by a `data-emoticon-bubble` marker the room's `pointerdown` looks for. A tap on the history closes the panel; this tap re-aims it, and `pointerup` runs a frame before the `click` — left armed, the panel collapsed and reopened on every 따라하기
- **The room blurs the field before asking for the panel**, for the reason § 13.6.'s toggle does: the panel is gated on the keyboard being down, and iOS lowers it for a blur alone
- **Nothing is unhidden and no preference is written.** A tap in the conversation is not a settings change, and § 13.5. is where hiding is undone

#### 13.9.1. Search runs in Postgres and is ranked in JavaScript, and both halves are deliberate ✅

**`GET /api/emoticons/items?q=` is the search.** It used to be a pass over a library the browser already held, which § 13.6. stopped shipping — and which would not have survived the library growing in any case. What replaced it is a normalized index and two statements: `emoticon_keywords` selects the candidate ids off its own indexes, the items are then read whole, and `toKeywordRelevance` ranks them **in JS, on the server**, against the item's authored `keywords` array.

**No search library was adopted, and the reason is a rule none of them can express.** The match is containment in **both** directions — the keyword containing the query, and the query containing the keyword (§ 13.8.'s particle case, where the drafted `고민되는군요` has to reach the keyword `고민되는군`). Fuse, MiniSearch, match-sorter and uFuzzy all model one direction only, "does this document answer this query"; a document shorter than the query is not a hit to any of them. Adopting one therefore means dropping the half of § 13.8. that Korean grammar pays for, or running a custom pass beside the library — two matching systems where there was one. The corpus is the second reason: items of three to five short words each, where BM25's term frequency is always 1 and its inverse document frequency has almost nothing to separate.

- **The two directions are answered separately, because one of them is not an indexable predicate as written.** Forward — `keyword LIKE '%term%'`, `고민` reaching the item tagged `고민되는군` — is what a `pg_trgm` GIN is for. Reverse — `term LIKE '%keyword%'` — has the wildcard on the wrong operand, and no index answers it
- **Separately means a `UNION` of two selects, not an `OR` of two predicates**, and the first cut of this got it wrong.
